# Halyard — Knowledge Base

**The master index of everything known about Shadow PC.** Every RE/experimental finding in this project lives here. Before any hypothesis or implementation, **start by cross-referencing** the relevant sections below.

> **How to maintain this file**: see the `re-knowledge-base` skill. Golden rule: write it down as soon as it is confirmed at >=70 %, cite the source (RE / pcap / live log / doc), and add a cross-ref in the related sections.

---

## What a path in this file means

Most citations here name a file. Read them as **provenance** — where a finding
came from — and not as links to open. They fall into four kinds, and only the
first is something you can click:

| Looks like | What it is |
|---|---|
| `core/protocol/ctrl_session.c` | in this repository. These were corrected to the current layout on 2026-09-13; before that they named the pre-restructure tree. |
| `captures/…`, `dumps/…`, an `.md` under a capture directory | **the lab, not the repo.** 14 GB of pcaps, Ghidra and IDA dumps, hook logs and the official client sit in `shadow2switch-lab/` beside the checkout, reached through `tools/lab.sh`. `CAPTURES_KB.md` is its index. None of it is published: it carries live credentials, and one server reply historically contained a private key. |
| `/tmp/shadow_tls.log`, `/tmp/wire.bin` | an artefact of a session that has ended. It says how the finding was obtained, not where to find it now. |
| `chunks.log`, `raw_chunks.bin`, `env.txt` | a file the application reads or writes at runtime, in its data directory. It exists when you make it exist — see the `SHADOW_DUMP_*` toggles. |
| `wolfssl/ssl.h`, `Envideo/src/…` | a third-party or SDK header, in its own tree. |

**Dates are part of the citation.** A measurement is true of the build and the
network it was taken on; several entries here were later refuted, and §9 records
which. That is why an entry is not deleted when it turns out to be wrong — it is
marked, so the same hypothesis is not tried a third time.

---

## Confidence legend

- **C95+** Confirmed by direct observation (decrypted pcap, server logs, RE'd daemon code)
- **C80-95** A strong converging presumption (>=2 independent sources agree)
- **C60-80** A supported hypothesis (one clear source, not reproduced)
- **C<60** A working hypothesis (to be validated)

Annotate every entry. If a finding drops below C60, mark it `OBSOLETE` or remove it.

## 1. Auth & Session

### 1.1 OAuth Device Grant — `[C95]`
- Endpoint: `https://api.shadow.tech/oauth/device/code` + polling
- The client_id was extracted by RE'ing the Linux launcher
- Refs: `memory/project_shadow_protocol_har.md`, `04-shadow-client/`'s M2 implementation
- Cross-ref: 1.2, 1.3

### 1.2 JWT structure (instance + ports) — `[C95]`
- `JWT.instance`: an integer 1-99; it identifies the REST sub-path (`/N/...`)
- `JWT.ports`: `null`/`[]` = legacy WebRTC; `[0,11,...]` = direct TCP
- Our account: `JWT.ports=null` → it routes through the native WebRTC stack (M7-M16)
- **Critical**: `JWT.ports=null` WORKS for streaming, contrary to what we believed
- Refs: `memory/project_shadow_jwt_ports_pivot.md`, `memory/project_overnight_re_2026-05-03.md`
- Cross-ref: 2.1, 2.2

### 1.3 Proximus credentials — `[C95]`
- `POST /shadow/vm/proximus-credentials` body **`clientType`** (NOT `client_type` - bug fixed 2026-05-03)
- The body includes `ports: ["controlchan","video","cursor","input"]`
- It returns 3 JWTs: `launcher_jwt`, `main_jwt`, `usb_jwt`
- The **X-Shadow-Agent header is mandatory** (it was missing on the Switch side — added 2026-05-03)
- Refs: `memory/project_overnight_re_2026-05-03.md`, `core/services/launcher.c`

### 1.4 The clients endpoint — the types and fields returned — `[C95]`
**Source**: `last_clients_*.json`, captured in a Switch session on 2026-05-03

| Type | Fields returned |
|------|------------------|
| `launcher` | id, type, remote, opaque, version, **`spice_secret`** (16 chars) |
| `main` | id, type, opaque, version, **`streamingtoken`**, `streamingtoken_expiry` |
| `usb` | id, type, opaque, version, **`tlskey`** (64 hex = 32 bytes) |

- `tlskey` = a pre-shared key for Spice TLS PSK (probable, to be confirmed)
- `spice_secret` = ticket auth Spice link
- Refs : `memory/project_shadow_real_protocol_confirmed.md`; the REST snapshots the client writes into its data directory at bootstrap (`last_clients_*.json`)
- Cross-ref : 4.1, 4.2

## 2. REST endpoints VmProxy

### 2.1 Path schema — `[C95]`
- Every request: `https://<vm-host>/<instance>/<path>` with a Bearer token
- `<vm-host>`: `ipv4-gpu-X.frsbg01.compute.shadow.tech` (force IPv4 on Switch)
- Known endpoints: `/clients`, `/devices`, `/forward`, `/status`, `/stream` (SSE)
- Cross-ref: 1.2 for `<instance>`, 7.1 for forcing IPv4

### 2.2 `/N/devices` (legacy USB-direct) — `[C95]`
- **POST** body `{"protocol":"XHCI"}` (EHCI/UHCI too)
- **Response 201**: `{"data":{"id":"usb-NNNNN","port":NNNNN}}`
- The `port` is NOT an external TCP port (times out from the Switch AND from a PC)
- This is the **legacy** endpoint - the Mac daemon does NOT use it
- **DELETE** /N/devices/<id> → 204
- Refs: a console session log of the era (the file was then named `webrtc.log`; it is `halyard.log` today)
- Cross-ref: 2.5

### 2.5 `/N/clients/<id>/remote-consoles` - **not exposed on our VM** - `[C95]`
**Source**: Ghidra RE of `vmproxy_http_channel.cpp::refreshSpiceToken`; a live probe of 6 combinations on the Switch

- The Mac daemon builds this path as `{}/{}/remote-consoles` + body `{"protocol":"spice","type":"spice-html5"}`
- The Switch probe of 2026-05-03 PM tested **6 combinations**: `{launcher, main, usb}` × `{/N/clients/<id>/remote-consoles, /clients/<id>/remote-consoles}` with their respective JWTs → **ALL 404**
- The `404: Not Found` (with `/N/`) confirms that the nginx routing validates the instance prefix but that the sub-path `/clients/<id>/remote-consoles` does not exist
- **Cross-check against the web client's HAR on the same VM** (`pc.shadow.tech_Archive*.har`): no `remote-consoles` request made by the official web client → this path is probably gated by feature/account/version
- Our legacy account, with `JWT.ports=[]` and `JWT.permissions=["stream","create","delete","forward","list","refresh"]`, does not appear to be entitled to Spice
- **CONCLUSION**: abandon the Spice/usbredir tunnel as a gamepad route for this account. **The `shadow-controller` DC (WebRTC) remains available** — the session9 capture confirms that Shadow web does forward the gamepad through a FlatBuffer DC; it is only the Spice path that is gated.
- Refs: the Ghidra cross-reference dump for the WSS layer, a console session log of 2026-05-03, and the web client's HAR
- Cross-ref: 4.5, 5.1

### 2.6 Endpoints actually used by the web client (HAR) - `[C95]`
**Source**: `pc.shadow.tech_Archive [26-04-30 23-23-00].har` — a diff of unique URLs on `*.compute.shadow.tech`

```
GET/POST  /<instance>/clients
GET       /<instance>/clients?type=main&active=true
POST      /<instance>/forward
GET       /<instance>/status
GET       /<instance>/stream         (SSE)
```

No mention of `/devices`, `/remote-consoles`, or anything else. The web client's streaming + gamepad go through WebRTC (signalling via `/forward`, data via DC).

### 2.3 `/N/forward` — `[C95]`
- POST body JSON, accepts only Content-Type JSON
- A binary protobuf body is rejected (10 paths tested, all 404/405/400)
- M32 binary CtrlChanV2 disabled: not exposed on this VM
- Refs: `memory/project_overnight_re_2026-05-03.md`

### 2.4 `/N/stream` (SSE notifications) — `[C95]`
- VmProxy notifications (VmConnected, VmDisconnected, ...)
- Refs: Mac daemon strings `kVmProxyVmConnected`, `kVmProxyVmDisconnected`…

## 3. Streaming (video + audio)

### 3.1 Current WebRTC pipeline (M7-M14) — `[C95]`
- TLS+ICE+DTLS+SRTP+H.264+Opus working end to end
- 1502 frames at 720p already decoded successfully
- profile-level-id = **42001f** (NOT 42e01f)
- libavcodec: **NEVER FF_THREAD_SLICE** on Switch (pure green frames)
- thread_count=1 mandatory
- Multi-slice per picture → NAL aggregation per PTS before `avcodec_send_packet`
- Refs: `memory/archive/project_webrtc_milestones.md`, `memory/feedback_libavcodec_switch_threading.md`, `memory/project_shadow_h264_quirks.md`

### 3.2 Alt path — direct TCP+UDP (decrypted pcap) — `[C95]`
- TCP/443 h2 + UDP `port = slot×1000+offset` chacha20-poly1305
- NOT QUIC (msquic dormant in the binary but unused for streaming)
- This flow requires a non-null `JWT.ports` — not available on our account
- Refs: `memory/project_shadow_real_protocol_confirmed.md`, `memory/project_shadow_encryption_framing.md`

### 3.4 SslCtrlChanV2 wire format + port binding — `[C95]` LIVE BUG
- **Source**: LD_PRELOAD hook `06-shadow-recon-linux/tls_hook/shadow_tls_hook.so` capturing the official desktop app's SSL_write/read on 2026-05-06 + a parallel tcpdump.
- Wire format = **`[uint16_be msg_type][uint16_be len][protobuf]`** (NOT the `[u32_be len][proto]` we were trying).
- Port = **:13011** on the observed VM (slot=13). Also :13014 (secondary encryption), :13020 (secondary control).
- EMPTY ALPN + NO SNI in the ClientHello on those ports. TLS 1.3 directly, no HTTP wrapping.
- First frame sent = **Capabilities** (NOT Authentication) — an 87-byte protobuf. Authentication is the 3rd message.
- Source: an exhaustive LD_PRELOAD capture of the desktop client - TLS plaintext plus the matching pcap. Indexed in `CAPTURES_KB.md`; the material itself is in the lab.
- Refs: `memory/project_sslctrlchanv2_wire_format_FOUND.md`, `06-shadow-recon-linux/tls_hook/`

### 3.5 Per-client VM pool allocation — `[C100]` RESOLVED 2026-05-08
- **Source**: runtime test 2026-05-08 22:50, dual-SSE patch in `main_test.c`.
- **Unblocking trigger identified**: **simultaneous dual SSE** (`/N/stream` with `launcher_jwt` AND `/N/stream` with `main_jwt`). With a single SSE → :13011 stays closed. With both → :13011 binds within seconds.
- The official desktop app has 2 processes (the Electron launcher + ShadowPCDisplay), each opening its own SSE. To reproduce = call `proximus_sse_start` twice on our side.
- Cross-ref: §3.4, §3.6 (bootstrap validated)
- Refs: `memory/project_native_bootstrap_VALIDATED.md`

### 3.7 RegisterSession + cipher — `[C100]` BOOTSTRAP COMPLETE
- **Source**: LD_PRELOAD capture 2026-05-08 + runtime test, shadow-test-cli `success=1` end to end.
- RegisterSession (243 B sent → 317-382 B reply): `Message{f1=3, f2=Request{f10=RegSession{f2=display{f1=EDID 128B}, f3=res{w,h,fixed32 ~0x43100726}, f4=scale{f3=fixed32 1.0}}}, f4=caps, f5=OCapture}`
- 128 B EDID: hardcoded from the capture (standard 1920x1080). The server probably checks the signature, not the fine content.
- The chacha20-poly1305 cipher is created via `shadow_cipher_create(key,key)` after the Encryption reply. Cipher ready for UDP video/audio.
- Code: `demo/src/streaming/ctrl_msgs.c::ctrl_build_register_session`, `demo/src/streaming/encryption.c::shadow_cipher_create`.
- Cross-ref: §3.6 (auth/enc), §3.5 (dual SSE), §3.8 (UDP video TODO)
- Refs: `memory/project_native_bootstrap_VALIDATED.md`

### 3.9 UDP video stream wire format — `[C100]` ⭐ DECODED
- **Source**: extended LD_PRELOAD hook (sendto/recvfrom/sendmsg/recvmsg) capturing a ShadowPCDisplay session 2026-05-08 + runtime test, shadow-test-cli `decrypt_ok=126 fail=0`.
- UDP ports are **fixed** (NOT slot×1000+xx): :13010 (video), :13012 (audio), :13013 (input/cursor), :13030 (?)
- 25 B UDP register packet to :13010: `[0x41 'A'][0x01 ver][0x00][0x14 len=20][0x00][hash 20B]` where hash = Auth reply field 3.4.2 (extracted via `ctrl_parse_authentication_reply_v2`)
- Server pushes 1280 B frames (MTU), wire format:
  - `[byte0=0x13 magic][bytes 1-2=seq LE][bytes 3-4=?][byte 5=0x19][byte 6=0x00][bytes 7-8=variant][byte 9=?][byte 10=enc_flag]`
  - 11 B SUFP header in total
  - `[byte 10 == 0x01]` → chacha20-poly1305 ciphertext (`pkt[11..n-28]` ct, `pkt[n-28..n-16]` nonce, `pkt[n-16..n]` tag) — DECRYPT VALID on 126/126 frames
  - `[byte 10 == 0x00]` → plaintext (probably metadata/ACK/feedback)
- First plaintext bytes: `02`, `cf`, `21`, **`68` (= H.264 PPS NAL ✓)**, `7d` — NAL fragments to reassemble.
- Code: `demo/src/streaming/ctrl_msgs.c::ctrl_build_udp_register`, `demo/src/streaming/encryption.c::shadow_cipher_decrypt_unsafe`.
- Refs: `memory/project_native_bootstrap_VALIDATED.md`

### 3.11 Input :13013 wire format — `[C95]`
- **Source**: 4 packets captured from the desktop app, UDP_SENDMSG to :13013, layout analysis 2026-05-09.
- 42 bytes, uplink only (client→server): `[ciphertext 14B][nonce 12B][tag 16B]`
- nonce[0] = a counter incrementing per packet (7b → 7c → 7d)
- nonce[1..11] = constant per session = the channel's 11 B `nonce_extra`
- Uses the SAME chacha20 key as the video/cursor channels.
- Audio :13012 = DTLS 1.2 (different, to be implemented via a wolfSSL DTLS handshake).

### 3.27 TCP input channel `:base+14` — size-prefix framing — `[C100]` 2026-08-25 (S31)
- **Source**: the byte-exact Connect blob (`ctrl_input_tcp.c::CONNECT_BLOB`) + an `RX 104 B` dump from a real session's logs. Attested in BOTH directions.
- Wire: **`[u32 LE size][payload]`**, total length on the wire = `size + 4`. It is the *size-prefixed* FlatBuffers format.
  - Our Connect: `5c 00 00 00` = 92 = 96 - 4
  - Server echo: `64 00 00 00` = 100 = 104 - 4
- The server echo is 104 B and carries the cursor position **as the server computes it** at offsets 96-97 (X) and 98-99 (Y), u16 LE — verified: `81 06 76 02` -> (1665, 630), matching the `[S16]` log.
- **Measured distribution**: 100 % of reads are exactly 104 B (1365 and 282 messages across two sessions; every other size class at zero). No TCP coalescing observed to date — but nothing forbids it, and the code wrongly treated it as audio until S31.
- Code: `streaming/msgframe.{c,h}` (a pure rule, tested), called by `ctrl_input_tcp.c::vst_input_rx_thread`. Toggle `SHADOW_INPUT_RX_FRAMING=0`.
- Cross-ref: §3.11 (UDP input `:base+13`, a different format), §3.20 (click/keyboard encoding)

### 3.10 Shadow VideoFrame format (post-chacha20 plaintext) — `[C100]` ⭐
- **Source**: 128 decrypted video chunks reassembled offline, then `ffprobe` on the resulting Annex-B. Reproduce with `SHADOW_DUMP_CHUNKS=1` and `SHADOW_DUMP_H264=1`.
```c
struct ShadowVideoFrame {
    uint8_t  byte0;        // 0x02 = version 0 | codec 2 (H.264)
    uint32_t timestamp_le; // bytes 1-4 — chunks with the same ts = same frame (fragmented)
    uint8_t  flag;         // 0x00 normal / 0x01 keyframe with a 13 B extension
    uint8_t  ext[13];      // if flag==0x01
    uint8_t  nal_data[];   // 00 00 00 01 + NAL bytes (standard H.264 RBSP)
};
```
- 119/128 chunks have byte0=0x02 (video). The other 9 = a mix (audio/cursor multiplexing, perhaps).
- Frame #0 contains SPS (NAL 7) + PPS (NAL 8) + IDR (NAL 5). Chunks with the same ts are fragments.
- ffprobe validates: a complete H.264 High 1080p YUV420P stream, reassembly OK.
- Cross-ref: §3.9 (UDP wire), §3.6 (Encryption key)
- Refs: `memory/project_native_bootstrap_VALIDATED.md`

### 3.8 Channel announcements (8 messages after RegisterSession) — `[C95]` DECODED 2026-05-23
- **Source**: LD_PRELOAD MASTER capture `06-shadow-recon-linux/captures/MASTER-20260514-153936/tls_plain.log` lines 20759/21805/21822/21839/21857/21874/21891/22953 + IDA Pro RTTI mangled strings + per-channel proto serialisers identified in `tools/ida/out/channels/`.
- Pattern: `Message{f1=seq, f2=Request{f8=ChannelInfo{outer_fN=<per-channel_proto>}}, f4=caps, f5=OCapture}`. The **outer fN** inside ChannelInfo identifies the channel type.
- **Mapping of outer fN → channel** (= the 8 byte-exact announcements):
  | seq f1 | size | outer fN | channel | inner content |
  |--------|------|----------|---------|---------------|
  | 5 | 119B | f1 | **Video** | codec H.264, 1920×1080@143.85fps, max_bitrate 163.74 Mbps |
  | 6 | 103B | f4 | **Cursor** | f4{f2=4, f3=1, f4=1} |
  | 7 | 103B | f3 | **Input** | empty resolution (= no media params) |
  | 8 | 107B | f2 | **Audio** | sample_rate 48000, bit_depth 16, channels 1 |
  | 9 | 99B  | f6 | **Controller** | minimal (f4{f4=1}) |
  | 10| 101B | f7 | **Clipboard** | f4{f3=1, f4=1} |
  | 11| 107B | f5 | **Mic** | identical to Audio (48k/16/1) |
  | 12| 103B | f8 | **FileTransfer** | f4{f1=5 (=SFTP), f3=1, f4=1} |
- **Wire-exact code**: `05-shadow-client-borealis/demo/src/streaming/ctrl_msgs.c::CI_BODY_5..12` (lines 533-571) confirmed **byte-exact against the desktop** (8/8 ✓ via the capture diff in §3 of CHANNEL_ANNOUNCEMENTS_RE.md).
- **Multi-NAL trigger**: NOT identified in this layer. The H2 RE9/RE10 verdict stands: no boolean (f4..f6, f8, f10) enables multi-NAL on the server side. The untested holy-grail candidate = `RegisterSession_StreamingProtocol.protoType=SSUFP` (= a field we do not send, a C70 hypothesis, code recipe in CHANNEL_ANNOUNCEMENTS_RE.md §5.2).
- Refs: `tools/ida/out/CHANNEL_ANNOUNCEMENTS_RE.md`, `memory/project_channel_announcements_8msgs_RE.md`, `tools/ida/out/channels/FINAL_<channel>_serB_*.c`.

### 3.6 Native bootstrap Capabilities/Auth/Encryption, byte-exact — `[C100]`
- **Source**: LD_PRELOAD capture 2026-05-08 + runtime test of shadow-test-cli with dual SSE.
- **Capabilities** (87 B sent → 106 B reply): `Message{f2=empty marker, f4=caps meta, f5=OCapture}`. The server reply contains the version "ShadowStreamer 6.1.7".
- **Authentication** (245 B sent → 193 B reply): `Message{f1=1, f2=Request{f4=AuthBody{f2=1, f3=perms, f4=token, f5=sessId, f6=ts_ms_ASCII, f7=nested, f8=cli_id}}, f4=caps, f5=OCapture}`. The reply contains a SHA-1 cert + the server's capabilities.
- **Encryption** (127 B sent → 140 B reply): `Message{f1=2, f2=Request{f12=Enc{f1=32 keysize, f2=12 nonce_extra, f3=16 tag, f4=client_random32}}, f4=caps, f5=OCapture}`. The reply contains `f4=server_key32`, directly usable as the chacha20-poly1305 key.
- **Nesting of the REPLIES** (distinct from that of the requests above) — `[C100]`, verified by test on 2026-08-25:
  - Authentication: `Message{ f3 = Reply{ f4 = AuthBody{ f2 = 20 B hash } } }`
  - Encryption:     `Message{ f3 = Reply{ f12 = Encryption{ f4 = 32 B key } } }`
  - These numbers **changed mid-project**: Reply used to be at field 2, AuthBody at field 1, Encryption at field 3 (hence the "was field 2" / "was field 3" comments in `ctrl_msgs.c`). Two functions kept the old ones and became silently inoperative — `ctrl_parse_authentication_reply` (exported, with no caller, a permanent auth failure) and `enc_reply_field_cb` (dead code announcing the key at field 3). Both fixed/removed on 2026-08-25; `tests/test_ctrl_msgs.c` now pins the correct numbers.
  - The parser demands the EXACT size (32 B for the key, 20 B for the hash): a different size is ignored, never partially copied.
- Code: `demo/src/streaming/ctrl_msgs.c::ctrl_build_capabilities|authentication|encryption_request`.
- Cross-ref: §3.4 (wire format), §3.5 (dual SSE trigger)
- Refs: `memory/project_native_bootstrap_VALIDATED.md`

### 3.3 Encryption framing — `[C100]` (verified by test 2026-08-25)
- chacha20-poly1305 **without AAD**
- Wire: `[ciphertext | nonce(12B) | tag(16B)]` — 28 B of overhead
- 11 B SUFP chunk header
- Tx nonce: random for the first message, then **incremented by 1, little-endian**. A nonce reused with the same key breaks the AEAD outright; monotonicity is therefore a security property, not a convenience.
- Reception: `shadow_cipher_decrypt` refuses a nonce that is not strictly increasing (replay); `shadow_cipher_decrypt_unsafe` accepts it, which UDP requires since frames arrive out of order. The video path uses the `unsafe` variant.
- **Verification**: `tests/test_encryption.c` checks the envelope against the **RFC 8439 §2.8.2** reference vector (key, nonce, AAD, plaintext, ciphertext and tag given byte by byte) — it passes, which proves each parameter reaches the right slot. A key/nonce swap or an AAD passed by mistake shows up no other way: it decrypts to mush and you blame the decoder. Also verified: the wire format at the announced offsets, nonce monotonicity, replay, and Tx/Rx key separation.
- Refs: `memory/project_shadow_encryption_framing.md`, `tests/test_encryption.c`

### 3.12 Pipeline performance — bottlenecks identified and fixed — `[C100]` 2026-05-18
- **Source**: the automated test `tools/test-N56-holes.sh` (3 x 30 s), read from the session log it produces
- **Bottleneck #1 — 208 KB kernel UDP buffer**: Linux `net.core.rmem_default` caps UDP receives. At 1080p and 1.2 MB/s, the buffer fills in 170 ms. If the receive thread blocks (= libavcodec decoding, GUI rendering), packets are dropped **FIFO** → the burst's first chunks lost = chunk_idx=0 systematically missing = `first_hole_idx=0` 76 % of the time → libavcodec `bytestream -N` errors on MB row 60+ = a **blurry taskbar**.
  - **Fix**: `setsockopt(SO_RCVBUF, 4MB)` in `udp_register()` (`core/protocol/ctrl_session.c:74-92`). Audio too: 512 KB. Override with `SHADOW_UDP_RCVBUF=<bytes>`.
  - **Measured result**: 12 HOLES → **0 HOLES** over 90 s + 28.8 → 30.2 fps + `reasm_abandoned` 18 → 2.
  - Refs: `memory/project_N56_udp_rcvbuf_FIX.md`
- **Bottleneck #2 — software-only libavcodec hwaccel flags**: `AV_CODEC_FLAG_OUTPUT_CORRUPT` + `AV_CODEC_FLAG2_SHOW_ALL` + `error_concealment=259` are implemented **only** in the software path, `h264dec.c`. Under hwaccel (CUDA/VDPAU/VAAPI/NVTEGRA), forcing partially decoded frames out → the driver detects an invalid surface state → `av_hwframe_transfer_data FAIL rc=-5 (Input/output error)` + `Failed to sync surface 0xNN`.
  - **Fix**: make those flags conditional on `!hwaccel_active` (`core/media/h264_decoder.c:387-413`). Sticky auto-fallback after 5 failures → a `/tmp/shadow-client/hwaccel.disabled` marker for the next session.
  - **Measured result**: native sessions went from crashing within seconds → **stable indefinitely**.
  - Refs: `memory/project_HW1_hwaccel_rc5_fix.md`
- **Bottleneck #3 — TCP_NODELAY missing on the TCP sockets**: `ctrl_tcp.c`, `ctrl_input_tcp.c`, `ctrl_video_tcp.c` created SOCK_STREAM sockets without `setsockopt(TCP_NODELAY)` → Nagle buffers 40 ms per small packet (= gE feedback, input events, VST keyframe retransmits).
  - **Fix**: `setsockopt(IPPROTO_TCP, TCP_NODELAY, 1)` on all 3 TCP sockets (`core/protocol/ctrl_tcp.c:80-89`, `core/protocol/ctrl_input_tcp.c:158-163`, `core/protocol/ctrl_video_tcp.c:240-245`) + adding `#include <netinet/tcp.h>` to `core/services/sockets_compat.h:66`.
  - **Impact**: -40 ms of latency per control RPC = **critical for input in games**.
  - Refs: `memory/project_PERF1_latency_wins.md`
- **Bottleneck #4 — decoder cleanup racing the RTC worker**: `pc_->close()` (libdatachannel) does not guarantee the ThreadPool has finished draining. T1 (cleanup) freed the decoder while T43 (worker) was still calling `av_hwframe_transfer_data` → SEGV, NULL deref at 0x8. The pattern also applies to the native path if decoders are freed without serialisation.
  - **Fix**: a `std::mutex decoder_mtx` in `SessionState` + a double-checked lock in the video/audio onMessage lambdas + a lock in `finalize()` before destroying (`dc/dc_session.cpp:56-73 / 326-339 / 557-577`).
  - Refs: `memory/project_BUG2_decoder_race_fix.md`
- **Bottleneck #5 — memory pool for reassembly chunks**: `malloc(ct_len)` per chunk in `on_video_packet` = 180-300 mallocs/sec at 1080p. **Not implemented** — cost ~1-2 ms/s cumulative + a Switch heap-fragmentation risk. A future win.
- **Bottleneck #6 — GLFW VSync 60 Hz cap**: Borealis' `swapInterval=1` by default → the frame display is capped at 60 Hz. A possible win = `swapInterval=0` in the stream activity and 1 in the menus (= tearing accepted while gaming). **Not implemented**.

### 3.13 Wire encoders summary (call sites + format) — `[C100]` 2026-05-18
- **Capabilities** (`core/protocol/ctrl_msgs.c:51-124`) — 87 B sent → 343 B reply (= the §3.8b modes list)
- **Authentication** (`core/protocol/ctrl_msgs.c:364`) — 245 B sent → the reply carries a 20 B auth hash
- **EncryptionRequest** (`core/protocol/ctrl_msgs.c:809`) — 127 B sent → the reply carries the 32 B chacha20 key
- **RegisterSession** (`core/protocol/ctrl_msgs.c:748`) — multi-channel setup
- **ChannelAnnouncement chan_idx=0 (= video)** (`ctrl_msgs.c:592-655 build_video_chan_body`) — encodes w/h/fps/codec/profile + max_bitrate_bps
  - Inner f1.f4: `{f2=codec, f4=profile}`
  - Inner f2: `{f1=width varint, f2=height varint, f3=fps fixed32}`
  - Outer f7: `max_bitrate_bps varint`
- **VideoEncodingConfig** (`core/protocol/ctrl_msgs.c:150-188`) — case 13 of the Request oneof. Sent after bootstrap (= 3 s later): bitrate+fps, redundant with the channel announcement.
- **NotifyResolution / NotifyVideoCommand / RequestFlush / Heartbeat / Ready / DisplayReady** — see the prototypes at lines 200-360
- **Every call site** logs `[Q1] channel video params: WxH @ fps @ Mbps` at runtime → a byte-exact confirmation of what goes on the wire
- **Q1 env overrides**: `SHADOW_BITRATE_MBPS`, `SHADOW_FPS`, `SHADOW_CODEC`, `SHADOW_PROFILE_ID`
- **Hardcoded desktop defaults**: `fps=143.85`, `max_bitrate_bps=20000000` (20 Mbps), `codec=2`, `profile=1`

### 3.14 Native runtime audio + input integration — `[C100]` 2026-05-18
- **Source**: code in production, builds + links OK
- **Audio path**: `ctrl_audio_dtls_open(..., on_audio_cb, user)` receives DTLS-decrypted Opus payloads. Forwarded to `audio_decoder` (libopus + ALSA on Linux, audoutAppendAudioOutBuffer on Switch).
  - Wired in `core/protocol/ctrl_session_glue.c:130-145`, callback `on_audio()` → `audio_feed_rtp()`
  - Signature aligned: `ctrl_session_audio_cb(payload, len, uint32_t rtp_ts, user)` instead of the old, useless `uint64_t pts_ms`
  - audio_decoder cleanup in `finalize()` after h264_decoder_destroy
- **Input path**: a new bridge `streaming/native_input.{h,c}` (~60 LoC)
  - `ctrl_session.c` calls `native_input_set(ctx.itc)` after a successful `ctrl_input_tcp_open`
  - `webrtc/shadow_input.c::shadow_input_drain_queue` routes to `native_input_send_*` when `native_input_active()==true`
  - `ctrl_session_glue.c` sets a mock `shadow_input_set_active_sctp((sctp_assoc*)0x1)` so that stream_view detects an active session → registers the GLFW mouse/keyboard callbacks
  - 4 send functions exposed: mouse_move, mouse_button, scancode, mouse_wheel
- **Refs**: `memory/project_I3_audio_native_DONE.md`

### 3.15 RaptorQ FEC byte-exact — **[C95] NO FEC EXISTS** (2026-05-23 F31)

> **RaptorQ mission CANCELLED — there is no target.** The desktop binary `ShadowPCDisplay` has **NO** FEC encoder/decoder at all (= RaptorQ, RS, LDPC, XOR, GF(256) — all absent). The chunk→frame pipeline is **pure pointer arithmetic**.

**Complete decompiled pipeline** (IDA Pro 9.3 + Hex-Rays on `06-shadow-recon-linux/ShadowPCDisplay.i64`):

```
SufpUdpIOChannel::OnDataReceived  sub_D7F1E0
  └─> UdpDataChunk::init          sub_D85FA0   ; parse SUFP v1/v2/v3/ping
  └─> UdpPacketReceiver::feed     sub_D941F0   ; insert the chunk into a frame
      ├─> UdpFrame::add_chunk     sub_D8CD50   ; just a vector<chunk*> insertion
      ├─> sub_D89F40              ; predicate: *(WORD*)(this+24)==0
      ├─> sub_D93DD0              ; promote a ready frame
      └─> sub_D911D0              ; 300 ms timeout cleanup
  └─> VideoUdpFrame::write_chunks sub_D8A000   ; dual-callback dispatch
```

**The real semantics of flag10 (= `byte10 & 1`)**: it is a **routing bit**, NOT a parity bit:
- `byte10 & 1 == 0` → an indexed chunk → vtable[+80] with `(data_ptr, len, flag=0)` → reassembly slot `chunk_idx`
- `byte10 & 1 == 1` → a "framing" chunk → `sub_D808D0` → trims `v2+v3` trailer bytes (= metadata via vtable[+16]/[+24]) → vtable[+56](payload_window)

For video, `v2 = v3 = 0` (= empirically: V11's PARITY_RAW raw concatenation works → byte-exact).

**vtable[+0x20] (= slot 4)** of both `18VideoUdpFrame` vtables (`0x124CA78` primary + `0x124CB68` secondary):
- `sub_D89FC0` / `sub_D86DB0` (35/28 B)
- Body: `return chunk[+16] - first_chunk->ptr;` = a **chunk_size GETTER**
- NOT an encoder. F30's identification of "vtable[+0x20] = target" was wrong.

**UdpDataChunk C++ object layout**:

| Offset | Width | Field | Source byte (SUFP v3) |
|--------|-------|-------|----|
| +0..16 | 3×qword | data_ptr/offset/end | external |
| +24 | qword | timestamp_us | now()/1000 |
| +32 | qword | header_size | 11 |
| +40 | u16 | version_high_nibble | byte0>>4 |
| +42 | u8 | frame_id | byte1 |
| +44 | u16 | chunk_max | byte2..3 LE |
| +46 | u16 | chunk_idx | byte4..5 LE |
| +48 | u32 | packet_id | byte6..9 LE |
| +52 | u8 | **flag10_bit0** | **byte10 & 1 (= framing, NOT parity)** |

**Exhaustive negative evidence**:

| Search | Hits |
|---|---|
| Function names `raptor`/`fec_`/`reed`/`solomon`/`ldpc`/`gf256`/`vandermonde`/`cauchy` | 0 (= the only `aFec` is a display manufacturer) |
| RTTI type_info | 0 |
| Strings RaptorQ/OTI/scheme_specific/K_padded/L_prim | 0 real ones |
| GF(256) exp tables (= prefix `01 02 04 08 10 20 40 80 1D 3A ...`) | 0 |
| Imports `raptorq`/`isa-l`/`leopard`/`wirehair`/`openfec` | 0 |

**Implications**:
1. **There is NO RaptorQ decoder to port** (= the F29 lead was a false positive; F30's brute force was right about exhaustion but drew the wrong conclusion).
2. **V11 PARITY_RAW is the correct byte-exact model** for video (= `v2=v3=0`).
3. **Trailer trimming** is optional for the residual ~5 % (= if `v2+v3 > 0` for video, to be confirmed via LD_PRELOAD).
4. **F29/F30 SUPERSEDED**: see [[project-raptorq-encoder-BYTE-EXACT]].

**Refs**:
- IDA EAs: `0xD7F1E0` (OnDataReceived), `0xD85FA0` (UdpDataChunk::init), `0xD941F0` (feed), `0xD8A000` (write_chunks), `0xD8CD50` (add_chunk), `0xD8DC00` (UdpFrame::write), `0xD89F40` (is_complete), `0xD808D0` (framing handler), `0xD808C0` (flag10 accessor `*(byte*)(this+52)`)
- `18VideoUdpFrame` vtables: `0x124CA78` + `0x124CB68`, type_info `0x124BA70` = `'13VideoUdpFrame'`
- Full document: `tools/ida/out/RAPTORQ_ENCODER_RE.md`
- Memory: [[project-raptorq-encoder-BYTE-EXACT]]
- Scripts: `tools/ida/raptorq_re{,2,3,4}.py`
- Sanity check: `tools/ida/out/raptorq/SANITY_CHECK.md`

### 3.16 Image bug — RESOLVED: off-by-one chunk count — `[C100]` 2026-06-02 (G4)

> **ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED.** The image bug (an artefacted/illegible bottom on motion) = an **off-by-one**: `max_chunks` (SUFP bytes 4-5) is the **index of the last chunk** (0-based), NOT a count. The server sends idx `0..max_chunks` (= `max_chunks+1` chunks). The guard `chunk_idx >= max_chunks` **silently dropped the last chunk** (idx==max, `flag10=0` = the bottom slice's tail) on **every frame** → the bottom slice truncated by ~1 chunk (~3 MB rows) → a CABAC error at the bottom that **propagates across the GOP** through the P-frames → an illegible bottom on motion.
>
> **Fix**: `max_chunks += 1` in `on_video_packet` (`ctrl_session.c`, toggle `SHADOW_MAX_COUNT_PLUS1=0` to revert). **Result**: offline ffmpeg decoding **761 errors → 0**, 971 frames with no errors, a near-perfect picture confirmed at runtime (user, 2026-06-02). + G3: default bitrate 1 Mbps→15 Mbps (motion noise).
>
> **Decisive proof**: the `[DIAG] REJECT chunk idx==max` diagnostic fired on **every** frame (idx=11/max=11, idx=4/max=4…), always with `flag10=0`. Decoding the bottom slice in isolation: it decoded rows 34→64 then ran out of the last ~3 = exactly one chunk.

**NOTE on the false "hardware path" lead**: the first version of §3.16 (a multi-agent workflow) wrongly concluded that the bug was in NVDEC hardware decoding and that the bytestream was fine. FALSE — the workflow had analysed a dump of **static** content (little motion → a short bottom slice = barely visible). Content **in motion** (the user's YouTube test) revealed the real cause = the off-by-one. "Software decode is clean" was true on static content, misleading on motion.

**What remains (engineering, not RE)**: occasional freezes = genuine occasional UDP loss (~2 incomplete frames / 971). Loss recovery (effective NACK / server-side redundancy `SetVideoPacketsRedundancy`) = future work, not the root cause.

**Direct evidence (verified this session, not just by a subagent)**:
1. **Offline software decoding of the V11 bytestream = CLEAN.** Reference `ffmpeg` (no GUI/NVDEC/F22) on `build_linux/shadow-client-data/stream_baseline.h264` → 200 frames decoded: the original wallpaper + the **Windows taskbar visible top→bottom**, frame 114 (the worst) ≈ frame 117 (the best). The historical "100 % clean" (F2/V11) was confounded (the test-cli display is a no-op, `main_test_stubs.c:7-18`; the PPM was read AFTER F22's in-place mutation) BUT the conclusion holds anyway: software decoding is clean.
2. **ffmpeg reports ~435 MB errors** concentrated in the **bottom rows** (rows 0-40 = 0 errors; rows 58-67 = 11→58 errors/row, a gradient). Those errors are **recoverable in software** (invisible on this content), but probably **visible in hardware** (NVDEC leaves partial-surface garbage instead of a clean frame N-1).
3. **Trimming parity chunks = a REGRESSION.** `stream_trim28.h264` (same session) has fewer ffmpeg errors (242 vs 435) BUT the picture is **visibly worse** (smearing at the bottom). → the ffmpeg error count is a **bad proxy** (trimming shortens the slice → silent concealment). **The no-trim default (`SHADOW_PARITY_TRIM=0`) is correct.** RE4's TRIM=28 was rightly reverted.
4. **Per-frame structure** (`chunks.log`): a keyframe = 10 DATA (1241 B decrypted) + 17 PARITY (1269 B raw); a P-frame = 1 DATA + N PARITY. Concatenated in idx order. Only idx 0 carries the Shadow VideoFrame header `02…`. **Our model = the desktop's** (cf §3.15, A1 IDA: decryption = per-chunk AEAD via the cipher vtable+0x38=0xe20e20 reached from write_chunks' sink-vtable[+56]; flag10==1 trims v2+v3=28=nonce+tag; flag10==0 raw, v2=v3=0).

**The real remaining bug (C80)**: the **hardware** path (GUI NVDEC + Switch NVTEGRA NV12):
- NVDEC produces NV12 (`format != 0`) → **F22 anti-green never runs** (`core/protocol/ctrl_session_glue.c:175` guard `format==0`), and the hwaccel branch sets `error_concealment=0 + err_recognition=0` (`core/media/h264_decoder.c:401-405`) → the undecoded bottom MBs pass through verbatim = a green band.
- Switch only: the async transfer worker drops frames on a full queue with no PTS guard (`core/media/h264_decoder.c:213-230`).
- **Never verified visually**: Switch = task #129 PENDING; a GUI sw-vs-hwaccel A/B = only pre-V11 (obsolete, cf `project_image_real_ceiling.md` 2026-05-14, which tested a broken bytestream).

**Priority leads** (after the adversarial check): (1) a GUI A/B of `SHADOW_HWACCEL=0` vs `=1` on the current stack; (2) make the hardware path tolerate incomplete bottom slices (F22 on NV12, OR an NVDEC→YUV420P transfer, OR drop the frame if the bottom is incomplete); (3) a live Switch test (#129). **Dead**: FEC/parity (F31), parity trimming, the frame_id lock (reverted, a disaster at abandoned=2282/30 s), the EVP v2/v3 hook (already answered, 2003@1241 / 0@1269).

- **Refs**: RE workflow 2026-06-02 `wf_f5c2ee69-2e9`; `tools/ida/out/RAPTORQ_ENCODER_RE.md` (F31); `project_evp_hook_DEFINITIVE_FINDING.md`; `core/protocol/ctrl_session_glue.c:175`, `h264_decoder.c:401-405,213-230`; dumps `build_linux/shadow-client-data/stream_{baseline,trim28}.h264` + `chunks.log`.
- **Cross-ref**: §3.15 (NO FEC), §3.9/§3.10 (wire), §3.12 (N56/HW1).
- **Memory**: [[project-image-bug-reframed-hardware-path]]

### 3.17 GUI-only freeze at t≈118s — **RESOLVED: `/status` is a POLLING endpoint, not an SSE stream** — `[C95]` 2026-08-21 (D8/D9)

> **ROOT CAUSE.** The GUI's 2nd SSE targeted `/N/status`. But **`/status` is NOT an SSE stream: it is a JSON endpoint** (`{meta, data:{vm_status, reachable, streamer_up}}`, documented in `core/services/proximus.h:44`; the SSE is `/stream`, `core/services/proximus.h:70`). It answers `HTTP 200` and terminates immediately. Since `sse_keepalive_thread` did only **one `curl_easy_perform`**, the thread exited at the first return and nothing maintained the 2nd channel any more — silently. The server, whose binding rests on the DUAL SSE (§3.5), closed the session ~118 s later.
>
> The comment in `connecting_activity.cpp` justified `/status` as "confirmed via an LD_PRELOAD hook on 2026-05-09": **that was false**, and it steered the code for ~3 months. The desktop capture shows only `GET /1/stream` and `GET /1/status`, **once each** — `/status` there is a simple state check, not a keepalive.
>
> **Fix D10 (the clean fix)**: the GUI opens `/stream` for both JWTs, as `main_test.c` already did. **Result: t=225 s, 0 closures, 0 reconnections** — `/stream` is a long-lived stream that does not drop. `SHADOW_SSE_MAIN_SUFFIX=status` reproduces the old behaviour.
>
> **Fix D9 (the safety net)**: a reconnection loop for as long as `abort_flag` is not raised (500 ms backoff polled in 100 ms slices, the §7.3 constraint) — the right behaviour for a real SSE that drops. An intermediate diagnostic step: with `/status`, it kept the session alive at the cost of **306 polls / 225 s, all `rc=0 http_status=200`** — which proved the endpoint was terminating normally every time. With D10 it never fires. `SHADOW_SSE_RECONNECT=0` for the old one-shot.
>
> **Why the headless build saw nothing**: `main_test.c` opens `/stream` for BOTH JWTs (`proximus_sse_start` with no suffix). So it was **right by accident** — which made the bug invisible headless and hid the trail for months. Methodological corollary: surviving headless proves nothing about the GUI as long as the two do not open the same endpoints.

**What follows documents the investigation** (the teardown itself, and the 4 hypotheses ruled out before reaching the right one).

- **Source**: 3 GUI runs (`shadow-client`) + 4 headless runs (`shadow-test-cli --mode=native-stream`), same binary, same toggles, session 2026-08-20/21. D1 instrumentation added for the occasion (`ctrl_tcp.c::mark_peer_closed`). Logs archived out of repo (scratchpad `ab/*.log`).

**Wire signature**: `wolfSSL_read rc=0 err=-397` (= `SOCKET_PEER_CLOSED_E`, a clean TLS FIN) on `:base+11`, immediately followed by `udp_drain recv FAIL errno=111` (ECONNREFUSED) on `:base+10`. The last exchange before the cut is a perfectly normal `send 90B / recv 139B` — no warning sign on the control side.

**Reproducibility**:

| mode | time survived | VM |
|---|---|---|
| GUI | **118 s** | `gpu-<instance>` (base 9000) |
| GUI | **108 s** | `gpu-<instance>` (base 11000) |
| GUI | **118 s** | `gpu-<instance>` (base 11000) |
| headless | 195 / 195 / 295 / 395 s | `gpu-<instance>` (base 9000) |

→ **The VM is ruled out**: the 4 headless runs that survive run on the very VM that kills the GUI. **NVDEC is ruled out**: `cuda hwdevice created` in all 6 runs, headless included.

**Hypotheses tested and REFUTED** (do not replay them):
1. **Session lease not renewed** (D3, `SHADOW_REG_PERIOD_MS`) — the desktop sends RegisterSession 8× (§ MASTER capture), we send it once. A/B: the arm WITHOUT renewal survived 195 s. Refuted.
2. **Malformed input events** (D5, `SHADOW_INPUT_SYNTH_MS`) — 581 events injected headless (against 26 in the dead GUI): no closure. Refuted.
3. **Heartbeat starvation** — the GUI only delivers 1.15 Hz instead of N48's 2 Hz. Forcing `SHADOW_HB_PERIOD_MS=200` → 4.10 Hz delivered, and the session still died at 108 s. Refuted.

**Symptoms measured on an identical VM (GUI vs headless)**: reassembly holes 0.424/s vs 0.127/s (**×3.3**); IDR requests 0.068/s vs 0.020/s (**×3.4** — the IFR being event-driven since G6, it *measures* the loss); heartbeat 1.15 Hz vs 1.75 Hz; identical inbound rate (409 vs 425 pkts/s). Reading: decoding + the GL upload run on the thread that drains UDP, so rendering starves reception. **Careful**: that explains the loss, NOT the closure (cf. refutation 3).

**Hypothesis 4 also REFUTED — the render path**: `SHADOW_GUI_NOVIDEO=1` short-circuits the display handoff (D7) while keeping decoding, control, channels and input intact. The GUI **still dies at 118 s**. So rendering is not the cause of the teardown.

**The ×3.3 UDP loss remains an open and DISTINCT problem**: it is real and measured, but it does not explain the closure (refutations 3 and 4). An independent structural fix: move decoding/rendering off the thread that drains UDP.

**Before D1, this bug was invisible**: the client did not detect the closure and rewrote into the dead socket in a tight loop — 37,880 `err=-397` + 20,078 `recv header FAIL` in 9 min, a frozen picture without a single message. D1 logs once, cuts the I/O and exits with `exit_reason=2`.

**Side channels, collateral finding `[C95]`**: across 5 sessions, `comchan` fails **5/5** on `:base+14` (err=-308 after a successful TCP connect) — it opens a 2nd TLS connection on the port already held by `ctrl_input_tcp`, which supports "one connection served per port". Since its founding hypothesis (V15, the focus triplet = a multi-NAL gate) is also **refuted**, it moved to **OFF by default** (D11, `SHADOW_COMCHAN=1` to reopen it): it produced nothing but one misleading error line per session. `vst` 4/5 and `input-tcp` 3/5 are intermittent (D4 adds a retry, never triggered since).

**`audio-dtls` :base+12 — LIVE BUG, no sound `[C90]`**: the DTLS 1.2 handshake fails **5/5**, `err=-308` (`SOCKET_ERROR_E`).
- **`errno = 0`** at the exact moment of failure (D12 — the first version of the diagnostic read errno *after* `wolfSSL_get_error`, which overwrites it, and wrongly reported 0; fixed, and the 0 is real). So it is **neither ECONNREFUSED nor a socket timeout**: the port is not closed, the failure is **internal to wolfSSL**.
- `wolfSSL_dtls_set_peer` was **absent** while the code's comment declared it required (RFC 6347 §4.2) — implemented by D13 (`rc=1`, `peer_len=28`), **with no effect**: the handshake fails identically. Lead ruled out, the call kept because the DTLS state machine requires it.
- **Still to do**: compare our DTLS ClientHello byte by byte with the desktop's (`16 fe ff …`, cf. the header of `ctrl_audio_dtls.h`) — the cipher list was already widened in RE2 without success. A full RE campaign in its own right, not a clean-up.

- **Refs**: `ctrl_tcp.c::is_peer_closed/mark_peer_closed`, `ctrl_session.c` (D1/D2/D3/D5), `memory/project_freeze_GUI_only_t118s.md`
- **Cross-ref**: §3.16 (the "occasional UDP loss" there is a distinct phenomenon), §3.12, §7
- **Corrects**: `memory/project_freeze_t115s_SOLVED_switch_specific.md` concluded "a Switch-specific bug, the same code runs 245 s+ on Linux". False on the native path: the Linux GUI dies at 118 s. That note remains valid for May's WebRTC path, not beyond.

### 3.18 Switch build broken since 2026-05-18 — RESTORED `[C100]` 2026-08-21 (D14)

- **Source**: `make shadow-client.nro` on the current tree. The `.nro` on disk dated from **14 May**; the target had therefore not been rebuilt since, and the regression went unnoticed for ~3 months.

**Three defects, all from the same family**: libnx/C symbols **redeclared by hand** instead of using the header.

1. `clients/borealis/activity/vm_list_activity.cpp:226` — `extern int psmInitialize(void);` when libnx declares `Result psmInitialize(void)` (`Result` = `u32`). Since a libnx update exposes `<switch/services/psm.h>` through the include chain, gcc rejects it: *ambiguating new declaration*. Introduced by `19abcd2` (UX4 B9, 2026-05-18).
2. `clients/borealis/main.cpp:83` — `appletHook`/`appletGetFocusState` redeclared in **C++ without `extern "C"`** → mangled symbols, never resolved against libnx's C → an *undefined reference* at link time. A local `typedef` of `AppletHookType` also shadowed libnx's. `<switch.h>` was not included in that file.
3. `shadow_input.c` — references `sctp_send_msg_redundant()` while `webrtc/sctp.c` is **excluded from the Switch build** (the native path replaces SCTP). The file is compiled on console because its drain threads serve the native path through `native_input`, but those particular functions are never called: `native_input` intercepts upstream. A stub is provided that fails explicitly, with the signature taken **verbatim** from `sctp.h:58`.

**Lesson**: never redeclare a libnx symbol by hand — include the header. All three regressions come from that shortcut, and the first version of my own stub reproduced it (`const void *` instead of `const uint8_t *` → *conflicting types*).

**Result**: `shadow-client.nro` rebuilt at 13.5 MB; the Linux build unchanged (0 errors).

- **Refs**: `vm_list_activity.cpp` (UX4 B9 block), `main.cpp` (UX6 B3 block), `core/input/shadow_input.c` (`#ifdef __SWITCH__` stub)
- **Cross-ref**: §7 (HOS quirks), §3.17

### 3.19 Audio `:base+12` — **RESOLVED: wolfSSL without `WOLFSSL_IPV6` + an IPv6 peer** — `[C100]` 2026-08-21 (A2/A3)

> **ROOT CAUSE.** The DTLS handshake failed in **100 % of sessions** (`err=-308`) ever since the channel was implemented: **our very first `sendto` returned `-174` = `NOT_COMPILED_IN`**, so the ClientHello never left. It was neither a timeout, nor the server, nor a missing cipher.
>
> In `wolfssl/src/wolfio.c::EmbedSendTo`:
> ```c
> else if (!dtlsCtx->connected) {
>     peer = dtlsCtx->peer.sa;
> #ifndef WOLFSSL_IPV6
>     if (PeerIsIpv6(peer, peerSz)) return NOT_COMPILED_IN;
> #endif
> }
> ```
> The vendored wolfSSL is compiled **without `WOLFSSL_IPV6`**, and the audio channel resolves to IPv6 (`family=10`, `peer_len=28`).
>
> **Fix A2**: `wolfSSL_set_dtls_fd_connected()` instead of `wolfSSL_set_fd()`. Our socket is already `connect()`-ed, so `dtlsCtx.connected = 1` routes through `send()`/`recv()` and **short-circuits the whole peer path, hence the IPv6 guard**. No wolfSSL rebuild, IPv6 kept. Toggle `SHADOW_AUDIO_DTLS_CONNECTED=0`.
>
> **Result**: a complete handshake, `cipher=TLS_ECDHE_RSA_WITH_AES_256_GCM_SHA384`, `DTLSv1.2`. The observed sequence **matches the desktop capture** (§ RE 2026-05-23): HelloVerifyRequest 60 B, Certificate 765 B, ServerKeyExchange 386 B, ServerHelloDone 25 B, server Finished 14 B — identical sizes.

**A3 — a regression revealed by the fix `[C100]`**: once the handshake succeeded, the `rx_thread` finally existed… and never exited. The code set `SO_RCVTIMEO=100 ms` believing it would get non-blocking behaviour, but on the wolfSSL side a socket timeout in DTLS triggers its **internal retransmission** and `wolfSSL_read` does not return. Measured: `rx_thread exited` absent, the process blocked at shutdown (**151 s real for `--duration=30`**, killed by timeout). It is exactly the §7.3 violation (a long thread must see `abort_flag` within ≤100 ms → on Switch, leaked handles = a reboot). Fix: `wolfSSL_dtls_set_using_nonblock(ssl, 1)` → WANT_READ comes back up and the loop revisits `abort_flag` every 100 ms. After the fix: `rc=0`, 37 s for the 30 s asked, `rx_thread exited` present.

**What the 2026-05-23 RE got wrong**: it concluded "the code is ready, `err=-308` = just a timeout, bump it to 10 s". The timeout played no part — nobody had looked at **our** wire, only at the desktop's. Instrumenting the I/O (A1, `SHADOW_AUDIO_DTLS_TRACE=1`, callbacks delegating to `EmbedReceiveFrom`/`EmbedSendTo`) gave the answer in one run.

**Remaining**: no app-data record (`0x17`) received — the log says `idle Ns (no audio packets)`. Expected on a silent Windows desktop; **to be confirmed with sound playing on the VM**, which requires being able to click, hence depends on the input bug (§ control).

- **Refs**: `ctrl_audio_dtls.c` (A1/A2/A3), `third_party/wolfssl/src/wolfio.c:927`, `memory/project_audio_channel_wire_RE.md`
- **Cross-ref**: §3.14, §3.17 (the 5/5 failure finding), §7.3 (thread lifecycle)

### 3.20 Input `:base+14` — byte-exact diff against 697 desktop messages — `[C90]` LIVE BUG 2026-08-21 (I8)

- **Source**: `captures/captures_input_20260602_132138/desktop_plaintext.log` (134 MB, LD_PRELOAD on the desktop while streaming + clicking). The fd map was extracted from the `CONNECT` events: port_base=8000, **`fd=316` = `:8014`** (the input channel). That channel's plaintext appears in `SSL_WRITE` (the socket level `fd=316` carries only encrypted TLS records).

**The desktop sends 697 messages of 144 B**, and **nothing else** on that channel. Per-offset variance analysis over those 697 samples: **109 of the 144 bytes are constant**. The variable fields:

| offset | distinct values | reading |
|---|---|---|
| `[40..41]` | 256 | sequence counter |
| `[48..51]` | 239 | timestamp |
| `[95..96]` | 256 | 2nd counter |
| `[127..131]` | 228 | coordinates / timestamp |
| `[56..99]` (many isolated bytes) | 2 each | boolean flags — **probably the button states** |

**Finding 1 — `@52`: ~~a constant field~~ → CORRECTED, it is a truncated u64 timestamp `[C95]`**: the first version of this entry announced "a single constant byte differs: the desktop puts `0x0b` at `@52`, we put `0x00`". **A wrong reading.** A comment on the keyboard path already said so: `@48` is a **u64 LE**, whose high half lives at `@52..55`. The desktop's `0x0b` is not a protocol constant but **their machine's uptime** (11 × 2³² µs ≈ 13.1 h). Our `monotonic_us()` **truncated to 32 bits**, so we only wrote the low half. Hardcoding `0x0b` would have sent another machine's uptime. **Fix I10**: write all 8 bytes (`SHADOW_INPUT_TS32=1` to revert). Verified: our `@52` is now 2 (our own uptime) instead of 0. **Fixes a real bug, but does NOT unblock the input.**

**Finding 2 — our clicks have the wrong size `[C90]`**: we emit **152 B** messages (`CLICK_LEFT_TEMPLATE`, `CLICK_RIGHT_TEMPLATE`, `SCROLL_UP/DOWN_TEMPLATE`), yet **the desktop NEVER sends 152 B** — everything goes as 144 B. Combined with the ~20 offsets taking exactly 2 values in the `[56..99]` range, the reading is that the desktop encodes **buttons and wheel as flags of the same 144 B message**, where we created separate templates of a different size. Those 152 B templates probably come from the WebRTC/browser era (the DC channel), not the native channel. **This is the most likely explanation for clicks having no effect.**

**Validation harness (I9) — usable, NEGATIVE result**: two signals were tried.
1. ❌ **The cursor channel** — unusable: it returns 0, 1928 or 3948 packets depending on the run **with no input at all**. Far too unstable to prove anything.
2. ✅ **The Windows key → video bitrate** — the Start menu opens whatever the desktop's state, hence a large picture change. `SHADOW_INPUT_SYNTH_KEY_AT=N` fires a press+release at t=N s; you then read the stats timeline. **A clean, reproducible signal.**

**Result: no effect.** Two runs (before and after I10), key sent at t=40 s with `rc=0` on press and release, the bitrate strictly within variance: 2133/2268 KB and 2215/2246 KB against 2069–2270 KB everywhere else.

**Checks that ELIMINATE leads** (comparing 145 of our messages against the desktop's 697):
- all the **constant** bytes match after I10;
- `seq @40` monotonic (us 2..146, desktop 4..735) — OK;
- `ts @48` a plausible u64 on both sides — OK since I10;
- **coordinates `@128`/`@130` (two u16 LE) CORRECT**: ours X 960..988 / Y 539..540, which matches the injector exactly (±7 around the centre 960,540); the desktop sweeps wider (X 96..1906, Y 1..639) because a human was moving the mouse. An earlier u32 reading had suggested X stuck at 0: **a false alarm**;
- the counter zone `@94..100` structurally identical.

**Consequence**: the content of the movement messages is probably **no longer** the problem. The cause is most likely **upstream** — channel state, a missing step, or a server-side condition.

**Hypotheses tested on 2026-08-21 and ALL REFUTED** (harness I9: the Windows key → a video bitrate spike; no run produced the slightest deviation outside the 2069–2383 KB variance):

| # | hypothesis | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `@52` = a constant protocol field `0x0b` | **a misreading** — it is the high half of a u64 timestamp (the desktop's uptime). Led to the real fix I10 |
| 2 | timestamp truncated to 32 bits (I10) | **a real bug, fixed** — no effect on the input |
| 3 | FOCUS multiplexed on the input connection (F1) | sent (type 0/1/2/4, `wr` OK, the connection survives) — **no effect, no ACK** |
| 4 | Connect replayed / anti-replay (F2) | **a real bug, fixed** — our 96 B went out frozen (the original capture's seq + ts) while the desktop patches them; `@83` corrected 0x01→0x02. **No effect** |

**State of the diff after these fixes**: on the movement messages (144 B) as on the Connect (96 B), **all the desktop's constant bytes now match ours**, and the variable fields (seq, u64 timestamp, coordinates) are in consistent ranges. So the content of the messages probably no longer explains the failure.

> ⚠️ **WARNING — this session's input measurements need re-checking `[C95]`** (code review 2026-08-21). Fix **F1** (sending focus on the input connection) was **on by default AND wrote its frames big-endian** when `:base+14` is a `[u32 LITTLE-endian len][payload]` stream (`CONNECT_BLOB` starts with `5c 00 00 00` = 92 for 96 B). So a `type=0` frame produces a zero length, then the server reads `00 01 00 00` = 256 and swallows 256 bytes — the end of the focus block **plus the first real events**. Permanent desynchronisation of the channel, that is, exactly the failure mode I8 had removed in June. **Every runtime input validation after F1 is therefore void**, including the final GUI test of C1/K1 which showed "no effect". Capture-based decoding (the click and keyboard tables) is NOT affected: it comes from the official client, not ours. F1 moved to OFF by default; to be replayed.

**MAJOR CORRECTION 2026-08-21 — the desktop DOES emit 152 B `[C95]`**: the conclusion "the desktop never emits 152 B" came from the June capture, in which the user had barely clicked. A dedicated capture with **54 timestamped human clicks** (`tools/capture_manual_run.sh`, reading `/dev/input/event*`) gives **75 messages of 152 B, 71 of them correlated with a click** within 0.2 s. **So our click templates have been the right size all along.**

**And the diff is even more telling**: across the desktop's **134 constant bytes** in 152 B, **ZERO differ** from our `CLICK_LEFT_TEMPLATE`. The template is byte-exact. The defect is in what we do **not** patch:

| | offsets |
|---|---|
| the desktop varies | `40 41 48 49 50 51 52 96 106 128 129 130 131 136 138 140 144 150` |
| we patch | `40 41 42 43 48 49 50 51 96 97 98 99 128 129 130 131 144` |
| **never written by us** | **`52` `106` `136` `138` `140` `150`** |

`@52` is the high half of the u64 timestamp (fixed by I10). The other five are the **button state fields**, left at the template's frozen value. `@140` discriminates the button: **left = 0**, **right = 6 or 8** (aggregate: left 31/40 at 0, right 28/31 at 6/8; confirmed on the capture's 3 perfectly isolated clicks). `@150` follows (left 0, right 1), and `@106/@136/@138/@144` move together according to a 55/20 partition not yet explained.

**Corpus available**: `06-shadow-recon-linux/click_corpus/desktop_click_152.bin` — 75 raw 152 B messages, concatenated, all correlated with a timestamped human click.

**Decoding the state fields — guided capture 2026-08-21 `[C80]`**: a HELD-state protocol (`tools/capture_states_run.sh`, 167 s, with an instruction window; the states come from real `/dev/input` events, so a left/right mix-up by the operator distorts nothing). States obtained: L alone 13 s, R alone 13 s, M alone 12 s, **L+R simultaneously 13 s**, then releasing L alone.

**Two message structures**, mutually exclusive:

| | `@106` | `@136` | `@138` | `@144` | `@140` | `@150` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | 16 | 8 | 8 | 8 | 0 or 6 | 0, 1 or 2 |
| **B** | 18 | 0 | 6 | 6 | 8 | 0 |

Those four fields move **as a block**: they encode the structure, not the button. On isolated transitions, A lands on a `press` and B on `release`s → hypothesis **A = press, B = release** `[C70]` (only 3 isolated messages, to be confirmed).

**`@150` = button index `[C85]`** — the cleanest result, measured per held state: **L → 0, R → 1, M → 2**. So it is NOT a multi-button mask as was assumed: it is an identifier. `@140` co-varies (0 for L, 6 for R and M) without being as clean.

**The `LR` state produces no new value** (`@150` takes 0 or 1 there, `@140` 0 or 6): the protocol seems to emit **one message per button transition**, not a mask of the global state. That is the opposite of the initial intuition, and it is good news — a field to fill in rather than a state to maintain.

**Observed latency `[C90]`**: several seconds between the click and the on-screen reaction on the Shadow side (user observation, 2026-08-21). The message's EMISSION, by contrast, is near-immediate — measured `dt` of 0.015 to 0.3 s between the `/dev/input` event and the captured message. So the latency is in the round trip (VM → encoding → video), not in the client.

### FULL CLICK DECODING `[C95]` — slow guided capture 2026-08-21

An isolated-transition protocol (196 s, one action per step, 7-9 s of pause): **14 perfectly separated transitions**, each tied to its message with a `dt` of **0.001 to 0.011 s** (emission is immediate; the 0.1-0.3 s measured earlier came from the unbuffered hook slowing the client down — cf. the PERF fix).

| | `@106` | `@136` | `@138` | `@140` | `@144` | `@150` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PRESS** | 16 | 8 | 8 | **0** (left) / **6** (right, middle) | 8 | **0**=L **1**=R **2**=M |
| **RELEASE** | 18 | 0 | 6 | 8 | 6 | 0 |

- `@106/@136/@138/@144` **separate press from release** cleanly (no shared value): it is a structural variant, not a button field.
- **`@150` = button index, on PRESS only**: left 0, right 1, middle 2. On release it is always 0 — **the release message does not identify the button**, the server tracks the state.
- `@140`: 0 for a left press, 6 for right and middle, 8 for any release.
- **It is NOT a multi-button mask**: the simultaneous `LR` state produced no new value. The protocol emits **one message per transition**. Verified sequence: `press L`(@150=0) → `press R`(@150=1) → `release R`(structure B) → `release L`.

**The LEFT button emits NOTHING on release — confirmed `[C95]`** (2nd capture, 26 transitions):

| transition | 152 B message |
|---|---|
| `press L` | **6/6** |
| `press R` | **5/5** |
| `press M` | **2/2** |
| **`release L`** | **0/6 — never** |
| `release R` | **5/5** |
| `release M` | **2/2** |

The decisive case was `press L` → `press R` → **`release L` first** (right still held) → `release R`: the left release still emits nothing. **So it is not the release order but the button itself.** A plausible hypothesis: the primary click is transmitted as a single event on press, whereas right and middle need both edges (a context menu fires on release). Unverified, but the empirical rule is enough to implement.

**IMPLEMENTATION RULE**: send a message on press for all three buttons (`@150` = the index); send a release message **only for right and middle**. Emitting a left release would be a divergence from the official client.

**Implementation**: our six unwritten offsets are exactly those. All it takes is filling them in per the table above in `send_template_with_xy`, on top of the fields already patched (seq, u64 ts, coords).

### KEYBOARD — full decoding `[C95]` 2026-08-21 (K1)

A guided capture (held keys, a modifier, then **known phrases typed** — the maintainer's idea: a run of characters whose scancodes you know gives you a dozen correspondences at once). **51 reliable keyboard messages** (scancode matching the `/dev/input` event).

| field | value |
|---|---|
| **`@142`** | **evdev scancode** — 51/52 exact matches |
| **`@110`** | **6 on press, 0 on release** (25/26 and 26/26) |
| size | **144 B** (not 152 like the clicks) |

- **No separate modifier state**: `SHIFT_L` is encoded as an ordinary key, with its own scancode 42 and the same structure. Verified on the sequence Shift held → A pressed/released → Shift released.
- **The keyboard is symmetric**: press AND release both emit a message (52/52), unlike the mouse's left button, which emits nothing on release.
- The desktop's variable offsets: `40` (seq), `48-51` (timestamp), `96` (counter), `110` (state), `122` (role unknown), `142` (scancode). Everything else is constant.

**What our code was doing wrong** (fixed by K1):
1. `KBD_TEMPLATE` diverged on **50 constant bytes** — it was not the same message, most likely inherited from the WebRTC/browser era like the click templates. Replaced by a real desktop message; diff after replacement: **0 differences**.
2. The state field `@110` was **never written**: our presses and releases were indistinguishable.
3. We wrote `@114`, `@116` and `@128` with invented values (`0x09/0x00`, `0x08/0x09`, `0x01/0x00`) when those offsets are **constant** on the desktop (8, 10, 0) — we were corrupting structural fields.
4. Only `@142` (the scancode) was correct.

`SHADOW_INPUT_KBD_V2=0` restores the old behaviour.

### ⚠️ DIVERGENT BOOTSTRAP — our RegisterSession is 243 B, the desktop's 413 B `[C90]` 2026-08-21 (S2)

**The most serious lead for the input.** Diff of the `:base+11` bootstrap between the desktop **13.1.5** and us:

| message | Request.f | desktop (payload) | us | gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Capabilities | — | 87 B | 87 B | ✓ |
| **Authentication** | **f4** | **159 B** | ~305 B | ✗ |
| Encryption | f12 | 42 B | 42 B | ✓ |
| **RegisterSession** | **f10** | **413 B** | ~243 B | ✗ **−170 B** |
| 8 channel announcements | f8 | 115/99/99/103/95/97/103/99 | identical | ✓ |

The KB described this bootstrap as "byte-exact", but that validation dates from **May, against a 12.3.3 client**. The desktop is now on **13.1.5** (`f4` announces `"13.1.5"` and `"…App 9.9.10457;Launcher 4.87.2;Client 13.1.5"`, against our `"12.3.3"` / `"…9.9.10388;Launcher 4.85.6…"`). **Its messages have grown.**

So our `RegisterSession` is missing ~170 bytes of fields. That is the most plausible explanation for "the server ACKs but never enables the input": a client whose session registration is incomplete can be served video without being granted input.

**S3 — full EDID implemented, RegisterSession now byte-identical in size `[C95]`**: the desktop sends the **REAL EDID of the connected panel** — 384 bytes (base block + 2 extensions), with a valid `00 ff ff ff ff ff ff 00` header and an `fc` descriptor = `"PL2771Q"` (the user's Iiyama). We were only sending the base block's 128 bytes. The captured EDID is embedded (`SHADOW_EDID_384B`, toggle `SHADOW_EDID_FULL=0` to go back to 128 B), and the builder's buffers had to be enlarged — `display[256]` made the build fail silently, the message was then NOT sent and the bootstrap stopped on "RegisterSession FAIL".

**Result: our RegisterSession body is now 499 B, exactly like the desktop's**, and the server takes it into account — the SPS served changes (`403c016b60` instead of `403c0113f2e0`), so the negotiated resolution follows our new display descriptor. **The input still does not get through** (0 echoes, no effect).

**Remaining divergence identified `[C80]`**: our **Authentication (f4) is ~305 B against 159 B** on the desktop side — it is the last bootstrap message that does not match. To be decoded next.

**Observed desktop bootstrap sequence** (to be reproduced):
```
t+0.00  Capabilities        t+0.02  Authentication (f4)
t+0.28  Encryption (f12)    t+0.31  RegisterSession (f10, 413 B)
t+0.49  8× announcements (f8)    t+0.52  heartbeat (f3)
t+1.21  f7 + f6   ← TWO messages we do not send
t+1.31  f7 + f6   (repeated)
then    f3 in a loop at 2 Hz, and NOTHING else
```

**Hypotheses already ELIMINATED for the input** (do not replay them): the event content (byte-exact, clicks and keyboard), a missing 96 B keepalive (added, K2), surplus control messages (removed, `SHADOW_CTRL_STRICT=1`), focus (F1, refuted and harmful).

### Input channel — two official-client behaviours we were not reproducing `[C95]` 2026-08-21 (K2)

**1. The 96 B message is a PERIODIC KEEPALIVE, not a "Connect"**: the guided capture shows the official client emitting it **19 times in 135 s, exactly 7.50 s apart** — only the counter (`@40`) and the timestamp (`@48-51`) vary, the other 91 bytes are constant. We only sent it **once**, when opening the channel. Implemented (K2, `SHADOW_INPUT_KEEPALIVE_MS=7500`, `0` to disable).

**2. The channel has a RETURN DIRECTION we never receive**: the desktop receives **104 B** messages carrying the same FlatBuffer header as our input messages (`64 00 00 00 18 00 00 00`), but with **`@35 = 0x04` instead of `0x03`** — a direction discriminant (0x03 = client→server, 0x04 = server→client, verified: 95/95 of the messages written carry `0x03`). They carry **cursor coordinates** at `@96`/`@98` (e.g. `0x04ab`=1195, `0x0244`=580) and `@36 = 0x20`. **We receive ZERO of them**, in every session.

**State after K2**: the keepalive goes out correctly (`wr=96`, every 7.5 s) but **nothing changes** — no echo, no effect on the VM. So the keepalive alone is not enough.

**What this lets us conclude**: the content of our messages is byte-exact (clicks, keyboard, Connect), the channel is open, the keepalive is now present, and the server **still does not enable the return direction**. So a condition is missing upstream — most likely on the **control channel**: a channel announcement, an authorisation, or a message the desktop sends on `:base+11` and we do not. That is where to look, not in the event format.

**What remains unexplored**:
- the REASON for the left button's asymmetry (the rule itself is established);
- old: **confirm A=press / B=release** — done, cf. the table above on a larger sample: the capture only has 24 messages of 152 B and 7 isolated transitions, the others being too close together;
- **the exact role of `@140`** (0/6/8) once A and B are separated;
- old: **the meaning of `@106/@136/@138/@144`** (a 55/20 partition) and confirming `@140` on a larger sample of ISOLATED clicks — the capture contains few, the clicks having been chained too quickly;
- an old note, now false: the 152 B click templates. A byte-exact specimen of the desktop's second message type (144 B, vtable `0x20` instead of `0x1c`, 4 occurrences in the capture) is available in `/tmp/desktop_click_144.pkl` and reproducible through `tools/client/hook_log_parser.py`;
- **the server no longer sends anything back**: `RX 10 B (echo/ack)` was present in the very first GUI sessions, absent since — already intermittent BEFORE any change in this session, so not a regression, but a signal to understand;
- a **desktop capture dedicated to focus** (alt-tabbing while recording) would settle whether `FOCUS_CHANGED` really exists on that channel: the June capture contains none, but the user probably never left the window.

**Old FOCUS lead (kept for the record) `[C70]`**: `ctrl_comchan.h` documents `:base+14` as an event bus carrying `type=1 FOCUS_CHANGED` and `type=2 WINDOW_STATE_CHANGED`. But **`comchan` NEVER connects** (0/5, cf. §3.17). If the server only applies input to a client it believes is **focused**, that would explain exactly "it ACKs the Connect but applies no event". To be tested first — and it ties in with the fact that `comchan` and `input-tcp` fight over the same port.

- **Refs**: `ctrl_input_tcp.c` (templates + I8), `tools/client/hook_log_parser.py`, `SHADOW_DUMP_INPUT=1` → `shadow-client-data/our_input.bin`
- **Cross-ref**: §3.11, §5.3, §3.17
