On console it is worse than on desktop: the file arrives over FTP, the channel answers once, then goes silent without a word — and you conclude the network channel is broken. Fix in three parts: `load()` goes and LOOKS at the disk when the key is absent (the file IS the state), on both of its paths including the total absence of a settings file; `save()` no longer writes a value that is not established; and the disarming, when the setting legitimately wins over a file that is present, SAYS SO along with its remedy. Verified on both cases: key absent + file present -> channel armed, 619 mirrored lines, no rename; `canal_dev=0` + file present -> the setting wins, and the line `[channel] logsink.txt found but the setting says OFF` goes into the log.
  **CANAL-2 — and the same block overwrote the address the tool had just written.** When `logsink.txt` AND `logsink.off` both exist, re-arming did `rename(off -> txt)`: but `rename` replaces its target, so the current address went under the disarmed copy, the OLDER of the two by construction. Observed on the console the same day, right after `switch-logsink.sh setup`. **That day both addresses were live** (same machine, one per interface: wired and Wi-Fi) — so the PROVEN defect is that the deliberate choice is silently discarded, not that the channel dies; it dies the day the leftover names a vanished interface, and suspicion then falls on the network. A destination already present wins, and the stale guard copy is deleted.
  **DEVL-3 — the console build was BROKEN since the merge, and nothing said so.** `ctrl_session.c` places its `#include <pthread.h>` after `ctrl_audio_dtls.h`, which pulls in wolfSSL; but `wolfssl/options.h` does `#undef _POSIX_THREADS` and newlib hides half of pthread.h behind that macro — mutex, create, join; the reader/writer locks stay visible, hence a message that suggests `pthread_rwlock_unlock` and misleads. So the file compiles on Linux and on Windows and fails for the CONSOLE ALONE. The trap is already documented in `ctrl_tcp.c`, `ctrl_video_tcp.c` and `ctrl_input_tcp.c`: this is the FOURTH file to pay for it, and it was introduced by a batch that no loop compiles for the Switch — the exact mirror image of the warning already recorded here about Windows.
  Refs: `clients/borealis/devlink/devlink.cpp`, `clients/borealis/settings.cpp`, `clients/borealis/include/settings.hpp`, `core/protocol/ctrl_session.c`, `tools/client/DEVLINK.md`.

- **2026-09-12 (Windows client, K21 + HOLD-1 + CFG-5: three defects reported in use)** — **TWO OF THEM WERE NOT COMPILED OFF-CONSOLE.** `[C95+]` for the causes, read in the code and confirmed by the build; `[C60]` for the result ON SCREEN, **not verified** — see the last line. Reported: in the VM list, the footer names the buttons of a gamepad that is not there; the pause menu's "link quality" page shows "hold B to go back" and never exits, the game continuing behind it; "apply now" on the video side does not seem to apply cleanly, and does not exist at all on the AUDIO side.
  **K21 — our screens do not go through Borealis' hint bar.** They draw their OWN footer from a `std::vector<ui::Hint>` whose button field is a letter HARDCODED at fifteen call sites; the K20 fix (`patches/borealis/hint.extract`), which already names the keys when no gamepad is plugged in, therefore could not reach them. The table moves into `clients/borealis/ui/key_label.h` — pure, no Borealis, no NanoVG, no global — and BOTH loops that paint hints (`clients/borealis/ui/screen.cpp`, `clients/borealis/ui/steps.cpp`) run the DRAWN text through it; the `HintBox` keeps the LOGICAL id, which the touch test matches on, which avoids touching the fifteen sites. Since a round 24 px pill cannot hold "Enter", `paint::hintButton` widens into a rectangular KEY when the name overflows — decided by MEASURING the text rather than counting characters, "↑" being three bytes for one glyph. The condition is the NUMBER OF GAMEPADS, never the platform; it inherits K20's blind spot (`glfw_input.cpp` only increments `controllersCount` in the plug callback, so a gamepad present BEFORE launch counts as zero) — at least the two bars stay consistent with each other. `tests/test_key_label.c`, 28 checks.
  **HOLD-1 — the hold that closes the three testers was wrapped in an `#ifdef __SWITCH__`.** Off-console it was NOT COMPILED: `draw()` could never return true, and those pages have no other exit — `closeNetTest()`, `closeMouseTest()` and `closePadTest()` are called from nowhere. So all three were unquittable on desktop, not just the one that was reported; `pad_test` for a neighbouring but distinct reason (its code was compiled, but it read `rd.pressed[pad::B]`, i.e. `readConsole()`, entirely under `#ifdef __SWITCH__` and therefore always empty elsewhere). An `#ifdef` that excludes code produces neither a warning nor a failing test: that is what let all three through. The timing moves into `clients/borealis/ui/hold_exit.h` (pure, state owned by the caller, never a function `static`) and the button read into `clients/borealis/ui/hold_exit.hpp`: libnx on console, UNCHANGED, and elsewhere Borealis' unified state — the right choice and not merely the convenient one, since `glfw_input.cpp` adds the keyboard ON TOP of the buttons (Escape feeds `BUTTON_B`) while respecting the A/B swap; reading the Escape key directly would have exited on the wrong key half the time and ignored a gamepad plugged into a desktop. Second part: `stream_activity.cpp` only consumed B for `padTestOpen()`, so every press meant to leave the page went to the VM as a RIGHT CLICK — the report's "I can hear the game moving behind it". The sentences now name the real key (`net/hint`, `menu/pm_hint`, `pad/sortie` take a `{}`). `tests/test_hold_exit.c`, 130 checks, including the counter-case that matters: releasing CANCELS.
  **CFG-5 — the gesture left no trace, and the AUDIO page had no equivalent.** Two live session logs carried only `[UI] video demandee … src=g19` lines: **no send of user origin**, with no way to distinguish "the press did not land" from "there was no press in those sessions" — so the cause of the complaint is NOT established. `ctrl_session_set_video_config` now logs the QUEUING, before the loop comes into play. The notification names the value that left (`menu/applied_bitrate`, "Bitrate sent back to the session: N Mb/s") instead of an indifferent "Settings sent", and the description says that frame rate and resolution wait for the next connection (CFG-4). The AUDIO page gets the same gesture: it re-applies volume and equaliser — local, hence with no session guard, which also fixes the case where `env.txt` forced `SHADOW_VOLUME` — and its description says that the codec, for its part, waits for a reconnection.
  **Offline**: Linux (WSL) 139,003 checks, 0 failures; Windows (MinGW) 41 suites, 0 failures, both new suites included; `verify_i18n.py` 518 keys, 0 failures; `shadow-client.exe` rebuilt. **Not verified, and that is this batch's limit**: the result ON SCREEN. Five approaches failed — a screenshot aiming off target because of DPI scaling (twice private content, deleted), a single `devlink shot` catching only the boot screen (t≈0.66 s, the VM list arriving at 1.85 s), and a multi-command scenario made impossible because **the devlink connection drops after the FIRST command on this desktop**, with `attends` as with `--cadence`. To be confirmed by a human, or on console.
  Refs: `clients/borealis/ui/key_label.h`, `clients/borealis/ui/key_hint.hpp`, `ui/hold_exit.{h,hpp}`, `clients/borealis/ui/paint.cpp`, `clients/borealis/ui/screen.cpp`, `clients/borealis/ui/steps.cpp`, `ui/{net,mouse,pad}_test.cpp`, `clients/borealis/activity/stream_activity.cpp`, `core/protocol/ctrl_session.c`, `tests/test_key_label.c`, `tests/test_hold_exit.c`.
- **2026-09-11 (audio stack, OUT-1 + AUD-INS-4: output underruns, counted on both sides and confirmed — W6)** — **NO OUTPUT COULD SEE ITS OWN GAPS.** `[C95+]` for the rule, offline; `[C80]` in service on WASAPI (real engine into digital silence, then eighteen live sessions); `[C60]` for ALSA and audout, whose hooks have not yet run on any real device. `snd_pcm_recover` returns 0 for -EPIPE/-ESTRPIPE/-EINTR and, with `silent=1`, writes nothing: `writei FAIL` only reports a failed RECOVERY; audout only counted overflow. W6a delivers the pure rule, `core/protocol/audio_gap.h` (per-decoder state, atomic counters, no clock and no `getenv`): an underrun is only counted if the output is OBSERVED dry at fill time, if the frame accepted before the gap is audible, if the filling frame is audible too, and if the next one arrives within 30 ms, also audible. Audibility is the peak of the DECODED PCM, read before EQ and gain (> 16: blind below -66 dBFS). `xrun=` counts raw -EPIPEs SEPARATELY. `tests/test_audio_gap.c`: 158 checks, including 13 counter-cases, against the evaluation's known-truth simulator (reproduced bit for bit under MinGW and under glibc) — muted VM: 0 underruns, where counting every -EPIPE gives 88 in 90 s; an 80 ms outage: 1 on ALSA and 4 on audout, the truth, where a single-sided rule sees only 1 out of 4; nine sound endings: 0 false positives, against 18; the same counts at volume 0, because the peak is read before the gain (read after, everything disappears). Offline after W6a: Linux (WSL) 44 suites, 138,845 checks; Windows 46, 3,248; 0 failures (+158 on each side). AUD-INS-4, verified in the code: on console, the FLAC report writes `deborde=0` for life (it reads `buffers_dropped`, which only ALSA increments), while `[L5] file audio ... jetes=` carries the real count. **W5 measurement worth keeping for the hooks**: on WASAPI, "fill 0 on wake-up" is NOT an underrun (1674 wake-ups out of 2945 at rest, queue flat to ±5 ppm) — in event-driven mode with two periods, the engine has just taken our period when its event fires.
  **W6b — wired up, and measured live.** Each output has its hook, on the thread that feeds it: audout, a question to the driver (`played == samples_submitted`) only when 2 buffers or fewer are in flight; ALSA, XRUN state or `snd_pcm_delay <= 0` when a chunk comes back after a wait, raw -EPIPEs going to `xrun=`, separately; WASAPI, where the "dry" observation is taken ONE WAKE-UP EARLIER — the engine asks, the ring has nothing to give, the engine holds less than a period — and not at fill time: read at fill time it reads 0 whether the engine starved or not. That is the difference between an instrument and a noise counter: on the engine model, 0 false positives across 9 scenarios against **1833** for the literal "fill 0" reading over 60 s; on the real engine, that reading counts **449 / 185 / 63** underruns in 20 s of healthy music, the rule none. Published as ` sousflux=N (X ms) xrun=M` at the end of the session summary, of the 500-frame FLAC line and of `[L5] file audio`, only where an output is measured — never a `0` where nothing is — with `xrun=n/a` off ALSA; plus one `audio: sous-flux #N (X ms)` line per underrun, 20 per session, so that a `[D4]` freeze can be matched by its timestamp. AUD-INS-4: `aud_drops()` is read by the FLAC report AND by `audio_decoder_get_stats`, so `deborde=` finally agrees with `[L5] file audio`'s `jetes=` on console. The L12 and L13 claims of "no underruns (`writei FAIL` = 0)" are WITHDRAWN, with their reason. The playback thread is joined BEFORE the summary: its counters are final there. Toggles `SHADOW_AUD_SOUSFLUX` (1) and `SHADOW_DIAG_AUDIO_MUTE_AT_S` / `_MS` (off / 300).
  **Live** (Windows desktop, night of 11-12 September, `SHADOW_VOLUME=0`, continuous sound on the VM; these figures hold only for the WASAPI engine). **L1**, 5 sessions of 60 s: 3 out of 5 read `sousflux=0`. **The bench's start-up gaps did NOT reproduce** — the agent saw 1 to 2, of 1 to 21 ms, at every device open, because its producer followed the timer's 15.6 ms quantum; in the real client, the producer is driven by the NETWORK. Session 5 counts 24 underruns for 3050 ms, **and that is not a counter defect: the three instruments agree at the same instant** — `[D4] video RETABLIE — coupure de 761 ms`, `[AUD17] pendant la coupure video : audio perdues=0, plus long silence d'arrivee=759 ms`, `[AUD17] trou audio : 5 trames (arrivee +760 ms)`, then `audio: sous-flux #1 (711 ms)`. Reading: the link DELAYED the packets without losing a single one, the output played silence for that long, then the backlog arrived all at once (50 `[L13]` catch-ups) and the queue oscillated for ~5 s (gaps of 60 to 90 ms); summary `perdues=5 (0.08%) trous=1`. **This is the cross-attribution this work was done for — loss, delay and output silence finally readable apart — and it happened on its own, on a WIRED desktop.** Session 1 carries an isolated 18 ms gap with no correlate at all (no `[D4]`, no `[AUD17] trou`, no `[L13]`): about two engine periods, unattributable. **Positive control**, 300 ms injected at t=20 s: L2 (Opus, 5 sessions of 40 s) 5/5, one `injection` line of 260-270 ms per session, plus a `reprise` of 9-10 ms in 3 sessions out of 5 (the queue restarts empty), 0 OTHER lines; L3 (FLAC, 3 sessions of 40 s) 3/3, 269-280 ms, 0 OTHER, and the 500-frame FLAC line does carry the keys. The gap between 300 ms injected and 260-280 ms measured is what the queue still held at the moment of the cut: **the counter measures the OUTPUT's silence, not the duration of the injection.**
  **Control arms**: L4 (`SHADOW_AUD_SOUSFLUX=0`, 3 sessions of 30 s), 3/3 with no `sousflux=` key and no `sous-flux` line, the summary's other keys keeping their shape; L5 (`SHADOW_WIN_AUDIO=0`, 2 sessions of 30 s), 2/2 with no key either, with `poussees=n/a deborde=n/a` and `[L5] audio/file` never fed — the decode-only behaviour of before. **Neither arm writes a zero where nothing is measured**, which is what these keys were written for. Full live W6 series: L1 5 sessions, L2 5/5, L3 3/3, L4 3/3, L5 2/2.
  **Not measured**: ALSA (the hook compiles with 0 warnings on Linux, and its rule comes from the bench) and audout (compiled against the mock; whether the console has start-up gaps too is unknown); `xrun=` only means anything on ALSA. **Instruments that change meaning**: the `sousflux=` and `xrun=` keys at the end of three lines; the `audio: sous-flux #N` and `[DIAG] audio coupee/retablie` families; on console, `deborde=` now includes `buf_drop_count`, so it agrees with `[L5] ... jetes=`.
  Refs: `core/protocol/audio_gap.h`, `tests/test_audio_gap.c`.

- **2026-09-11 (audio stack, OUT-3: Windows has an audio output — WASAPI behind the ring)** — **THE WINDOWS CLIENT DECODED THE SOUND AND THREW IT AWAY.** `[C95+]` for the operation, live on this desktop; PARTIAL success against the plan's criteria; not listened to. Why event-driven WASAPI (OUT-3 evaluation, client conditions): waveOut with three buffers ran at ×0.23-0.82 of real time with 97 to 582 overflows, and `timeBeginPeriod(1)`, which rescued it, changes the whole process (`WSAPoll(5)` 15.4 -> 5.3 ms: the ING-1/ING-2 baselines would move); nothing calls `timeBeginPeriod`. Shape: `media/audio_out_win.{c,h}`, shared event-driven mode, 48 kHz 16-bit stereo, conversion by the engine, 20 ms buffer (22 granted, 10 ms period), ole32 and avrt alone at link time, behind ALSA's ring and playback thread (`HAVE_RING`): chunks of 480 frames, `eq_on_buffer` then `gain_on_buffer`. **The device opens ON THE PLAYBACK THREAD** (83 ms to 1.2 s), never in `audio_decoder_create`; until it is READY, the producer discards (`audio: sortie WASAPI : … echantillons decodes sans sortie`); a failed open leaves the decoder in decode-only mode (`audio: WASAPI indisponible`, summary `n/a`); device lost: reopened at most once per second, ring emptied on return. DEC-4's `audio: taux` report becomes common to Linux and Windows. Switch pre-processing unchanged; Linux -2/+29 lines, all of them from OUT-5 and from two `%zu` -> `%u` formats with unchanged text.
  **Offline** (the real `audio.c`, the real sink, digital silence). **Confounder**: the ambient load of Kontakt 8 on ASIO and OBS recording; and the default FiiO DAC answered `0x800700AA` (busy), so the figures come from the Realtek, at 48 kHz, with no sample-rate conversion. At rest, every Opus cell passes (9 runs: p50 20-40 ms, p99 30-70 ms, ±10 ppm, 0 L13). Under 4 busy threads, `burst/h4` fails 3 times out of 6 (L13, overflow, down to -4992 ppm); at 12 threads, the output collapses (27.4 s written out of 30). Lifecycle: handles back to baseline after 3 create/destroy cycles (destroy 9.5 to 19 ms); forced open failure = decode only; injected device loss = reopens more than 1 s apart, then recovery; `SHADOW_WIN_AUDIO=0` = decode counters equal to the previous code.
  **Live** (Windows desktop, `SHADOW_VOLUME=0`, continuous sound on the VM, 3 sessions of 60 s per arm): `WASAPI ouvert en 577 / 151 / 146 ms — tampon 22.0 ms (1056 trames), periode 10.0 ms`; `[L5] audio/file` fed; summary `poussees` = frames decoded; `0 reouverture(s)`. Queue in steady state: p50 30-41 ms, p99 31-51 ms; session 2 ends on a plateau at 61-74 ms (p99 73.7, above the plan's 70), with no catch-up — the ring, of the same design as ALSA's, does not absorb a backlog below the L13 ceiling. `[L13]` 5 / 1 / 1, ALL at start-up (0.2-0.7 s after `[K12] accord audio`: the start-of-stream burst, cross-faded by OUT-5), 0 in steady state. With `SHADOW_WIN_AUDIO=0`: `sortie Windows coupee`, `audio/file` never fed, `poussees=n/a` — exactly the decode-only behaviour of before. **Verdict**: the output works; the plan's criteria hold, except the start-up catch-ups and session 2's plateau; by ear, still to do. Since W5, the equaliser, the volume ramp (EQV1, EQV2) and OUT-5's cross-fade can also be listened to on this desktop, with a non-FLAT preset — not done yet. **Instruments that change meaning**: on Windows, `[L5] audio/file` measures THIS engine's queue (ring + filling of its buffer); `poussees`/`deborde` are numbers as soon as a device has been ready, `n/a` otherwise; `buffers_pushed` counts pushes into the ring, as on Linux; new lines `audio: WASAPI ouvert`, `WASAPI indisponible`, `WASAPI perdu`, `WASAPI rouvert`, `sortie WASAPI : …`, `sortie Windows coupee`.
  Refs: `media/audio_out_win.{c,h}`, `core/media/audio.c` (OUT-3, `win_play_loop`), `CMakeLists.txt` (ole32 avrt).

- **2026-09-11 (audio stack, OUT-3: MMCSS on the WASAPI playback thread)** — **SHIPPED ON, ON PARTIAL EVIDENCE.** `[C60]` for the effect. "Pro Audio" (`AvSetMmThreadCharacteristicsW`, `avrt`) for the playback thread only, behind `SHADOW_WIN_AUDIO_MMCSS` (1). The plan only allowed it if a loaded cell failed; `burst/h4` was failing. Interleaved A/B, 3 × 30 s per arm, same confounder as the matrix (Kontakt 8 on ASIO, OBS, Realtek device): `burst/h4`, 0 failed runs out of 3 with MMCSS, 3 out of 6 without (1 out of 3 in the A/B, 2 out of 3 in the matrix) — suggestive, and small; no cell is worse; `steady` shows no difference. Under saturation (12 threads, every core), both arms break and overlap (37 / 51 / 40 L13-or-overflow events with MMCSS, 51 / 38 / 47 without; the engine consumes 4.6 to 8.9 % less than real time), and about half the starved wake-ups found the ring EMPTY: it is the producer — the session's receive thread — that is late, and no priority given to the playback thread reaches it. The measurement ledger therefore concludes "MMCSS not retained" as an established effect; it still ships on because the plan names it as the remedy for a failing loaded cell, because no cell is worse with it, and because it is per-thread. `0` = normal priority. To be settled on a quiet machine: 10 sessions per arm, comparing `audio/file` p99, `[L13]` and `deborde`.
  Refs: `core/media/audio.c` (`win_mmcss_on`), `core/media/audio_out_win.c` (`mmcss_begin`).

None of them could ever complete a frame — the two pieces of a split frame carry different bytes 6-9 (AUD12: a send timestamp, not a frame id), and two separately sealed blobs would fail Poly1305 anyway. Audio frames go, as before, through the single-packet path and reassembly by byte 1; the fallback never served them. **An instrument goes away**: `sufp: window full` no longer appears by default; it never meant an audio loss.

- **2026-09-11 (audio stack, AUD-INS-1: the panel's audio gauge)** — **THE ONLY AUDIO GAUGE GRADED A MUTED VM AS A FAILURE, AND NO LINE CARRIED THE LOSS.** `[C95+]` for the code and the bench; `[C80]` for the rendering: the panel does not open remotely (devlink presses nothing). "Frames/s" was graded against 95/60 frames per second, whereas the server sends FRAMES and not time: a muted VM sends 2.5 to 5 per second, and the official client receives no more (§3.26, AUD14) — so every quiet moment in a game showed up red. The gauge keeps its value and its scale (100 frames/s = real time) but is drawn in a NEUTRAL colour. Health now reads on a new "Frames lost" line (`hud/audio_lost`, fr "Trames perdues"), fed by `opus_lost` (AUD-DEDUP-3): shown only when non-zero (the S119 rule), and graded (Warning above 0.5 %, Bad above 2 %) only when the last 10 seconds expected at least 500 frames, i.e. sound that is playing; below that, a neutral count — otherwise one frame lost out of 50 from a muted VM would show 2 %. The 10 s window is fed on every draw, panel open or not: a counter that is DISPLAYED is not a counter that is WRITTEN. `tests/test_audio_loss.c` carries 12 grading checks, including the silence floor and its counter-case. The comment on the default `0x27` mask is corrected: the video and audio sections have always been off by default. **An instrument changes meaning**: "Frames/s" no longer grades anything. To verify on screen (a human, or the held-key bench): with `SHADOW_DIAG_AUDIO_SEQ_JUMP_AT_S=30`, the line should appear around t=35 s, `3 (0.3 %)`, in neutral.
  Refs: `clients/borealis/activity/stream_view.cpp` (AUD-INS-1), `core/protocol/audio_loss.h` (`audio_loss_win_grade`), `resources/i18n/{fr,en-US}/shadow.json` (`hud/audio_lost`), `tests/test_audio_loss.c`.

- **2026-09-11 (audio stack, AUD-DEDUP-3: audio loss, counted where the sequence numbers are)** — **THE DE-DUPLICATION WINDOW COMPUTED EVERY SEQUENCE GAP AND THREW IT AWAY: NO COUNTER CARRIED AUDIO LOSS.** `[C95+]` on a Windows desktop (the plumbing, on a wired link); the instrument's value is on a Wi-Fi console, not measured yet. Losing BOTH copies of a run of frames — a burst, the console's ~300 ms freeze — left no trace at all. An exact accountant (`core/protocol/audio_loss.h`) is fed ONLY the sequence numbers the window accepts, so it changes nothing about what is played (0 different decisions over ~1.2 M packets, offline): a 64-number bitmap, a loss becomes DEFINITIVE when its number leaves the bitmap without having been seen (64 frames later, 0.64 s with sound), and a jump of more than 6000 in either direction is a renumbering, not a loss. Bench: bursts 330/330, jitter 0 false losses (the naive "gap - 1" rule counts 25 per 100,000 at 2 % jitter), 1 s outages 716/716 (the naive rule hides 700 in the "jump" bucket), 2^32 wraparound 1500/1500, muted VM 24/24 (a "previous frame active" guard hid all of them). Published at 4 Hz in the NET group (`opus_lost`); five fields in `ctrl_session_stats` (`audio_frames_lost`, `audio_holes`, `audio_hole_max`, `audio_renum`, `audio_stale`). New `[AUD17]` family: `trou audio` (a provisional gap of 5 frames or more, 40 lines per session), `pendant la coupure video : audio perdues=…, plus long silence d'arrivee=… ms` right after every `[D4] video RETABLIE` (unchanged), and `bilan de session : acceptees=… perdues=… (…%) trous=… plus_long=… tardives=… renum=… deltas 1/2-3/4-64/>64=…/…/…/… vieux=…`. The 5 s stats line gains `aud_perdues=` (DEFINITIVE loss, 64 frames behind) and `aud_vieux=` (refusals 64 numbers or more behind the window: the S36 class, made visible) at the end of the line, and its buffer goes from 560 to 768 bytes — a saturated line was already 611 characters and lost its tail (`lot=`, `ecart_max=`) before this fix. The `[D4]` line and the summary count definitive loss + pending: a 300 ms outage loses ~30 frames, fewer than the 64 that make a loss definitive.
  **Live** (Windows desktop with no audio output, GUI client in autotest, continuous sound on the VM, 3 sessions of 60 s per arm, 15 s video outage injected): `aud_perdues=0 aud_vieux=0` at end of session; `[AUD17] pendant la coupure video : audio perdues=0, plus long silence d'arrivee=` 15-17 ms (52 ms once) — the witness attributes the outage to the video path alone; summary `acceptees~6480 perdues=0 trous=0 renum=0`, deltas ~6480/0/0/0: **the numbering advances by +1 per frame sent, confirmed with continuous sound** (§3.26); no `trou` line. With an injected sequence jump (`SHADOW_DIAG_AUDIO_SEQ_JUMP_AT_S=30`), 3 sessions out of 3: `aud_perdues=3 aud_vieux=7`, summary `perdues=3 (0.05%) trous=1 plus_long=3 deltas ...=~6500/0/1/0 vieux=7` — the 7 packets following the injected number are refused as too old, the 8th resynchronises the window (ING-A1), i.e. ~4 duplicate frames of which 3 never accepted: **a renumbering costs 30 ms of sound, and the accountant sees it**; `aud_pub` keeps going (~500 per 5 s). No toggle: counting only, the decision to accept stays identical to the bit (precedent: S117's loss publication). `tests/test_audio_loss.c`: 84 checks, against ING-A1's real window.
  **Still to do, and it is the whole point**: console on portable Wi-Fi, continuous sound, 370 s or more. Every `[D4]` line classifies its outage: loss ~ duration / 10 ms = the link LOST; 0 loss and a silence ~ the duration = the link DELAYED; 0 loss and normal arrivals = the video path. Useful if at least 8 of the ~11 freezes classify cleanly. The `[G44]` micro-freezes have no companion line: match them by timestamp to the `[AUD17] trou` lines. Limit: a single lost copy reads 0, the ×2 redundancy hides it.
  Refs: `core/protocol/audio_loss.h`, `core/protocol/ctrl_session.c` (AUD-DEDUP-3, `aud_loss_accepted`, `[AUD17]`), `core/protocol/ctrl_session.h`, `core/protocol/ctrl_session_int.h`, `core/common/stats.h` (`opus_lost`), `tests/test_audio_loss.c`.

- **2026-09-11 (audio stack, AUD-INS-3: the panel's audio counters went through the VIDEO thread)** — **DURING A VIDEO OUTAGE, THE PANEL'S AUDIO GAUGE FELL TO ZERO WHILE THE SOUND WAS PLAYING.** `[C95+]` on a Windows desktop. The panel's `opus_*` fields were only written by `glue_decode_frame`, once every 30 decoded video frames: real-time offline replay (real libopus), the gauge fell to 0 from +2.8 s into a video outage and read 0 on 81 % of the outage's draws. They now leave from `on_audio`, at most every 250 ms, on the thread that decodes them (`SHADOW_AUDIO_STATS_TICK`). Along the way, a race: the two writers of the whole structure (read, modify, publish) were losing updates. `webrtc_stats_merge` now publishes by GROUP (NET, VIDEO, AUDIO), one writer thread per group, and no application code calls `webrtc_stats_publish` any more. `tests/test_stats.c`: 19 checks; under stress, 0 regressions over 10.55 M + 4.98 M publications (MinGW) and 3.71 M + 1.44 M (WSL), where the old method hits its 20-regression cap within a few hundred. **Live A/B** (Windows desktop with no audio output, continuous sound, 15 s video outage injected via `SHADOW_DIAG_VIDEO_MUTE_AT_S=20` / `_MS=15000`, 3 sessions of 60 s per arm): with `SHADOW_AUDIO_STATS_TICK=0`, `aud_pub=` FROZEN (0, 0, 0 per 5 s) during the outage in 3 sessions out of 3, while 500 frames per 5 s are being played; by default, `aud_pub=` +484 to +514 per 5 s during the outage, 3/3. **Instruments that change meaning**: the `[glue] stats` line gains ` aud_pub=` at the end — the count of audio frames AS THE PANEL SEES IT (`opus_decoded`), not the session's `audio=`; "Frames/s" at 0 now means "no frame decoded for 3 s", no longer "no video"; a RateMeter step backwards (L21) can now only come from a real restart. Not measured: the panel's rendering (devlink does not open it).
  Refs: `core/stats.{c,h}` (`webrtc_stats_merge`), `core/protocol/ctrl_session_glue.c` (AUD-INS-3, `glue_fill_audio_group`), `core/protocol/ctrl_session.c` (4 Hz block, VI1 cleanup), `tests/test_stats.c`.
- **2026-09-11 (audio stack, EQV1: the equaliser reset its filter on every setting change)** — **EVERY TOGGLE IN THE AUDIO MENU — HARDWARE OPUS, QUALITY — PRODUCED A BROADBAND CLICK, EVEN WITHOUT CHANGING THE EQUALISER AT ALL.** `[C95+]` offline (the real `eq.c`, gcc 16.1 and 14.2); `[C80]` by ear: this desktop has no audio output and its preset is FLAT, nothing audible or measurable here live. **[Since OUT-3 (W5), this desktop has an output: the equaliser runs behind WASAPI and can be listened to with a non-FLAT preset — not done yet.]** `audio_eq_configure` zeroed the whole filter memory on every call, and every menu toggle goes through `applyToggles -> applyEq`: on music decoded from Opus, median 3955 LSB, worst -8 dBFS, ~9 ms; sweeping the notches: p90 9815, worst 16,552 LSB. (a) Re-applying IDENTICAL settings no longer touches anything (`eq_same`, bit-exact, no toggle): the log writes `[S90] egaliseur : … — inchange`. (b) A REAL change warms up for 20 ms alongside the filter in service then cross-fades in 10 ms (`SHADOW_EQ_CROSSFADE`): never worse than the reset across 19,440 cases (4 presets, every menu notch, 7 signals, 9 instants), worst notch 16,552 -> 639-769 LSB, worst profile change 15,637 -> 150-266; 14.7 µs per 10 ms buffer in steady state (14.8 before), 30.7 µs during a change's 30 ms, lock held 16-20 ns by the settings thread. Refuted by measurement: the raw carry-over (worst 28,430 LSB) and the EXACT per-band carry-over alone (1541 cases out of 19,440 worse than before by more than 16 LSB). (c) Volume now ramps across a buffer (`SHADOW_VOLUME_RAMP`): a 100 -> 0 % jump was 7455 LSB, 0 against an ideal 480-frame ramp; ALL THREE gain sites change together (the S90d lesson). The port is bit-identical to the measured implementation (57.6 M samples, 0 differences). `tests/test_eq.c` 85 -> 133 checks (26 fail on the previous code), `tests/test_audio_gain.c` 9 -> 16 (1 fails). **To verify by ear on console**, handheld, bass-heavy music, one arm per `env.txt`: a click on most presses before, on none after; expected suffixes `— inchange` (toggle), `— fondu enchaine (20 ms de chauffe, 10 ms de fondu)` (notch), `— bascule immediate, filtre remis a zero` (notch, `SHADOW_EQ_CROSSFADE=0`). A new setting is heard 20 ms after the press and is complete at 30 ms: to be judged while fine-tuning.
  Refs: `streaming/eq.{c,h}` (`eq_same`, `eq_chain_*`), `core/protocol/audio_gain.h` (`audio_gain_ramp`), `core/media/audio.c` (EQV1, `gain_on_buffer`), `tests/test_eq.c`, `tests/test_audio_gain.c`.

- **2026-09-11 (audio stack, EQV2: the equaliser's filter memory carried from one session to the next)** — **`eq_reset` HAD NO CALLER AT ALL: THE END OF ONE SESSION WAS HEARD AT THE START OF THE NEXT.** `[C95+]` offline, against the real `audio.c`; `[C80]` by ear (handheld console, PORTABLE preset by default; Linux with a non-FLAT preset; Windows never runs the equaliser, and the FLAT preset is an exact workaround; **[since OUT-3 (W5), Windows does run it]**). `g_eq` is process-global and only `audio_eq_configure` reset it — at start-up, on saving a setting, on a mode change — never at session creation, contrary to the contract written in `eq.h`. `audio_eq_session_start()` resets it just before every `audio_decoder_create()`, in the glue; no code touching the equaliser runs between the two. Bench against the real `audio.c` compiled 8 times within one process (ALSA/Opus, audout/Opus, audout/FLAC; 5 presets, 5 materials, 10 cut points, 2 session starts): before, 628 session starts out of 3000 identical to a clean start (the 600 FLAT ones and 28 FLAC cuts that fell in silence), PORTABLE median -24.0 dBFS, worst -8.1 dBFS, gone in 13.2 ms, HEADPHONES 68.4 ms; after, **3000/3000**, and 1500/1500 on 20 ms frames, 0 LSB. Control: EQV1's chain WITHOUT the call reproduces the old line exactly — it is the reset that fixes it. No toggle (precedent of the session resets). Deliberately left aside: the mid-session AUD16 revival (a reset under a live stream is itself a ~-21 dBFS transient; it waits until AUD16b has served) and the legacy `dc_session.cpp` path (Linux, `SHADOW_NATIVE=0`), which does not go through the glue. **To verify by ear on console**: quit during bass-heavy music, reconnect to a silent desktop, listen to the first second, 3 reconnections per binary — a thump before, nothing after. No counter moves either way.
  Refs: `media/audio.{c,h}` (`audio_eq_session_start`), `core/protocol/ctrl_session_glue.c` (EQV2), `core/protocol/eq.h` (`eq_reset`'s contract), `tests/test_eq.c` (`session_reset`).

- **2026-09-11 (audio stack, ING-A4: `audio=` mixed raw packets and accepted frames)** — **WITH SOUND, THE PANEL UNDERSTATED THE AUDIO FRAMES PLAYED BY ~24 %.** `[C95+]` on a Windows desktop. `udp_audio_pkts` (the stats line's `audio=`) was incremented BEFORE de-duplication on the single-packet path, but AFTER it on both split-frame paths. Invisible on a muted VM, which splits no frame; on FLAC with sound, measured against the old code (session 8, t=20 s): `audio=3510 dup=1993` = 3986 - 2×476 + 476 exactly, and the panel (`udp_audio_pkts - audio_dup_skipped`) read 1517 frames played instead of 1993. The three paths now go through one single gate (`aud_accept`, `audio_dedup_admit`), which counts BEFORE the decision, duplicates included, then refuses afterwards. **Live** (Windows desktop with no audio output, continuous sound): on FLAC, 10 sessions, `audio` = 2 × `dup` in every session (3989/1994…), against 3510/1993 before; on Opus, 3 sessions, 5987/2993. Counter-case in the test: the old accounting reads 750 for 1000 frames played. No toggle: an instrument. **An instrument changes meaning**: `audio=` counts EVERY frame that reaches the de-duplication window, duplicates included, on every path, and `audio= - dup=` is the number of frames played. The single-packet path — the only one a muted VM uses — already counted that way: old soundless logs stay comparable (§3.31). `udp_audio_bytes` is still counted after de-duplication.
  Refs: `core/protocol/audio_dedup.h` (`audio_dedup_admit`), `core/protocol/ctrl_session.c` (`aud_accept`), `core/protocol/ctrl_session.h`, `tests/test_audio_dedup.c`.

- **2026-09-11 (audio stack, ING-A1: the de-duplication window resynchronises)** — **ONE SINGLE NUMBER FAR AHEAD MADE THE REST OF THE STREAM SILENT.** `[C95+]` on a Windows desktop. `plus_haut` never went back: a number far ahead — a stale reassembly buffer (ING-A2), a stream renumbered by an AUD16 revival (AUD16b), some future defect — made every subsequent frame be refused as "too late", the §3.31 class. Eight consecutive packets beyond the window re-prime it (`AUDIO_DEDUP_RESYNC_N`). Bench: 0 different decisions over 658,018 calls, decoded PCM identical in 150 runs out of 150 (Opus and FLAC, 0.1 to 5 % loss), 0 double playback; stale buffer 174 [47..3246] -> 5996 frames out of 6000; renumbering or stray frame 0 % -> 99.8-99.9 % played; +0.15 ns per call. The two-frame alternative produced false resynchronisations on late arrivals. Only other change: when 8 or more packets arrive with more than 640 ms of disorder, the late ones are played once — never twice. New line `[ING-A1] fenetre anti-doublon resynchronisee seq=…` (8 per session), and a diagnostic, `SHADOW_DIAG_AUDIO_SEQ_JUMP_AT_S` (the VI1 model), which shows the window a number + 1,000,000 ONCE per session without delivering or emitting anything (`[DIAG] numero audio decale volontairement`). **Live A/B** (Windows desktop with no audio output, continuous sound, 3 sessions of 60 s per arm, jump injected at t=30 s): with `SHADOW_AUDIO_DEDUP_RESYNC=0`, no resynchronisation, 93 to 100 % of packets refused after the jump, `recues~2994` in the summary — **30 s of sound out of 60, then silence**; by default, one `[ING-A1]` line 0.03-0.04 s after the `[DIAG]`, 50 % refusals after the jump (the duplicates), `recues~6475` — **60 s of sound**. 3/3 against 3/3. Cost of a renumbering: 30 ms of sound (AUD-DEDUP-3). Refuted and not shipped: having AUD16 judge "channel alive" on ACCEPTED frames — it would re-register a live `:base+30`, the S57 emission that killed the channel in 43 sessions out of 69 (§3.38). Nothing is emitted on `:base+30`. `tests/test_audio_dedup.c` 14 -> 34 checks; its 4 resynchronisation checks fail on the old window.
  Refs: `core/protocol/audio_dedup.h` (ING-A1), `core/protocol/ctrl_session.c` (`aud_accept`, `g_diag_aud_jump_at_s`), `tests/test_audio_dedup.c`.

- **2026-09-11 (audio stack, ING-A2: split audio frames outlived the session)** — **A FLAC FRAME FRAGMENT LEFT BY THE PREVIOUS SESSION COULD MAKE THE NEXT ONE SILENT FOR AS LONG AS THE FIRST HAD LASTED.** `[C95+]` offline for the effect, and live for the trigger. The reassembly slots for split frames (AUD12) lived at file scope, and their reset had had no caller since AUD12. Deterministic replay on the thread's model, real FLAC frames: after a 10-minute session 1, session 2 plays 406 frames out of 30,000 and stays silent for ~296 s; after 1 minute, 25,871 out of 30,000. Monte-Carlo, 10,000 trials per cell: 0.60 / 3.30 / 12.25 % of FLAC -> FLAC reconnections silent at 0.5 / 1.66 / 5 % loss (sequential copies), 0.80 % at 1.66 % under bursty loss; with the reset, 0 out of 200,000. 45 to 64 % of the merged frames reached the speaker as wrong PCM (a click), not as a decode error, and AUD16 saw nothing: the packets keep arriving. The reset is called at the start of every session, right after `vid_reasm_reset_session()`, on the session thread and before the single flush of `:base+30`; it logs `[ING-A2] N trame(s) audio decoupee(s) de la session precedente ecartee(s) au demarrage`. **Live** (Windows desktop with no audio output, continuous sound, FLAC, 10 sessions within one process): the line appears at the start of sessions 3, 5 and 9 of W1's old arm (DEC-1 toggles off), and in session 8 of the new one — fragments really did survive from one session to the next, 3 transitions out of 9 with sound. The two copies of a split frame arrive one after the other on the wire (§3.26): the higher rates of the "sequential copies" model are the right ones. ING-A1 additionally bounds the silence if something else pushed the window. No toggle (precedent of `vid_reasm_reset_session`). `core/protocol/aud_reasm.h` (pure module, equal to the previous code over 5.0 M chunk steps), `tests/test_aud_reasm.c`: 41 checks; internal counter-case: without the reset, on the old window, 90 frames played out of 30,000.
  Refs: `core/protocol/aud_reasm.h`, `core/protocol/ctrl_session.c` (`ctrl_session_aud_reasm_reset`, ING-A2), `tests/test_aud_reasm.c`.

- **2026-09-11 (audio stack, AUD-LC-3: `:base+30`'s state lived in `static`s)** — **FROM A PROCESS'S 2ND SESSION ONWARDS, `:base+30`'s INSTRUMENTS WENT SILENT OR ACCUMULATED.** `[C95+]` on a Windows desktop. The defect family named at the top of CLAUDE.md, once more: the `[AUD2] :+30` census, the frame dumps, the `[AUD11]` and `[AUD15]` budgets, the 15 s sentinel, `g_aud_seen`, `n_ping` and `warned_cursor_silent` were process `static`s. In an autotest series or after an automatic reconnection, sessions 2 to N lost `premier paquet`, dumps #1-#3, pings #1-#3 and the sentinel, and inherited a cumulative census (old code: session 8 of a series started at `audio=8475`). AUD12 additionally spent `[AUD11]`'s 6-line budget within a few split packets, as early as session 1. All that state moves into a structure reset together with ING-A2's slots; `n_ping` and `warned_cursor_silent` become loop locals; `[AUD11]` gets its own per-session budget; `[AUD2] premier paquet` and the sentinel's two lines, which only went to stderr — so never to the log nor to the mirror on console — now also go to the log, text unchanged. **Live** (Windows desktop with no audio output, continuous sound, Opus, 3 sessions of 30 s per arm): each session has its `premier paquet`, its 3 `trame audio #` (#1 len=271), its 3 `[AUD15] ping`, its `canal :base+30 actif` and a clean first census (`audio=` 1986-1990, not a cumulative total); on FLAC, `[AUD2] trame audio #1` (first byte `02`) in EVERY session, against session 1 only before. No toggle: an instrument. **An instrument changes meaning**: in a multi-session process, the census, the dumps, the `#` numbers of `[AUD11]` and `[AUD15]` and the sentinel count PER SESSION; split packets no longer silence `[AUD11]`. The `[AUD15] ping #1..#3` criterion needs sessions of at least 30 s (pings at 7.5, 15 and 22.5 s).
  Refs: `core/protocol/ctrl_session.c` (AUD-LC-3, `g_aud30`, `ctrl_session_aud_reasm_reset`).

- **2026-09-11 (audio stack, DEC-1 + AUD-DEDUP-1: the stream descriptor reaching the decoder)** — **FLAC WENT SILENT AFTER AN OPUS SESSION IN THE SAME PROCESS, AND FROM THE 8TH FLAC SESSION ONWARDS; EVERY OPUS SESSION FROM THE 2ND ONWARDS COUNTED A FALSE ERROR.** `[C95+]` on a Windows desktop (reception and decoding; no audio output here). Every `:base+30` stream opens with a DESCRIPTOR of type `0x02`, sent twice with sequence number 0: 13 B on FLAC, 271 B on Opus (§3.26). The single-packet path passed it to the decoder, whose Opus offset probe (`audio_payload_offset`, S53) keeps its state in process `static`s. Once `chosen` was fixed at 5 — by an Opus session, or by eight descriptors from eight FLAC sessions, each counting as one more "consecutive" frame — the next session's descriptor classified it as Opus, and every FLAC frame was then refused with `cadrage invalide`: silence for the whole session, deterministically (bench, 7 repetitions: 0 frames out of 200; the panel said "Opus" and no warning was emitted). On Opus, the 271 B descriptor passed the probe and went to the decoder: one false error, the panel's red line. Fix: only `0x12` is audio — the descriptor is logged once per session then discarded at the entrance (`SHADOW_AUDIO_TYPE_FILTER`), by RETURNING, never towards `on_cursor` (S55) — and a FLAC frame promotes a session classified as Opus, in one direction only (`SHADOW_FLAC_LATE_DETECT`); FLAC sync also requires the `0x12` prefix, without which 3 of the 14 sessions of the legacy DC path (raw Opus RTP) were wrongly promoted and then silent. Either rule alone suffices on both routes. **Live, old code** (main d111715a, 2 series of 10 FLAC sessions within one process, with and without sound): flips at EXACTLY the 8th FLAC session both times — `en-tete de 5 octets avant l'Opus`, then nothing decoded; the trigger is the session count, not the arrival of split frames. **A/B, same binary** (Windows desktop with no audio output, continuous sound, FLAC, 10 sessions per arm; session 1 of the old arm discarded, server-side): with the toggles off, reproduced at the 8th session carrying a descriptor (sessions 9 and 10: `codec=Opus decodees=0 ecartees=2108 erreurs=1`), 7 valid sessions decoded out of 9; by default, **10/10** `codec=FLAC decodees=recues ecartees=0 erreurs=0`, one `[AUD2] descripteur` line per session, no `en-tete` line, no `[DEC1]` (the filter alone sufficed on this route). On Opus, 3 sessions per arm: `erreurs=1` in sessions 2 and 3 with the toggles off, `erreurs=0 ecartees=0` by default, with `descripteur de flux : 16 bits 48000 Hz 2 voies octet7=1`; 7 frames discarded in session 1 in both arms (the probe of a process's 1st session; the bench predicted 7 rather than 6). Not measured here: the Opus-then-FLAC route within one process (it goes through the Quality screen: to be replayed by hand), and, on console with `SHADOW_HWOPUS=1`, the descriptor that reached `hwopusDecodeInterleaved` from the 2nd session onwards and may have triggered the permanent software fallback, skewing the A5 comparison (unverified). New lines: `audio: bilan de session : codec=… recues=… decodees=… ecartees=… erreurs=… poussees=… deborde=…` at every decoder's destruction (`poussees`/`deborde` at `n/a` with no audio output), and `[DEC1] session reclassee Opus -> FLAC apres N paquet(s)` on every promotion. `tests/test_audio_route.c`: 25 checks, of which 8 fail under the old rules. **Instruments that change meaning**: the census's `audio=` and the stats' `audio=` lose the descriptor's two copies per session (`dup=` loses one); the first `[AUD8]` check reports `seq=1`.
  Refs: `core/protocol/audio_route.h`, `core/protocol/ctrl_session.c` (DEC-1, `[AUD2] descripteur`), `core/media/audio.c` (DEC-1, S53, `audio_log_session_summary`), `tests/test_audio_route.c`.

- **2026-09-11 (audio stack, AUD-CFG-2: the pause menu's Audio page hid `env.txt`)** — **WITH `SHADOW_VOLUME=0`, THE SOUND WAS MUTED WHILE THE LINE SHOWED 100 % AND EVERY PRESS SEEMED TO WORK.** `[C95+]` for the code (compiled with `build.ninja`'s flags); `[C80]` for the rendering, not yet seen on screen (devlink does not open menus). The pause menu's audio lines (hardware Opus, audio quality, volume) and the relative-mouse line (`SHADOW_INPUT_ABS`) passed raw descriptions, whereas the same menu's video lines and the Settings screen's three audio lines used `ui::envNote`; no counter showed it, the gain zeroes the samples after decoding. All four lines now carry the note; volume always shows the SAVED value, like the Settings screen. The hardware Opus line (`SHADOW_HWOPUS`, only read under `HAVE_AUDOUT`) was inert off-console: it now only exists under `__SWITCH__`, in both screens. `SHADOW_VOLUME` now has a single reader (`audio_volume_forced`, read once per process, clamped to 0..300, negative = 0): a negative value, silent on open, restored the saved volume on the first press; it now stays at 0. Along the way (EQV3, text only, fr and en-US, no key changed): the handheld profile also applies to headphones plugged in while handheld. No toggle (`env.txt`'s precedence does not change). **To verify in the UI** (a human or the held-key bench): `SHADOW_VOLUME=0` -> note on Volume, saved value; `SHADOW_AUDIO_CODEC=1` with `audio_hifi=1` saved -> note on quality and `[K14] codec audio demande : Opus (SHADOW_AUDIO_CODEC=1)`; with no variable, no note; hardware line absent on desktop.
  Refs: `clients/borealis/activity/stream_activity.cpp`, `clients/borealis/activity/settings_view.cpp` (`SET_HW_AUDIO`), `core/media/audio.c` (`audio_volume_forced`), `resources/i18n/{fr,en-US}/shadow.json` (EQV3).

- **2026-09-11 (audio stack, AUDC-1 + OUT-2: UI sounds and the stream fought over the console's audio output)** — **ON CONSOLE, A RECONNECTED SESSION WAS SILENT, AND THE "CONNECTED" CHIME HAD NEVER BEEN PLAYED SINCE S88.** `[C95+]` offline, against the real `clients/borealis/ui/sfx.cpp` compiled with `-D__SWITCH__` against a mock of libnx's single `IAudioOut`; `[C80]` for HOS itself: whether it REFUSES or ACCEPTS a second Start is still not established, and the fix holds under both models. Console defaults (`auto_reconnect=1`, `sons=1`): every failed attempt plays "Failed", then `sfx` holds audout until +2.0 s, while `audio_decoder_create` comes through around +1.0-1.3 s. Bench: 0 % heard for a reconnection within 0.9 s; REFUSE -> no decoder, ACCEPT -> `sfx`'s idle stop at +2.0 s freezes the stream (33 -> 3 % heard). And `release()` cut the voices: the chime, which lasts 718 ms and not 420 (`docs/UI_SOUNDS.md` corrected), was killed before playing — 0 frames out of 34,464. Fix: a claim (`ui::sfx::StreamClaim`) around `ctrl_session_glue_run` and `dc_session_run` — `sfx` closes its output, acknowledges it (even with no device, the Windows case) and stays silent until the end of the stream, on every exit path; `release()` no longer cuts voices (`SHADOW_SFX_HANDOFF`). Plugged along the way, with no toggle: the leak of an hwopus session on every failed creation after `audoutInitialize` / `audoutStartAudioOut` (1 -> 0). `tests/test_sfx_handoff.cpp`, run twice: 34 checks with the fix (chime 34,080 frames out of 34,464, bit-exact; reconnection lossless and heard 100 % of the time; 0 calls from `sfx` to audout under the wake lock; 25 claims with no race, p50/worst 16.0/25.6 ms under MinGW and 10.1/20.3 ms under Linux), then 19 with `SHADOW_SFX_HANDOFF=0`, which ASSERT the defect (chime 0/34,464, stream never opened under REFUSE, 57 % heard under ACCEPT, 25 claims out of 25 returned with the output still started). **Live** (Windows desktop, plumbing only: `sfx` has no device there, nothing is audible): `[AUDC1] sons d'interface suspendus pendant le flux (sortie rendue en 30 / 15 / 31 ms)` in every session (1 or 2 ticks of the Windows 15.6 ms clock), decoder created 84-222 ms later, no `sortie non rendue` line; with `SHADOW_SFX_HANDOFF=0`, a single `… desactive` line at start-up. Noted, not fixed: every UI sound loses its last partial 10 ms block (34,080 frames out of 34,464), against the "nothing is cut at the end" rule in `UI_SOUNDS.md`. **Console, to do**: a `SHADOW_SFX_HANDOFF=0` arm in `env.txt`, at least 3 reconnections — `audio: audoutStartAudioOut FAIL` followed by `[glue] audio_decoder_create FAIL` will say HOS refuses, `audio: dropping packet — no free buffer` from ~1 s that it accepts: the `[C80]` question is settled in that log; new arm: sound on every reconnection, and the chime by ear.
  Refs: `ui/sfx.{cpp,hpp}` (AUDC-1, `StreamClaim`), `clients/borealis/activity/connecting_activity.cpp`, `core/media/audio.c` (hwopus leak), `tests/test_sfx_handoff.cpp`, `tests/mock/`, `docs/UI_SOUNDS.md`.

- **2026-09-11 (audio stack: the campaign, what it measures and what it cannot measure)** — `[C95+]` for the methodological facts. Audio-stack analysis workflow: 18 items in the plan, plus 7 "to check next" below the cut; applied the same day in five waves — W3 (output and UI), W1 (input and decoding), W4 (equaliser state), W2 (instruments), W1b (four of the seven items below the cut) — each one compiled, run offline and measured wherever the machine allows (entries above). Deferred with their trigger: OUT-3 (Windows output), OUT-1 (underruns on audout and ALSA), AUD-DEDUP-4 (loss masking, after an `aud_perdues` measurement on console), EQV3 (equaliser profile by actual output; only its text ships), AUD-CFG-1 (limiter); and, below the cut, AUD-INS-4, AUD-CFG-4 and OUT-5. **[Corrected the same day: OUT-3 and OUT-5 were done (W5), OUT-1 and AUD-INS-4 too (W6, wired up on the 11th-12th) — entries above. Still deferred: AUD-DEDUP-4, EQV3 (apart from its text), AUD-CFG-1 and AUD-CFG-4.]** **What the machine measures**: a Windows desktop, GUI client in autotest, WITHOUT audio output (`HAVE_ALSA=0`, `HAVE_AUDOUT=0`) — reception, de-duplication and decoding only. **[Up to W5: since OUT-3, this desktop has a WASAPI output, whose figures hold only for it.]** **What it does not measure**: the console's audout output, Linux's ALSA output, the ear; everything that is heard (EQV1's clicks, EQV2's thump, AUDC-1's chime and reconnections) still has to be listened to on console. **[Since W5, EQV1, EQV2 and OUT-5 can also be listened to on this desktop, with a non-FLAT preset — not done yet; AUDC-1's chime cannot: `sfx` still has no device there.]** Offline after the five waves: Linux (Debian under WSL) 138,687 checks, Windows (MinGW without `-Werror`) 3,090, 0 failures; W1b adds no suite (its bench is out of repo). **[After W5, which adds no suite, and W6a (`test_audio_gap`, 158 checks): 138,845 and 3,248.]** Three methodological lessons:
  1. **A muted VM hides part of the audio defects.** Split FLAC frames only appear with real high-bitrate sound (baseline, session 8: 952 chunks, 476 frames in 20 s); without them, ING-A4's counter drift and ING-A2's stale fragment are invisible. **Wallpaper Engine on the VM provides continuous sound**: every live measurement in this campaign had it, except sessions 1 to 5 of the first FLAC baseline.
  2. **The first session of a series launched ~5 s after closing the previous client twice found `:base+11` closed for 12 to 21 s** (`connect TIMEOUT` on v6 then on v4, `M8.retry` 3 then 4 times), server-side: only 8 s of stream for one (W3), the other discarded as `ok=0` (W1). Wait 30 s between two series.
  3. **A session dead in its entirety** (`video=0 curseur=0 audio=0`; 1 in 10 in the first baseline) is a session the server did not serve: discard it before reading the audio.
- **2026-09-11 (network, DNS1: one single resolution of the VM's name per session)** — **EVERY CHANNEL RESOLVED THE SAME NAME, AND A SLOW RESOLUTION WAS PAID FOR IN LOST FRAMES.** `[C95+]` on a Windows desktop; `[C80]` on console (same code, and that is where the fix should pay off: the HOS resolver is known for its slowness, S108). The control channel, then every UDP registration (video, audio, gamepad), the `vst` cursor channel and the input channel each redid their `getaddrinfo` on the same name. Once (CONC-1), opening `vst` waited 1.21 s: the video socket, registered before it, accumulated 1.43 s of frames, delivered in one burst.
  From now on the numeric address the control channel ACTUALLY connected to is remembered, and any later resolution of the same name within the session answers from it (`AI_NUMERICHOST`: no resolver); if it does not suit the caller (different family), normal resolution resumes. Reset every session; the bootstrap order does not change, only where each channel's address comes from. `tests/test_session_host.c`: 12 checks, in both settings.
  **Live A/B**, with a simulated slow resolver (`SHADOW_DIAG_DNS_DELAY_MS=1200`), 2 pairs: without the fix, video registration -> live session 5.0 s, `vst` waits 1.2 s before its first attempt, 222 and 227 frames dropped at start-up (at depth 16); with it, 0.19-0.20 s, 0 s, 0 frames dropped — 5 resolutions avoided out of 6. With no delay: 0.18 against 0.19 s, 0 dropped, every channel established (control, `vst`, DTLS input, audio, 8 grants out of 8), 0 loss. `SHADOW_RESOLVE_ONCE=0` restores one resolution per channel.
  Refs: `streaming/session_host.{c,h}`, `core/protocol/tls_chan.c` (DNS1), `tests/test_session_host.c`.

- **2026-09-11 (security, SEC2: secrets in the log)** — **THE SESSION'S ENCRYPTION KEY WENT INTO THE LOG IN CLEARTEXT, HENCE ONTO THE NETWORK.** `[C95+]`. The log is copied out by the `logsink.txt` mirror and leaves the SD card. The session's chacha20 key, the authentication hash and the nonce were written there in full at INFO level (F36, for offline key-derivation trials), as were the SPICE secret and, in the headless smoke test, the streaming token — which the `token=` redaction missed because of the spaces around the `=`. Anyone holding a log could decrypt that session's captured traffic: video, audio, gamepad.
  The rule now (`core/services/log_mask.h`): the start and the end only, at most 2 bytes or 4 characters on each side, never more than a quarter of the secret — recognisable (two logs of one session can be matched up), unusable. `tests/test_log_mask.c`: 20 checks, including counter-cases that fail for any mask that talks too much, at every length from 1 to 64. Second line of defence in `journal.c` (`redact()`) for `chacha20_key=` and `auth_hash=`. **Live**: `DEBUG_KEY: chacha20_key=7c53...1657 (32 o)`, `auth_hash=06e1...7b33 (20 o)`, `streaming_token=vmrQ...9_zt (34 car.)`; no key and no hash in cleartext in the verification's six logs. Consequence: the FEC-era tools that read `DEBUG_KEY` back (`tools/fec_bruteforce*.py`, `find_parity_key.py`, dead since F31) no longer find a key in a fresh log.
  Refs: `core/services/log_mask.h`, `tests/test_log_mask.c`, `core/protocol/ctrl_session.c` (SEC2), `core/protocol/shadowusb.c`, `core/protocol/smoke_test.c`, `core/services/journal.c` (`mask_hex_after`).

- **2026-09-11 (video stack, VI2 + VI5: the push copy and the panel's "latency" line)** — **`video/file-aff` COUNTED THE PUSH COPY, AND THE PANEL'S "LATENCY" LINE WAS THE AGE OF THE PICTURE ON SCREEN.** `[C95+]` for the code facts and the bench; live, no measurable effect. **VI2**: the push timestamp was taken BEFORE the plane copy, which was itself done inside the decode measurement: the copy counted in `decode` AND in `file-aff`, which therefore no longer measured "pushed -> popped". It now follows the copy, on the same monotonic clock as the whole report: `file-aff` no longer contains it, `decode` keeps it. Each frame additionally allocated 1.38 MB and freed the previous one UNDER `swap_mtx`; a pool of two recycled buffers (`SHADOW_PUSH_RECYCLE`) removes both — bench: copy p50 362 -> 115 µs, time under lock p99 281 -> 2.9 µs, 0 allocations and 0 frees under the lock. **Live A/B**, 3 interleaved 90 s pairs: nothing measurable at the resolution of the `[L5]` windows (`decode` mean 7.8 / 7.8 ms, `file-aff` p50 7.2 / 7.05 ms, `bout` unchanged) and no regression — the gain is the bench's, below the window-to-window spread, as expected.
  **VI5**: the panel's line resampled the age of the displayed picture on every draw (analysis: ~11 ms shown for 3.6 ms real) and carried the same label as the per-stage section just below it. It becomes "Display wait", fed sample for sample like `file-aff`, graded against the inter-frame interval. `[C80]` for its rendering: the panel does not open remotely (devlink presses nothing). **An instrument changes meaning**: `file-aff` values from before VI2 include the copy (~0.3 ms under Windows per the bench).
  Refs: `activity/stream_view.{cpp,hpp}` (VI2, VI5), `core/protocol/latency.h`.

- **2026-09-11 (video stack, ING-2: the network round trip measured by the loop)** — **THE PUBLISHED NETWORK LATENCY CARRIED UP TO 7 MS OF BIAS MANUFACTURED BY THE LOOP, AND ITS JITTER WAS MOSTLY LOCAL.** `[C95+]` on a Windows desktop; `[C80]` for the console (~2 ms expected, to be measured after a devkitPro build: `bsdPoll` must accept the TCP socket in the same array, and a runaway there would starve the other threads). The control channel's socket was not in the receive loop's wait set: a reply was only seen on the next datagram or at the wait's expiry (WSAPoll(5) waits 15.6 ms under Windows), then timestamped against the clock taken at the start of the pass, in ms — the send too. Fix: the socket joins the wait set, both timestamps move to monotonic µs. Runaway guard (the proposed version ran at ~2×10^5 passes/s when nothing reads the channel, the D1 mode): never added when `SHADOW_AUTO_FLUSH_ACK=0`, removed permanently on the first read or `poll` failure.
  **Live A/B**, 3 interleaved 120 s pairs, `SHADOW_CTRL_POLL=0` against the default: mean round trip 25.2 -> 18.2 ms, p90 30.9 -> 18.6 ms (disjoint ranges: 30.4-31.1 against 18.6-18.8), jitter 3.9 -> 0.4 ms. Guard: 268 against 270 waits/s and ~5,000 passes per 10 s on both sides, 0 against ~259 wake-ups from the channel; with `SHADOW_AUTO_FLUSH_ACK=0`, the socket stays out of the wait set (270 waits/s, 0 wake-ups). **The S118/S119 figures published earlier carry this bias** — ~+7 ms under Windows (measured here), ~+2 ms where the 5 ms wait is honoured — and their "1 ms jitter" was mostly the loop's. Noted along the way, not fixed: the S118 block's two log budgets are function `static`s (after a process's first session, `[S118]` only logs one reply in forty).
  Refs: `core/protocol/ctrl_session.c` (ING-2, `[ING2]` lines), `streaming/ctrl_tcp.{c,h}` (`ctrl_tcp_poll_fd`, `ctrl_tcp_buffered`).

- **2026-09-11 (video stack, ING-1 part 2: one loop pass, timed)** — **AN INSTRUMENT FOR ATTRIBUTING THE CONSOLE'S ~300 MS FREEZES.** `[C95+]` for the instrument, verified on desktop; its purpose is the console (S41d). New `[L5] reception/tour` stage: the duration of the receive loop's body, from the start of the pass to just before the wait — at the bench, a clock taken at the END of the body detected 100 % of absences in the body with no false positives; taken before the wait, it missed 10 to 100 % of them. Two keys at the end of the stats line: `lot=` (the largest batch of datagrams returned by one flush of the video socket) and `ecart_max=` (the worst gap between the end of one pass and the start of the next: the scheduling delay, which the pass itself cannot see).
  On a wired Windows desktop: ~500 passes/s, p99 0.2-0.4 ms, worst 4-6 ms; `ecart_max` 20-21 ms in EVERY window — the legitimate floor here, not the assumed "<=16 ms". **During a 20 s injected video outage** (`SHADOW_DIAG_VIDEO_MUTE_*`), nothing moves (pass p99 0.2-0.4, `ecart_max` 20-21): the instrument correctly attributes the outage to upstream of the socket. Reading on console: a spike in `reception/tour` or in `ecart_max` during a freeze points at the loop or the scheduler; flat values next to a D4 point at the network.
  Refs: `streaming/latency.{c,h}` (`LAT_RX_PASS`), `core/protocol/ctrl_session.c` (ING-1).

- **2026-09-11 (video stack, CFG-3: the NAL grammar frozen on the first session)** — **AFTER AN H.265 SESSION, AN H.264 SESSION IN THE SAME PROCESS READ ITS STREAM WITH THE HEVC RULES.** `[C95+]` offline (the real `vid_reasm.c`, the real dump of 1,928 frames); live, the half that does not go through the Quality screen. `g_hevc_nal` was a `static` resolved at the process's FIRST emission and never reset, whereas the decoder and the announcement re-read `SHADOW_CODEC` every session. On the dump: 0 frame starts, 0 IDRs, 0 SPS against 1928/8/4 — G40, F11 and G43 silent, a false K16d alarm ("the decoder will never start"), L10 disarmed, one frame of lag per frame. And the -1 sentinel, true in C, made the process's first incomplete frame be judged with the HEVC rules: a TRUNCATED IDR went to the decoder.
  The grammar now comes from the high nibble of byte 0 of each VideoFrame (RE-3), as in the official client; `SHADOW_NAL_FROM_STREAM=0` takes it back from `SHADOW_CODEC`, re-read every session. Bench: single-codec output identical byte for byte, a switched session identical to a fresh one; `test_vid_wire` 56 -> 63 `[CFG3]` checks, including a counter-case that fails under three wrong helpers. **Live**: a real H.265 session (`codec=1` granted, byte `0x12`) gives non-zero counters under the HEVC grammar (`top=2870 idr_t=2 sps=2`); six H.264 sessions: no `[K16]`, K16d or L10 line, top = bot, 0 loss. Switching within one process needs the Quality screen: not replayed here. New lines `[K16] codec du flux %u != SHADOW_CODEC %d` and `[K12] codec accorde %u != demande %u`.
  Refs: `core/protocol/vid_reasm.c` (CFG-3), `streaming/vid_wire.{c,h}` (`vid_wire_vf_is_hevc`), `tests/test_vid_wire.c`.

- **2026-09-11 (tooling: devlink)** — **DEVLINK HAD NOT RECOGNISED A SINGLE REPLY SINCE S81.** `[C95+]`. Since 2026-08-29 the log writes `[12.345] I/systeme  [devlink] ...`; `devlink.py` only stripped the timestamp, so every command ended in "timed out" while the app had answered (observed: a reply to `state` in 0.35 s in its own log). Fixed (`RE_HORODATAGE`), counter-case in `--autotest`. Traps recorded in `tools/client/DEVLINK.md`: `attends` at the head of a plan runs BEFORE the port is opened; `--attente` / `--delai` go before the subcommand. Still open: on a Windows desktop the connection is lost after a few seconds of local pause (`attends`, during which devlink does not read the socket) — this is not a full buffer on the app side (`WSAEWOULDBLOCK` is handled there, verified at the preprocessor). Consequence: VI1's banner rendering could not be captured.
  Refs: `tools/client/devlink.py`, `tools/client/DEVLINK.md`.

- **2026-09-11 (video stack, incidental findings)** — `[C95+]` for the facts. (1) **FIXED the same day (SEC2, above)**: `ctrl_session.c` wrote the session's chacha20 key, the authentication hash and the nonce unconditionally at INFO level (`DEBUG_KEY` / `DEBUG_HASH` / `DEBUG_NONCE`, F36) — hence into the log's network mirror too; an ephemeral key, but whoever held the log could decrypt that session's capture. (2) **FIXED the same day (DNS1, above)**: every channel redid its `getaddrinfo` on the same host: once, opening `vst` waited 1.21 s, and the video socket registered before it accumulated 1.43 s of frames (~65), delivered in one burst (57 losses at depth 8, see CONC-1). (3) The already-noted race between `vid_reasm_reset_session` and the TCP video thread, started before it. (4) The S118 log budgets in `static`s (see ING-2). (5) REC-2 deferred: its only trigger on desktop, the G38 losses, disappeared with depth 16; to be measured on console.

- **2026-09-11 (video stack, REASM-1: the single-chunk frame)** — **G43 ONLY FLUSHED THE PREVIOUS FRAME IF THE NEXT ONE HAD SEVERAL CHUNKS.** `[C95+]` offline, against the real `vid_reasm.c`, compiled under Windows (MinGW) and Linux (gcc 14.2 under WSL, `-Werror`). No live measurement possible on this desktop: its animated content never produces a single-chunk frame and wired loss is near zero — `[C60]` for real-world occurrence, to be read on a Wi-Fi console through the two new `[G43]` lines (5 per session at most).
  G43(A) says "when opening N, flush N-1", but the single-chunk path RETURNED BEFORE that trigger. Reproduced: emission order `38 39 41 40 42` with 3 `[G42] GAP` lines; or frame 40 never emitted and counted NOWHERE (`perdus` understated); or the orphan buffer resurrected ~256 frames (6 s) later, an ancient P frame sent to the decoder. Fix: the same trigger on both paths; an age guard (`SHADOW_FLUSH_PREV_MAX_AGE`, 8 openings), without which the one-line fix itself opened a resurrection path; and accounting through counters alone — the `vid_account_loss` call first proposed would have requested an IDR stale by 5.7 s and a NACK contrary to V10.
  `tests/test_vid_reasm.c` (new, links the real module, decryption replaced by identity): 54 checks, of which 27 fail on the previous code; under `SHADOW_FLUSH_PREV_INCOMPLETE=0` it expects the old behaviour (38 checks), so each pass replays the defect. A clean stream is identical byte for byte in every configuration (600 frames, same hash, `g_idr_needed` at 0, no GAP line). Deliberate trade-off: a frame whose tail arrives after a single-chunk successor goes out truncated with an IDR request, as on the multi-chunk path. Still open (pre-existing): a stale buffer can be completed by a later frame whose chunk 0 is lost and whose chunk count happens to match.
  Refs: `core/protocol/vid_reasm.c` (REASM-1), `tests/test_vid_reasm.c`.

- **2026-09-11 (video stack, HO-1: uploading a frame only once)** — **THE RENDERER RE-UPLOADED BOTH PLANES OF THE PICTURE ON EVERY DRAW, EVEN WITH NO NEW PICTURE.** `[C95+]` on desktop. The view draws at the screen's rate, and `render()` re-uploaded Y and UV (1.38 MB) on every call: on the 144 Hz baseline, `[L5] video/televerse` n=1440 per 10 s against `file-aff` n~450, i.e. 65-74 % of uploads of identical bytes (~137 MB/s). The textures now hold the picture. The key is the frame NUMBER supplied by the view (`nv12_seq_`): a per-draw "new picture" flag left a stale picture 40 times out of 40 when the pop landed on a skipped draw, and the buffer address gets reused. VI3 along the way: the lazy initialisation (shader compilation, 15-40 ms) fell inside the timed region and set `televerse`'s session worst to 23-25 ms.
  **Live A/B**, 3 interleaved pairs, `SHADOW_UPLOAD_EVERY_DRAW=1` against the default. 60 Hz screen that day (Windows session locked); the new `gl_video: renderer=` line reveals that GL runs on the Intel UHD 630 and CUDA decoding on the NVIDIA. `televerse` mean 0.5 -> 0.3 ms, p50 0.5 -> 0.2; and, not predicted by the benches, `cadence` p90 24.6 -> 17.4 ms, `bout` p90 20.5 -> 18.4 ms, `file-aff` p90 15.9 -> 14.8 ms; `decode` unchanged (7.3 / 7.4 ms). At 60 Hz only ~37 % of draws have no new picture; at 144 Hz the expected gain is larger (benches: UI-thread time in `render()` ×0.43-0.49).
  **An instrument changes meaning**: `video/televerse` now measures the per-draw render call, which only uploads if a picture has arrived. Its earlier values are not comparable with the new ones.
  Refs: `activity/gl_video_renderer.{cpp,hpp}` (HO-1, `kSeqUnknown`), `clients/borealis/activity/stream_view.cpp` (`nv12_seq_`, VI3), `core/protocol/latency.h` (`LAT_VID_UPLOAD`).

- **2026-09-11 (video stack, CFG-1 + CFG-4: the bitrate chosen in game)** — **G19 OVERWROTE THE BITRATE THE USER CHOSE, AND THE "LIVE FRAME RATE" DID NOT EXIST.** `[C95+]` (real session on desktop; same code on console).
  **CFG-1**: adaptive bitrate G19 and the UI wrote the SAME value (`kUpdateSession` f13) through the same setter, and G19 only knew the ceiling frozen at the start of the session (`p->max_bitrate_mbps`, which ignores `SHADOW_BITRATE_MBPS`). Lowering the bitrate in game — the only workaround for DEBIT-1 (100 breaks, 25 holds) — was therefore undone within seconds while the screen showed the chosen value (offline replay: 100 -> 25 at 60 s, back to 70 at 62 s). New rule: G19's ceiling is whatever the announcement carried, and the user's last choice BECOMES the ceiling, adopted without an emission of its own. **Live measurement**: (E) `SHADOW_BITRATE_MBPS=10` — announcement and f13 at 10; G19's first emission at 20 under the old rule, at 10 under the new one. (A) ceiling 100, G19 climbs from 25 to 55 in steps of 3; a choice of 10 at 54 s through the menu's entry point (`SHADOW_DIAG_USER_BITRATE`) -> `[G19] choix de l'usager adopte : plafond 10 Mbps`, then NO G19 emission at all for the remaining 32 s. `tests/test_bitrate_ctl.c`: 34 checks, 19 of which fail under the old rule. `SHADOW_ADAPT_USER_CAP=0` restores the old rule identically — including not telling G19 about the thread, without which it would send its own value back over the user's.
  **CFG-4**: since S18 the live message carries NO frame-rate field, yet the API took one, ignored it, and the log displayed it as sent (`fps=142.0` when the SPS said 143.85). The parameter disappears from the API, so that the compiler finds every caller that still believes in it; `[Q1+C]` and `[UI] video demandee` no longer carry a frame rate (verified live); the screens say that the frame rate applies at the next connection, like the resolution.
  Refs: `core/protocol/bitrate_ctl.h`, `tests/test_bitrate_ctl.c`, `ctrl_session.c` (`send_bitrate`, G19), `ctrl_msgs.{c,h}`, `main.cpp` (`SHADOW_DIAG_USER_BITRATE`).

- **2026-09-11 (video stack, CONC-1: decode queue depth)** — **L1 REVISITED: THE QUEUE ONLY FILLS ON AN ARRIVAL BURST. DEFAULT 3 -> 16.** `[C95+]` on desktop; `[C60]` for the console, to be confirmed (Wi-Fi bursts, nvtegra). L1 (2026-08-22) had brought the queue from 64 down to 3: since the hardware decoder never falls behind, a deep queue would only have accumulated delay. The premise holds (`[L5] video/file-dec` p50 0.10 ms), the conclusion does not: a queue the decoder empties immediately adds NO delay, and "3" only held 2 (the ring is full when `next == head`).
  **What fills it is the ARRIVAL.** Wave 1's `[G38]` lines show two families: (1) at every session start, the video socket accumulates ~0.17 s of frames while the side channels open, then delivers them in one burst (`ecart_entree=0`, `depuis_depile` 0-6 ms) — SPS, PPS, IDR, then ~8 P frames back to back; (2) mid-session, a decoder stall of 50-70 ms (`depuis_depile=51-73 ms`). Every loss breaks the reference chain until an IDR is requested, and eviction takes the OLDEST frame, which may be the key frame itself.
  **Live A/B**, 5 sessions of 130 s per depth (`autotest.txt`, one process per arm, `SHADOW_DEC_QUEUE` through the environment, confirmed by the `file=N` of the `[L1]` line; a loss counts as "start-up" if its `[G38]` line falls within the 5 s after `[glue] fil de decodage demarre (G25)`): depth 3 -> 24 losses (16 at start-up across 3 sessions, 8 mid-session across 2, one KEY frame dropped); 8 -> 59, all at start-up (including 57 from an abnormal backlog, below); 16 -> 0. L1's guard has not moved: `file-dec` p50 0.10 ms throughout, p90 2.8 / 2.7 / 2.4 ms, `video/bout` +p99 30.7 ms throughout. `SHADOW_DEC_QUEUE=3` restores L1.
  **Incidental finding, not fixed**: in one session, `vst: opening` -> `vst: try v6` took 1.21 s — resolving the `:base+20` channel's hostname, the SAME one as the control channel's, resolved 0.6 s earlier. The video socket, registered before it, accumulated 1.43 s of frames, i.e. ~65: no reasonable queue absorbs those. Reusing the already-resolved address, or opening the side channels before registering the video, would remove the burst at source; the bootstrap order being byte-exact, measure before touching it.
  Refs: `core/protocol/ctrl_session_glue.c` (CONC-1, `g_decq_cap`).

- **2026-09-11 (video stack, wave 1: the instruments)** — **THE VIDEO STACK'S COUNTERS WERE WRONG, OR WENT SILENT AFTER THE FIRST SESSION.** `[C95+]` unless stated otherwise. First wave of the video-stack analysis workflow (47 findings, 17 retained after an adversarial pass): the instruments first, since the fixes that follow are judged with them. Measurements on a Windows desktop, real session, paths common to every platform.
  **HO-2, display freezes**: the `[G44]` detector lived in a function `static`. Its 8-line budget was spent in the first seconds of the process's FIRST session; later ones were silent — the defect family named at the top of CLAUDE.md. The state moves into the session: every gap >= 45 ms is COUNTED in a `[G44] bilan de session` histogram (45-99 / 100-299 / 300-999 / >=1 s), and a `micro-gel` line is only written from 100 ms up (`SHADOW_DIAG_FREEZE_LOG_MS`). Three sessions in one process: three distinct summaries (worst 70 / 128 / 216 ms); an injected 8 s outage gives one `micro-gel` line of 8012 ms.
  **CONC-4, decode queue**: the `[G38]` line asserted a cause ("decode/render contention, not network loss") that it could not know, and that is wrong for the start-up burst. It now reports facts (`cle=`, `age=`, `depuis_depile=`, `ecart_entree=`), its budget is per session, and the copy, the free and the log write move out of the lock the decode thread is waiting on. The total goes into `[glue] stats ... jetees=` and into the `[AUTOTEST]` line. The panel's "frames dropped" line read `h264_async_dropped`, which nothing wrote: it now shows that total.
  **VI1, frozen picture**: `SHADOW_DIAG_VIDEO_MUTE_AT_S/_MS` discards video datagrams for N ms — the only way to provoke a real outage without a network failure. D4 fires at +309 ms, `video RETABLIE` after 8013 ms. The banner is drawn after the video and before the dev bar, only when a picture exists and after 5 s of freeze. `[C80]` for its rendering: not yet seen on screen.
  **ING-1, kernel losses**: on Linux, the `SO_RXQ_OVFL` counter was kept as `max(v)` in a process `static` that the session reset did not touch — with three sockets it reported their MAXIMUM rather than their sum, and after a reconnection it hid losses until they exceeded the previous count. The workflow's ING-1 bench against a real kernel: -17 in session 1, +31 in session 2; delta accounting, one value per socket, is exact in every case (`ovfl_accum.h`, `tests/test_ovfl.c`). `[C80]` in a real session: Linux only, not replayed here. Windows measures nothing of the sort: the log used to say `noyau=0`, it now says `noyau=n/a`, and the network test hides the line.
  **VI4**: an `[L5]` stage with no sample in the window displayed zeros, read as "fast". It now displays "no sample in the last window" and keeps its session worst.
  Refs: `core/protocol/freeze_stat.h` + `tests/test_freeze_stat.c`, `core/protocol/ovfl_accum.h` + `tests/test_ovfl.c`, `ctrl_session_glue.c` (G44, G38), `ctrl_session.c` (VI1, ING-1), `streaming/latency.{c,h}` + `tests/test_latency.c` (VI4), `clients/borealis/activity/stream_view.cpp`.
- **2026-09-10 (COL1)** — **THE STREAM'S COLOUR IS BT.709 LIMITED RANGE — AND THE 22 AUGUST COLOUR FIX HAD NEVER BEEN ACTIVE.** `[C95+]` The shader has received `u_range`, `u_kr` and `u_kb` since e72d02d2, but no code ever called `glUniform` on them and `SHADOW_COLOR_RANGE` / `SHADOW_COLOR_MATRIX` were read nowhere: every picture since was converted with u_range=0 and kr=kb=0, which makes green a copy of luma (darkened greens, reds pulled towards orange, correct greys — hence a defect mistaken for a tint).
  **Measured on the live stream** (captures of the client's window, 3×3 averages, two independent scenes): the Facebook button #1877F2 = (24,119,242) → before (1,107,254), full-range BT.709 (35,118,228), limited-range BT.709 (24,119,244, computed by inverting the full-range capture exactly). Known white and black (the Google and Steam buttons, the Epic badge): in full range 235 and 14-17 — the signature of a LIMITED stream displayed as full; in limited range 255 and 0. NVIDIA green #76B900 = (118,185,0) → before (108,158,0), limited BT.709 (116,186,2). BT.601 would put the Facebook button's red at 33 instead of 24.
  The stream declares nothing (`colorspace=2 color_range=0`: VUI absent, confirmed). **The "range" half of the 22 August diagnosis is SUPERSEDED**: a luma that touches 0 and 255 in places (probably overshoots at sharp edges) does not make a full-range stream; the bulk of the picture does. Its "matrix" half (BT.709) holds. Defaults now: limited range, BT.709, with both variables winning over whatever the stream would declare. Refs: `clients/borealis/activity/gl_video_renderer.cpp` (COL1), `core/media/h264_decoder.c` (`h264_decoder_colorimetry`, the log's `[couleur]` lines).

- **2026-09-10 (shutdown audit, AF1-AF13)** — **SHUTDOWN IS CLEAN, AND EVERY FIX HAS ITS MEASUREMENT.** `[C95+]` unless stated otherwise. Measurements on a Windows desktop, on paths common to every platform:
  **AF9, the log mirror** (`logsink.txt`): a receiver that stops READING without closing froze the whole app (blocking `send` under the log lock). A/B: without the fix, the window froze from 6.6 s, the log stuck for 88 s, the session never established, shutdown impossible (forced kill); with it, no freeze, 11.5 MB written, shutdown in 3.3 s. The first two measurements were SILENT: an INFO log (60 KB) does not fill a Windows TCP buffer — it took making the client emit screenshots (`shot`).
  **AF6, "Replay"**: the fade-out pop loop took the same activity twice and lost input-blocking tokens. A/B: without it, three presses of Y with no effect and "VM list becomes visible again" logged TWICE at the same millisecond; with it, the settings open.
  **AF2, lock on wake** (Switch; `SHADOW_DIAG_RELOCK_S` replays it on desktop): stack operations held back were replayed while the lock screen was still displayed. AND — the measurement revealed it, the audit had not seen it — two replayed pops in a row during a fade took the same activity. A/B with a session that ends under the lock: HEAD and the first fix alone both stay stuck on "Connection 6/7", keys dead; the complete fix (replay after the lock is dismissed, with no transition) returns to the VM list, keyboard alive.
  **AF3** HTTP requests on exit: 30 s → 3 s (1.5 s grace). **AF4** atomic writes (token, settings, lock): starting with only `refresh_token.new` → authenticated, token rewritten under its own name. **AF7** sockets left in `CLOSE_WAIT`: gone. **AF12** autotest plan preserved after an interrupted series. **UX11** closing during connection: 6.3 s → 0.8 s. **AF1** gamepad detach race: proven by injection (`SHADOW_DIAG_AF1_WIDEN_MS`).
  **Not measured**: AF8 (a few-instruction race), AF10 (nifm, Switch only, not compiled here), AF13 (`main_test.c`, Linux only: syntax check only). `[C80]` for those three.
  **Latent risk noted, NOT fixed**: at the end of a deliberate session, the connection thread sends two pops separated only by the duration of `delete_our_clients` (340 ms to 1.3 s measured), against a 200 ms fade. DELETEs that failed in under 200 ms (network down, immediate DNS failure) would reproduce the AF2/AF6 lock-up, lock screen or not. Refs: `clients/borealis/ui/nav_guard.cpp`, `clients/borealis/activity/connecting_activity.cpp` (end of session).

- **2026-09-03 (S119)** — **LINK QUALITY, VISIBLE WHILE YOU PLAY.** `[C95+]` The culmination of the evening: the user watched their picture degrade and had NO way of knowing why — the panel carried only frame rates, so they had to quit, pull the log over FTP and cross bitrate against loss by hand.
