The decoder was receiving **a slice without its parameter sets**: `Invalid data found when processing input`, 85 bytes missing from the access unit. Also fixed: reassembly's IDR detection (`nt == 5`, never true in HEVC, which would have emitted truncated IDRs in violation of **G43**), the NAL diagnostic counters (all zero in H.265, hence a mute panel exactly when you need it), and the first-slice flag offset (+1 in H.264, **+2** in HEVC). **NONE of these four defects produced an error** — each simply did nothing. That is this protocol's dominant failure mode, and it is why instrumentation (hex dump of the header, NAL-type log) settled things faster than reasoning at every step. Refs: `core/protocol/vid_wire.c` (+ 42 checks in `tests/test_vid_wire.c`, including both directions of the counter-case), `core/media/h264_decoder.c`, `core/protocol/vid_reasm.c`.
- **2026-08-28 (K15)** — **K13'S PREDICTION REFUTED: the codec is NOT readable in the `VideoFrame` header.** `[C95+]` We predicted `byte0 = 0x03` on H.265 (the CODEC nibble going from 2 to 3). Measurement gives **`0x12`**: version = **1**, codec = **2**. So it is not the codec that changes but the **frame-format version**; the codec nibble is 2 in both cases and **does not distinguish codecs**. The `version<<4 | codec` decomposition remains right, the interpretation "the codec is readable there" is WRONG. Measured practical consequence: a guard requiring `version == 0` dropped 100 % of H.265 frames, with a normal video bitrate and zero frames displayed — a defect that looked like "H.265 does not work". Header layouts confirmed by hex dump: KEY frame = 19 bytes (`12 f2 ab aa 23 | 01 | ext 13 B` then `00 00 00 01 40 01` = VPS), ORDINARY frame = 6 bytes (then `00 00 00 01 02 01` = TRAIL_R) — so **unchanged between H.264 and H.265**, only byte 0's version moves.
- **2026-08-27 (K13)** — **CODEC AND 4:4:4 IDENTIFIED — our codec selector WAS WRITING INTO THE VOID.** `[C95+]` Analysis of `captures/captures_settings_20260821_180409`, a guided capture made on 21/08 **for this very question** and never analysed for it. **The field we were writing the codec into is not the codec**: between the official client's "Set codec H265 requested" and "Set codec H264 requested", the sub-message `0a 06 22 04 10 02 20 01` is **strictly identical**, and is also identical across every change of bitrate, resolution and chroma. The values "H.265=1, AV1=3" were B1's guesses **and** the slot was wrong. **THE REAL SELECTOR is field 3 at the video level** (tag `0x18`), a WIRE enum: **H.264 = 0** (field ABSENT — proto3 default, the official client writes nothing), **H.265 = 1** (`18 01`, observed at t=368.4 s, 0.8 s after the request), **AV1 = 2** (by elimination, the descriptor pool carrying only "AV1H264H265"). **4:4:4 IS FIELD 5**, a BOOLEAN (`28 01`), named `high_color_fidelity` by the static RE — there is NO "chroma" field on the wire. It is exactly our `SHADOW_REG_F5`/`re8_f5`. **This explains F20's failure**: the campaign had enabled it then reverted it, the GUI staying on "Waiting for video..." for an "unknown reason" — the official telemetry shows `"chroma":"VC420","mode":"CUDA"` becoming `"chroma":"VC444","mode":"Software"`: enabling 4:4:4 **loses hardware decoding**, including for the official client on an NVIDIA machine. Renegotiation: changing the CODEC = `kUnregisterSession` + one video announcement; changing the CHROMA = `kRestart` + a full re-bootstrap (KB §3.24 wrongly grouped "codec return" with the kRestart). Two enums coexist: the INTERNAL one (H264=2, serving the telemetry and the `VideoFrame` header on the UDP wire, `byte0 = version<<4|codec` = 0x02) and the WIRE one (H264=0). **Falsifiable prediction**: an H.265 stream would carry `byte0 = 0x03`. Fixed: `SHADOW_CODEC` now drives `codec_fil` (field 3); `SHADOW_CODEC_LEGACY` keeps the old field; saved settings migrated (the old 2 = H.264 silently became AV1). Refs: `streaming/ctrl_msgs.{c,h}`, `clients/borealis/activity/quality_view.cpp`, `settings.cpp`.
- **2026-08-27 (K13b)** — **THE SWITCH WILL NEVER DECODE 4:4:4.** `[C95+]` Four independent locks, all opened and read in the sources ACTUALLY installed: `Envideo/src/nvgpu/device.cpp:523` forces `chip_id = 0x21` (T210) under `__SWITCH__`, hence `nvdec_version = V20`; `Envideo/src/constraints.cpp` case H264 refuses `subsample != 420` **on every generation** (H.264 4:4:4 exists on NO NVDEC); case H265, 4:4:4 requires V40 (Turing) and the V20 branch refuses `subsample != 420 || depth > 10`; and in the installed FFmpeg, `h264_slice.c:906` only offers `AV_PIX_FMT_NVTEGRA` outside CHROMA444/CHROMA422, `hevcdec.c` never on `AV_PIX_FMT_YUV444P`, and `nvtegra_hevc.c:291` hardcodes `chroma_format_idc = 1`. **To be said before anyone gets their hopes up**: "4:4:4" will remain an inoperative checkbox on this console. On the other hand `ff_hevc_nvtegra_hwaccel` **is compiled and linked** (verified: `nm` gives it as D in `libavcodec.a`, and `ar t` confirms the `nvtegra_*.o` files with no `envideo_*.o` at all) — **hardware H.265 is available**. The "empty surfaces" state of S7/S14 has been STALE since 2026-08-22 (FFmpeg 7.1.5 + Switchfin's nvtegra fix, transfer 5200 ms -> 1-2 ms, ~48 fps at 720p).
- **2026-08-27 (K14)** — **HIGH-FIDELITY AUDIO = FLAC, AND IT IS A SINGLE BYTE.** `[C95+]` The official setting is `audio-out-codec`, two values: `opus` = "Normal" (default) and `flac` = "High" (lossless). On the wire, **the last byte of the audio announcement body** (sequence 6, outer f2 = `RegisterSession_Audio`, field f4): `20 01` = Opus, `20 02` = FLAC — the rest of the message is identical byte for byte between an Opus session of 21/08 and a FLAC session of 26/08. The server **acknowledges the granted codec** in its announcement reply, along with the port: `28 f6 36` = 7030 and `20 02`. The setting touches ONLY the outbound audio: within the same session, the mic stays on Opus (`28 f8 36` = 7032, `20 01`). **The account already allows both**: `capabilities ... audio=yes codecs=opus,flac`, logged by our own client. **FLAC arrives with exactly the same wrapping as Opus** — 11-byte SUFP, chacha20-poly1305, cleartext `[0x12][u32 LE seq]` — then a **native** FLAC frame (sync `ff f8`), 48 kHz, stereo, 16-bit, **480 samples = 10 ms**, i.e. the same frame duration as Opus. Our client requests Opus **unconditionally** (`CI_BODY_8`) and **discards FLAC after correctly recognising it** (`core/media/audio.c:350`). So what is missing is not the understanding but the decoder — and `libavcodec` already ships `flac`. Not measured: the bandwidth overhead (no FLAC capture contains anything but 21-22 bytes of silence) and switching MID-SESSION (`CtrlChanV2ReconfigureAudioCodec`, neither captured nor decompiled). **Trap**: the binary's enum name pools (`FLACOPUSPCM`) are sorted ALPHABETICALLY and do not give the ordinals — it is the difference between two captures that establishes Opus=1 / Flac=2, not the pool.
- **2026-08-27 (K12)** — **`:base+20` CARRIES THE CURSOR — the "VideoSslTcpChannel = video" error is REFUTED BY ITS OWN BYTES.** `[C95+]` COMPLETE enumeration of the `:8020` channel **in cleartext** in `MASTER-20260514-153936`, the very capture from which the opposite conclusion was drawn: **1467 `0x12` frames** of 4142 B (= 10-byte header + 36-byte image header + 4096 px, a 32×32 **BGRA** image, format 2, not 128, hotspot 0,0), **30 `0x02` frames** of 10 B (cursor hidden), 1 split frame 1024+3118, and on the client side 1 registration `41 01 00 14 00 [hash20]` + 31 `70` + 6 `64`. **1499 reads = 100 % of the channel, zero bytes of video, zero Annex-B start codes.** METHOD, and it is what was missing: the hook logs the PLAINTEXT under `fd=-1` when it cannot resolve the descriptor — you have to group by `ssl=` pointer, not by `fd`, otherwise the channel looks unobservable. Three concordant proofs: exhaustive socket census (`8010 UDP, 8011 TCP, 8012 UDP, 8013 UDP, 8014 TCP, 8015 TCP, 8020 TCP, 8030 UDP, 8032 UDP` — **a single video socket, over UDP**); every key-frame request (`iP`, 130/130) goes out on `:8010` UDP; and `VideoChanManager::create_channels` (`sub_923B10`) is an **EXCLUSIVE** branch — `*(a1+140) != 2` -> `VideoUdpChannel` ("video: using UDP"), otherwise `VideoSslTcpChannel`/`VideoTcpChannel` ("video: using {}TCP"), all three variants reading the **same host and the same port**. The **STFP** format is real but common to ALL the protocol's TCP channels: **`byte0` does not identify the medium, the CHANNEL does** — and that is what made people believe for months that a video stream went through there. Artefacts corrected: `tools/ida/out/VIDEO_SSL_TCP_CHANNEL_RE.md` §0 (its TL;DR instructed "the implementing agent" to push those frames into the H.264 decoder), `core/protocol/ctrl_video_tcp.h`, and the user auto-memory line, loaded in EVERY session.
- **2026-08-27 (K12b)** — **THE UDP/TCP VIDEO MODE IS A USER SETTING, NAMED AFTER ITS TRANSPORT.** `[C95+]` UI bundle shipped: `options:{reliability:"Prefer reliability (TCP)", speed:"Prefer speed (UDP)"}`, description "Sets how the streaming data is sent to your device". On the command line: `--streaming-profile`, help "streaming profile to use. Values: speed (default), reliability". So the default is **UDP**. A purely **LOCAL** choice (`Client::SetStreamingProfile` is in-process, no transport field in our REST calls). **No capture in the `reliability` profile exists** — `reliability` appears in no file under `captures/`, `streaming_profile":"speed"` everywhere: evidence of absence, not absence of evidence. In TCP mode the video would go on the **Video** channel's port (base+10), not on +20 — deduced from the decompilation, to be confirmed by capture. `[C80]`
- **2026-08-27 (K12c)** — **THE SERVER ANSWERS ALL EIGHT ANNOUNCEMENTS; WE WERE DISCARDING SEVEN.** `[C95+]` `session_announce_channels()` sent eight announcements then did ONE SINGLE `ctrl_tcp_recv_cleartext`, on the strength of a comment ("server replies once after the batch") that is false. The other seven replies fell into the drain, truncated to four bytes by a log capped by a `static`. Yet the official client gets one line PER CHANNEL from them: `control chan video session granted : 1280x720@59.912 - H264 - UDP - SUFP`, `cursor : TCP - SUFP`, `filetransfer : TCP - SFTP` — i.e. the **granted codec, transport and protocol, channel by channel**. So the server had always been telling us that the video was over UDP and the cursor separate: the §3.37 campaign would have been unnecessary. Fixed: all eight replies are read (150 ms each, stopping at the first missing one, +150 ms worst case on start-up) and logged in hex and ASCII. `SHADOW_ANN_REPLIES=0` reverts to the old behaviour. This is now the shortest path to settling the **audio codec** (Opus or FLAC) and the **reliability mode's port**, without capturing the official client.
- **2026-08-27 (K7)** — **EXTENDED KEYS ARE A FIELD, NOT A PREFIX — K4 partially REFUTED, arrow keys FIXED.** `[C95+]` Guided capture of the official client (`tools/capture_keys.sh`, 91 keyboard messages, 22 distinct scancode/shape pairs). **There is NO `0xE0` prefix on the wire**: `@143` is 0 in 100 % of messages, live as well as in the 22,000+ across the repo's 29 captures. The client sends the BASE "set 1" scancode in one byte, plus a **separate boolean** — an optional FlatBuffers field, absent when false. Structural proof: the same byte serves TWO keys, and only that boolean distinguishes them — `0x1C` = main Enter **or** keypad Enter, `0x47` = KEYPAD 7 **or** Home. Layout: the table starts at `@136` and points its vtable backwards; ordinary shape vtable `@130` (size 6, one field, `+6` = u16 scancode), extended shape vtable `@128` (size 8, two fields, `+6` = scancode, `+5` = boolean at `@141` = 1). Seven bytes of difference, measured **identically on two scancodes**, hence structural. **22/22** agreement with set 1; Num Lock arrives extended without having an E0 prefix, which is Windows' documented oddity (bit 24 of lParam) and confirms the field really is "Windows' extended flag". **What we were doing**: writing `0xE0` into `@143`, hence sending a non-existent `0xE048` scancode — the exact cause of the dead arrow keys. `KBD_SET1_ETENDU` becomes an INTERNAL MARKER, stripped by `ctrl_input_tcp_send_scancode`, which selects the extended template. `SHADOW_KBD_ETENDU=0` restores the old behaviour. See `core/protocol/ctrl_input_tcp.c` (the K7 block above `KBD_TEMPLATE`), `core/protocol/kbd_scancode.h`, `tests/test_kbd_scancode.c` (299 checks), `tools/watch_keyboard.py`.
- **2026-08-27 (K7b)** — **THE K7 FIX WAS NOT TAKING: THE ARROW KEYS TYPED "8 2 4 6".** `[C95+]` The extended-template choice was made in `ctrl_input_tcp_send_scancode`, which receives the **RAW evdev** code — the conversion to set 1 only happens further down, in `send_template_with_xy`. Testing the `0xE0` marker against an evdev value could therefore **never** be true: the ordinary template was always chosen, the byte left correctly but **without its flag**, and `0x48` without the flag is KP8, hence "8". Likewise 0x50 -> "2", 0x4B -> "4", 0x4D -> "6". The test suite was green: it checked the TABLE, not the WIRING. **Structural fix**: `kbd_octet_fil(evdev, &etendue)` returns the byte AND the flag in one call, and both sites use it — divergence becomes impossible by construction. 823 checks in `test_kbd_scancode.c`, each case naming its symptom ("up arrow without the flag = KP8 = types 8"). **Lesson**: checking the data does not check the wiring.
- **2026-08-27 (K7c)** — **NUMERIC KEYPAD: already solved, with no new capture.** `[C95+]` KP1..KP9 (71,72,73,75,76,77,79,80,81) were ALREADY on the wire in two independent captures never exploited: `captures_states_20260821_034730` (correlated to the millisecond by `actions.tsv`) and `scenario_presse-papier_20260826_175800`. A keypad key's message is **structurally identical** to a letter's. Two documentation traps along the way: `CAPTURES_KB.md` lists **none** of the six `scenario_*` directories of 26/08, and §3.20 names `captures_input_20260602_132138` as "THE input reference" when it contains **not a single keystroke** (697 messages, all mouse). `tools/query_captures.sh` never inspects `captures/` — it could not have answered.
- **2026-08-27 (S63)** — **THE "B FROM SETTINGS" CRASH HAD NOTHING TO DO WITH THE SETTINGS.** The `ScrollingFrame` in the call stack is the MACHINE LIST's, redrawn after the pop — and it is in fact the app's only `ScrollingFrame` in `NATURAL` mode (the three settings screens are `centered`), hence the only one that runs `naturalScrollingBehaviour()`. Chain verified link by link: `popActivity` calls `toShow->onResume()` (application.cpp:845) -> `VmListActivity::onResume` calls `refreshVms()` from the 2nd visit onwards -> `refreshVms` did `giveFocus(contentView)` THEN `clearViews()`, **and that `giveFocus` was a NO-OP**: `giveFocus` resolves its target through `getDefaultFocus()` and `Box::getDefaultFocus()` RECURSES DOWN via `lastFocusedView` (box.cpp:357-361), returning the card that already has focus — `oldFocus == newFocus`, and the application.cpp:786 guard skips the whole body. So the "CRITICAL" comment described a protection that never ran. `clearViews()` then freed the card, `currentFocus` dangled, and on the next frame `naturalScrollingBehaviour()` -> `giveFocus(this)` -> `oldFocus->onFocusLost()` (a VIRTUAL call) jumped into a freed vtable. Hence **Instruction Abort** and not Data Abort — the distinction was the best clue and it was right in front of me. Fix: clear FIRST, give focus AFTER, to `vmScroll`, which survives the cleanup. **"I changed a setting" was NOT causal**: the real discriminant is `premiere_apparition` — the 1st return to the list reloads nothing, the 2nd and later ones crash. Secondary cause, fixed as a safety net: `giveFocus(nullptr)` was a complete no-op, which made `View`'s destructor's "Focus sanity check" (view.cpp:1486) DEAD — any view destroyed while holding focus left a dangling pointer.

- **2026-08-27 (tooling)** — `library/borealis/` is **ignored by git** (`.gitignore:74`). Any local patch there is invisible and disappears on a fresh clone. Now tracked in `05-shadow-client-borealis/patches/borealis/` (README + `apply.sh`, which checks they are present). To be verified after any re-vendoring.

- **2026-08-27 (S61/S62 + Borealis)** — Three navigation defects, diagnosed from a CRASH REPORT and not from a hypothesis. (1) **B from the settings crashed the app**: `ScrollingFrame::naturalScrollingBehaviour()` dereferenced `Application::getCurrentFocus()` WITHOUT a null check, and that pointer is set back to `nullptr` when an activity is popped — the frame is still drawn once between the two. An UPSTREAM defect (present in `h_scrolling_frame.cpp` too), fixed locally in both. Atmosphère stack: `Instruction Abort` -> `ScrollingFrame::draw` -> `naturalScrollingBehaviour`. (2) **Leaving the stream returned to the BOOT SCREEN** (stuck on "Connected", B quit the app): two paths popped the connecting screen — the B button and the worker thread — and the cleanup between the two does a network `DELETE`, a window wide enough for a B press to slip into. The second pop took the VM list with it. Popping made exclusive by a shared flag. (3) **Auto-connect re-triggered on returning from the stream** (`onResume` -> `refreshVms` -> auto-connect 500 ms later): impossible to reach the settings. It becomes a SETTING (it only existed as an environment variable, unreachable on console) and arms only once per lifetime of the screen.
- **2026-08-27 (G49)** — **GAMEPAD RECOGNISED END TO END** (G48 + S58/S59/S60 validated live: detected, usable on a gamepad test site). But Steam shows **FOUR** of them, and P6 is REFUTED: its announcement reminder at 1 s / 3 s / 6 s creates a NEW gamepad each time instead of refreshing the existing one. The P6 comment asserted that "a surplus announcement is harmless" — that is false, and it is what breaks games: a test site enumerates EVERY device and reacts to any of them (so the pad looks perfect there), whereas a game binds to just ONE — if it picks a phantom instance it receives nothing more while we are still sending. `SHADOW_GAMEPAD_REPLUG` moves to **0** by default. See §3.39.

- **2026-08-27 (K4)** — **THE KEYBOARD'S EXTENDED KEYS HAD NEVER WORKED** (arrows, Delete, Windows). Our virtual keyboard sends **evdev** codes; but for the whole main keyboard the evdev code and the "set 1" PC scancode are the SAME number (Esc 1, Tab 15, Q 16, Enter 28, Shift 42, Alt 56, Space 57, F1-F10 59-68, F11/F12 87/88). The K1 guided capture contained only LETTERS: it was therefore **incapable of distinguishing** "the server wants evdev" from "the server wants set 1" — both predict the same bytes. The two numberings diverge exactly on the extended keys: Up 103 vs `E0 48`, Left 105 vs `E0 4B`, Right 106 vs `E0 4D`, Down 108 vs `E0 50`, Delete 111 vs `E0 53`, Windows 125 vs `E0 5B`. The correspondence "what works = where the two coincide" is perfect. **⚠️ PARTIALLY SUPERSEDED by K7 (2026-08-27)**: the evdev-vs-set-1 diagnosis is CONFIRMED, but the prefix shape announced here — `0xE0` in the high byte of `@142/@143` — is **REFUTED by measurement**. There is no prefix: extended keys carry a **separate boolean field**. The guided capture took place and settled it; see the K7 line at the top of this log. `SHADOW_KBD_SET1=0` reverts to raw evdev. See `core/protocol/kbd_scancode.h`, `tests/test_kbd_scancode.c`.

- **2026-08-27 (K2)** — Virtual keyboard: function row added (Windows + F1-F12). Without the Windows key there is no way to open the Start menu without the mouse. Scancode trap: F1..F10 run in sequence (59..68) but **F11 = 87 and F12 = 88**, the run is not contiguous.

- **2026-08-27 (i18n)** — The French translation of Borealis' labels had **never** been applied: the i18n key is the **filename** (`i18n.cpp::loadLocale`), and `fr/` contained a `brls.json` nesting everything under `hints` — producing `brls/hints/*` keys that nothing queries. Hence the English "On"/"Off" in the settings. File split into `hints.json` / `crash_frame.json` / `thumbnail_sidebar.json`.

- **2026-08-27 (S56/S57)** — INTERMITTENT AUDIO RESOLVED, **10/10 against 30 %**. We were killing the channel ourselves: both our counters were 0, so the ping and re-registration left ~200 ms after registration — exactly when the first audio frame is in flight (201-227 ms). The official client stays quiet for 7.5 s. And the server was TELLING us: the notification that correlated 32/32 means "AUDIO channel down", decoded thanks to the official binary's channel-name table. See §3.38.

- **2026-08-26 (channel map)** — THE OFFICIAL CLIENT NAMES ITS SOCKETS in its telemetry, port included. THREE lines of our map were wrong: `+20` is the **Cursor** (not the video — and we have been discarding its bitmaps all along through the `[RE11]` guard), `+30` is **AudioOut alone** (not "cursor + audio multiplexed"), `+14` is the **Clipboard** (not the input). `+15` = SFTP/SSH, `+32` = mic. The audio codec is NEGOTIATED (Opus on 21/08, FLAC on 26/08). See §3.37.

- **2026-08-26 (guided captures)** — GAMEPAD: wire format decoded (`04 00 01 00 … axis value value+0x80`, Y axis inverted, scale 32767/127), `:base+13` channel CONFIRMED. "Controllers" and "USB" are two distinct functions — the usbredir note did not describe the gamepad. CLIPBOARD and FILE TRANSFER: both on `:base+14`, identified by their cleartext content; we never open that channel. `DELETE /N/clients/{id}` at end of session on the official side — wired into the UI (S51). And a HOOK DEFECT fixed: it logged `EVP_ENCRYPT_IN` AFTER the call, but Shadow encrypts in place — so all our encryption captures showed CIPHERTEXT instead of cleartext. See §3.36.

- **2026-08-26 (S48)** — PER-STREAM deregistration (8 messages like the official client, instead of one empty one): a real discrepancy, fixed, but **REFUTED** as a cause — 3/10 on a clean series, i.e. the baseline rate. The "Video stream reported => no sound" correlation holds across **18 consecutive sessions**. New fact: sound arrives in GROUPS (3 sessions in a row), so it is persistent server state, not an independent draw. See §3.35.

- **2026-08-26 (S46)** — THE SERVER ANNOUNCES WHY THE AUDIO IS SILENT. Its small status reports, until now logged only by their size, list the registered entities: entity `05` alone -> sound present (3/3), entity `01` present -> sound absent (5/5). **Correlation 8/8.** The first explanation compatible with the intermittency, the binary character and the byte-exact bootstrap. See §3.35. Along the way: S42+S47 (bootstrap and timing identical to the official client) REFUTED with power (3/8 vs 3/10).

- **2026-08-25 (S38/S40/S41)** — The ~120 `SHADOW_*` toggles were UNUSABLE on console (no environment at all): `env.txt` now loads them, with a check that `setenv` took. STREAM EXIT RESOLVED (§3.33): the trial loop was hijacking the abort flag even outside a series. `SHADOW_AUTH_FULL_MAP=1` REFUTED for audio (applied, accepted, channel still silent). New live bug: the video UDP dies at t=57 s with the server alive (§3.34), the S41 recovery still to be measured.

- **2026-08-25 (S37)** — CRASH RESOLVED: `&ctx` passed as the TCP video channel's `udata` when `ctx` was already a pointer (a regression from our extraction of `session_open_media`). The callback read a dead stack slot and crashed on the FIRST key-frame resend — hence a late, seemingly random crash. `SESSION_CTX_MAGIC` sentinel added on the 4 `void *` callbacks. See §3.32.

- **2026-08-25 (S36)** — SOUND RESOLVED: the audio's de-duplication window lived in a `static` and outlived the session; since the server renumbers on every stream, **100 % of frames** were discarded from the 2nd session on (3997/3997, against 1999/3998 on the first). So the sound was only audible on the very first launch. See §3.31. Along the way §3.29 is rephrased: the `:base+30` channel only lights up when the VM emits sound — 3998 audio packets out of 4003 — so an autotest on a silent desktop does not measure audio.

- **2026-08-25 (S35)** — The UDP receive buffer is capped at **42,240 bytes** on console (`0xA500`, libnx's default): N56's 4 MB `SO_RCVBUF` has no effect, because Borealis initialises the socket driver without raising the buffer sizes. Explains the ~0.7 % UDP loss and the repeated key frames. See §3.30.

- **2026-08-25 (S34)** — BLACK SCREEN FROM THE 3rd SESSION RESOLVED: the server only sends a key frame on the first session; two function `static`s (`g_last_evt_idr_tick` compared against a counter reset to zero, and G13's `== 15`) prevented any request afterwards. 18 sessions out of 20 without a single frame, with a normal inbound bitrate. Rules isolated in `idr_policy.h` + tests. See §3.28. Same series: cursor/audio channel active 13/20 (65 %), not a platform defect (§3.29).

- **2026-08-25** — 🔬 **FIRST AUTOMATIC SERIES: ten back-to-back sessions, 8146 lines received live.** The app streams its log to the dev machine (`logsink.txt`) and runs N sessions on its own (`autotest.txt`): a trial now costs ONE human gesture instead of four. Four results only a long series could give. **(1)** The `:base+30` channel **DID START on console** — one session in ten (`cursor` 26 → 1003 → 5006, `audio` up to 5001). So it is NOT systematically broken on Switch: it is INTERMITTENT, as on desktop where it starts ~6 times in 7. Previous wording corrected. **(2)** **Audio does NOT degrade the video**: session with audio, 1277 frames decoded; sessions without, 1205/1278/1288/1291. Identical, comparable losses. The reported "frame loss when the sound arrives" was the trial loop's own cycle. **(3)** Software Opus decoding **costs 135 µs per frame** (A5 instrumentation) — negligible, which confirms Moonlight-Switch's and Switchfin's choice not to use `hwopus`. Keep `SHADOW_HWOPUS=0`. **(4)** **2785 fallbacks to a CPU copy**: ffmpeg reports "Frame address/pitch not aligned to 256" on nearly every frame — so the `nvtegra` path was not using its VIC transfer. The pitch is fine, though (1280 = 5×256): it is the destination ADDRESS, `sw_frame` being allocated by `av_hwframe_transfer_data` with the default alignment of 32. Fixed (S33) by pre-allocating the destination with `av_frame_get_buffer(.., 256)`, falling back to the old behaviour if the allocation fails. **TO BE MEASURED in the next series.**


- **2026-08-25** — ❌ **REFUTED: the missing registration on `:base+13` does NOT explain the invisible gamepad.** The input channel was the only one of the three UDP channels not sending its 25-byte register packet (video `+10` and cursor `+30` do). The hypothesis held together: without it, the server would not associate our source port with the session and would discard our gamepad packets. **Tested on console**: registration duly sent (`:11013 OK reg=25B sent=25`), 670 gamepad messages emitted, **Steam still sees nothing**. The second half of the original hypothesis — "our unexpected packet invalidates the input channel" — is refuted too: mouse and keyboard kept working (40 `[S16]` position echoes received). BOTH fears fall. `udp_register_input` setting kept, default 0.
- **2026-08-25** — 🔇 **AUDIO AND CURSOR: the `:base+30` channel is INTERMITTENT, cause NOT FOUND.** Two hypotheses raised then REFUTED the same day, by measurement: (1) *"it's IPv4"* — the channel failed once on desktop over **IPv6** (27 s session, video at 52.9 fps, `cursor=0`), so it is not the address family; (2) *"it's the slow bootstrap"* — the correlation looked strong (failure at 8410 ms, successes at 4158/4661/5363/6039 ms) but a later session at **11580 ms worked perfectly**. Real state: on desktop the channel starts in ~6 sessions out of 7; on Switch it has never started (6 sessions, 210 s, 100 re-registrations, the 7.5 s ping correctly emitted). The 25-byte registration goes out, the server never answers. **Reproducing the Switch conditions on desktop remains impossible**: `SHADOW_FORCE_IPV4=1` prevents the CONTROL channel from connecting from this machine (3 attempts), while the console manages it on the same network — which is itself an unexplored lead. Previous entry kept below for the IPv4 lead's history.
- **2026-08-25** — 🔇 **(IPv4 lead, since refuted — see above)** The `:base+30` channel carries both the cursor and the sound (§3.26). Measured: **Linux 6001 packets, Switch ZERO**, over 210 s with 100 re-registrations and the 7.5 s ping correctly emitted. The socket is created, the registration goes out, the server never answers. The only structural difference noted: **Linux connects over IPv6** (`<VM public IPv6>::`), **the Switch over IPv4** (`87.121.209.x`), the HOS resolver being unreliable on AAAA. Yet the video channel (`+10`, UDP, IPv4) works on Switch, so it is not "UDP does not work" — the tenable lead is NAT return on a low-bitrate channel. `SHADOW_FORCE_IPV4=1` toggle added to reproduce the console's conditions on a desktop; the decisive experiment is still to be done (it requires the console to free the client slot, without which creating the main client returns HTTP 409).


- **2026-08-25** — 🔐 **A REST call carrying the session JWT went out without verifying the certificate.** `proximus_delete()` set `SSL_VERIFYPEER=0` and `SSL_VERIFYHOST=0` while targeting the SAME proximus host as the file's six other requests — which do verify — and while carrying the token in its `Authorization` header: a network intermediary could present any certificate and collect it. `git log -S` shows the function added in one block with those values (commit `8d1ad12`, 2026-08-21) and never touched since: a copy-paste omission, not a workaround. Verification restored (`1L`/`2L`). **There is now only one place where it is disabled**, `http.c::http_check_connectivity`, justified from the start (a request to a literal IP, with the name absent from the certificate). Found while trying to factor out the curl settings repeated across three files — factoring ABANDONED after measurement: `CURLOPT_TIMEOUT` is 5, 10, `SHADOW_HTTP_TOTAL_TIMEOUT` or a computed duration depending on the call, so what looked like duplication is per-call configuration, and unifying it would have changed timeouts on the authentication path.


- **2026-08-25** — 🧵 **The input channel ignored its own framing, and 3.07 MiB per session went into a reassembler that was never fed.** (1) `:base+14` carries FlatBuffers **prefixed with their size** (`[u32 LE][payload]`, total = size+4), attested on both sides: our Connect starts with `5c 00 00 00` (92 = 96-4), the server echo with `64 00 00 00` (100 = 104-4) — cf. §3.27. Yet the receive thread treated every `wolfSSL_read` as a whole message (testing `n == 104`): two coalesced echoes would have been forwarded to the **audio decoder**. **Measured**: 100 % of reads are 104 B in the logged sessions (1365 + 282 messages), so the defect is **real but dormant** — it would wake the day this channel carries traffic, which is the work in progress. Framing implemented (`msgframe.{c,h}`, a pure function, 23 checks + 3 M ASan fuzz), toggle `SHADOW_INPUT_RX_FRAMING=0`. (2) `ctx.sufp`: a second SUFP reassembler created and destroyed every session **without ever receiving a byte**, ever since `vid_reasm.c` started parsing the header inline — **3.07 MiB** measured (1.07 structure + 2.00 buffer), removed along with its `on_video_full_frame` callback which **duplicated** the chacha20 decryption and the VideoFrame header read. (3) `cursor_state.c` and `sufp.c` audited **sound** (bounds and frees correct, verified under ASan); 32 checks added on SUFP. (4) **SETTLED the same day (S32)**: `sufp.c` treated `max_chunks` as a COUNT, whereas G4/G21 proved that field is the LAST INDEX. The proof for the cursor channel needed no capture — a single-packet cursor frame carries `max_chunks == 0` (the condition of the direct path in `on_cursor_packet`), which only makes sense under last-index semantics ("last index 0" = one chunk); under count semantics it would read "zero chunks". Consequence of the old code: a TWO-chunk cursor frame (max=1) had its index 1 rejected, then `recv_count == max_chunks` (1==1) declared it complete — **delivered with half of it missing**, G4 all over again on another channel. Fixed, field renamed `chunk_count`, toggle `SHADOW_SUFP_MAX_PLUS1=0`. Refs: commits `6ce3a10`, `58e9186`, `45edad6`. **(5) Coverage closed the same day**: the encryption is checked against the **RFC 8439 §2.8.2** vector (§3.3 moves to C100) and the message BUILDERS are pinned — the UDP register packet and the eight channel-announcement bodies, which had cost an LD_PRELOAD campaign and had no safeguard at all. Moment of truth: one altered byte in production code's `CI_BODY_7` makes the suite fail while naming the channel. **288 offline checks across six suites**, including three fuzz campaigns under ASan/UBSan.


- **2026-08-25** — 🛡️ **The protobuf decoder could hang the control thread on a malformed server reply, and two parsers were reading wrong field numbers.** `proto.c` checked its bounds with `(size_t)off + dlen > len`: with a declared length near 2^64 the addition **overflows**, the check passes, and `pb_skip_field` returns an offset that goes **BACKWARDS** (length 2^64-11 -> returns 0 when the offset was 1). The `while ((size_t)off < len)` loops in `ctrl_msgs.c` then spin **forever**; a **12-byte** message is enough. On Switch this is the worst case: a blocked thread no longer polls its `abort_flag`, `process_exit` kills it brutally, HOS leaks its handles, **reboot required** (§7). `pb_read_lendelim` also returned `out_len = 2^64-1` to the caller — our consumers demand an exact size and got away with it, but nothing guaranteed that. **Fix**: subtraction instead of addition (`dlen <= len - off`), refusal of negative offsets and of lengths beyond `INT_MAX`, rejection of a non-canonical 10th varint byte, and **strictly** increasing progress enforced in `pb_iter_fields`. **Verification**: `tests/test_proto.c` (57 checks, both exploits marked `[DoS]`) — run against the previous `proto.c`, the suite **hangs** (exit code 124); fuzzed under ASan/UBSan, **4,000,000 iterations**, zero crashes / zero out-of-bounds reads / zero hangs, the same campaign stopping dead on the old version. Non-regression: the **8 byte-exact captured channel-announcement bodies** are still accepted, sub-messages included. **In the same pass**, `tests/test_ctrl_msgs.c` (25 checks) flushed out two parsers still on the **old** reply field numbers (cf. §3.6): `ctrl_parse_authentication_reply` (exported, no callers, would have reported a permanent auth failure) now delegates to v2, and `enc_reply_field_cb` (dead code announcing the key at field 3 instead of 4) is removed along with its six `FN_ENC_*` constants. Refs: `05-shadow-client-borealis/tests/`, commits `5f9e8cc` + `639ba31`.


- **2026-08-22** — 🏆 **HARDWARE DECODING WORKING ON SWITCH — the culprit was the Averne fork's `envideo` generation, not our client.** Switched from Averne's FFmpeg `c8ff0ba` (**envideo** generation) to **upstream FFmpeg 7.1.5 + Switchfin's nvtegra patch** (`scripts/switch/ffmpeg/{PKGBUILD,ffmpeg-7.1.5.patch,getnameinfo.patch,avio.patch}`, `--enable-nvtegra`, archive verified by sha256). **Before/after measurement of the GPU->CPU transfer: 5200 ms -> 1-2 ms** (a factor of ~3000), clean picture, ~48 fps at 720p, `aband=0`. This confirms the code reading: the `envideo` transfer goes through the GPU copy engine, whose channel has no submission delay (nvgpu watchdog 5000 ms = our exact measurement) and waits on a host1x barrier invisible from the GPU side -> task killed, `rc=0`, surface never written; the **nvtegra** path goes through the **VIC**, which the project's original comment already assumed ("~11ms 1080p"). Log: `accelerateur trouve par nom -> type=13 (nvtegra)`, `hwaccel materiel retenu : nvtegra (fmt=228)`. **Our code needed NO modification at all for the switch**: since S16 the device is looked up by NAME at runtime (`av_hwdevice_find_type_by_name`, "envideo" then "nvtegra") and the hardware format recognised by its `AV_PIX_FMT_FLAG_HWACCEL` flag — that was exactly the point of that fix, and it paid off. **Remaining errors**: only 19 `Frame num gap` (no `decode_slice_header error`, no concealment) = a consequence of the **1.2 % UDP loss over Wi-Fi** (`perdus=331/27519`), not a decoding defect; G43 absorbs the common case (tail loss), hence the clean picture. Display (G44): 21-23 ms between frames (~45 fps), 3.1 % of freezes >=45 ms. **Rolling back**: Averne's tree remains intact in `07-averne-ffmpeg/FFmpeg` (a `make install` in there restores envideo); the nvtegra build is kept in `07-averne-ffmpeg/nvtegra-build/`. **Residual trap**: `hwcontext_envideo.h` is still lying around in portlibs without being backed by the `.a` (to be removed). **Opens the way to hardware 1080p** — the software ceiling was the real reason for 720p. Refs: `h264_decoder.c` (S16/S28/S30), previous KB entry (Moonlight/Switchfin comparison).
- **2026-08-22** — 🎯 **Switch hardware decoding: NOBODY does a GPU->CPU copy. The correct path is ZERO-COPY rendering in deko3d.** Comparison of two reference clients that decode in hardware on Switch (`Moonlight-Switch` by XITRIX, `Switchfin` by dragonflylee, cloned into `/tmp/hwref`). **Verdict**: neither of them calls `av_hwframe_transfer_data()` on Switch — they import the decoded surface **as is** into deko3d as a block-linear `DkImage` backed by the decoder's nvmap allocation (`DKVideoRenderer.cpp:981-1032`: `av_nvtegra_frame_get_fbuf_map` -> `DkMemBlockMaker.setStorage(cpuAddr)` -> `luma R8_Unorm` + half-size `chroma RG8_Unorm`, `DkImageFlags_UsageVideo` = block-linear; the import is **cached by nvmap handle**, so once per pool surface, not per frame). Switchfin does the same thing in an `ra_hwdec_nvtegra` mpv driver. Corroborated inside OUR own Envideo: `src/nvgpu/device.cpp:789-793` forces `map_cpu()` under `#if defined(__SWITCH__)` with the comment "used when mapping video buffers to deko3d" — the GPU->CPU copy is a portability fallback, not the intended path. **Mechanism of the 5.2 s** (read statically, not measured at runtime): the copy channel has **no** submission timeout (`Envideo/src/nvgpu/channel.cpp:165` only sets `set_submit_timeout(1000)` on the host1x branch; the `EnvideoEngine_Copy` branch `:171-188` inherits the nvgpu watchdog, **5000 ms** on HOS — hence our exact 5.2 s), and `hwcontext_envideo.c:344-356` makes the GPU channel wait on a barrier produced by NVDEC's **host1x** channel: if that sync point is not visible in the GPU's address space, the channel blocks until the watchdog, the task is killed, the barrier forced -> `rc=0` and a destination **never written** (all zeros). A chain consistent with our measurements (NVDEC succeeds: 65,593 B, 2 slices, a real fence, `tegra_layout=1`). Minor aggravating factor: we remap the destination on EVERY frame (`av_frame_unref(sw_frame)` -> `envideo_map_from_va` + `map_destroy` = ~3 MB of nvmap created/mapped/destroyed per frame). **Structuring constraint**: there is no evidence that hardware decoding + **OpenGL** rendering is viable on Switch without a CPU round trip — both references **require** deko3d (Moonlight even refuses to start if deko3d is active without NVTEGRA, `FFmpegVideoDecoderDekoHelpers.cpp:13-15`). **Cheaper lead (P2)**: our FFmpeg `c8ff0ba` has **removed nvtegra** (`nm libavutil.a | grep -c nvtegra` = 0 against 41 envideo symbols); both references pin the **7.1.x nvtegra** branch, whose transfer goes through the **VIC** (~11 ms, which our original comment already assumed) and not through the faulty copy engine. Recipe: `Switchfin/scripts/switch/ffmpeg/PKGBUILD:48` + `ffmpeg-7.1.5.patch`. **Trap to clean up**: `hwcontext_nvtegra.h`/`nvtegra.h` are still lying around in `/opt/devkitpro/portlibs/switch/include/libavutil/` without being backed by the installed `.a`. Refs: agent report 2026-08-22, `gl_video_renderer.cpp` (our GL renderer), `library/borealis/library/cmake/commonOption.cmake:36` (`USE_DEKO3D` already available, default OFF), `05-shadow-client-borealis/CMakeLists.txt:353-355` (`gen_dksh` already wired).


- **2026-08-22** — **Switch HARDWARE decoding (`envideo`): INOPERATIVE, cause not identified — but two real bugs fixed and an automatic fallback.** **(1) Inconsistent FFmpeg installation**: libraries = Averne build `c8ff0ba` (exposes "envideo"), headers = devkitPro package (declare "nvtegra"). **13 headers diverged** in `libavutil` alone, structures included: we were compiling against a different definition from what was running (`AV_PIX_FMT_NVTEGRA=228` on the header side against `envideo=243` on the library side; verified by parsing, NV12=23 matching as a control). Fixed outside the code: `sudo env "PATH=/opt/devkitpro/devkitA64/bin:$PATH" make install` in `07-averne-ffmpeg/FFmpeg` (the libraries are identical to the bit — md5 — only the headers and `.pc` are realigned; **without the PATH, the install fails on `aarch64-none-elf-ranlib: not found`**). After realignment: 0 divergences, `.pc` 59.53/61.28. **(2) Code made version-insensitive (S15/S16)**: Averne RENAMED `NVTEGRA` -> `ENVIDEO` between two versions; so we look the device up by NAME at runtime (`av_hwdevice_find_type_by_name`) and the hardware format by its flag (`AV_PIX_FMT_FLAG_HWACCEL`), no longer by constant. Negotiation becomes correct (`type=13 (envideo)`, `fmt=243` genuinely CHOSEN rather than fallen into). **(3) Persistent symptom**: `envideo` accepts everything — device created, format negotiated, `av_hwframe_transfer_data` rc=0 — but **NEVER** writes into the surfaces (`source luma moy=0 min=0 max=0` measured on the CPU side; software gives `moy=39 max=233` on the same stream). **Hypotheses REFUTED by measurement**: wrong format selected (S15 fixed, no effect), desynchronised headers (fixed, no effect), **applet mode** (S17: the console runs as `APPLICATION`, AppletType=0). **(4) Automatic fallback (S18)**: the existing fallback only counted FAILED transfers, so it never fired here; we now sample the first transferred frames and 5 entirely black frames set `hwaccel.disabled` + log what to do. **State**: clean picture in SOFTWARE decoding (~51 fps at 720p, fixes G37-G43 validated on console by the user). **Next lead**: isolate with Averne's tools/examples (Envideo has tests), check the Envideo library's init (nvgpu/nvmap) and compare with Moonlight-Switch, which uses the same stack. Refs: `h264_decoder.c` (S15-S18), `07-averne-ffmpeg/`, commit `ffa0ff2` then this one.


- **2026-08-22** — 🏆 **FIRST VIDEO PICTURE ON SWITCH** (complete chain) + **`envideo` HARDWARE decoding returns EMPTY surfaces**. Bootstrap, stable stream (45,000 packets, `rx_age=0`, 1.1 % loss) and a picture displayed at **~51 fps in SOFTWARE decoding** at 720p. **Root cause of the black screen, measured and not assumed**: the Switch's hardware decoder does NOT select `AV_PIX_FMT_NVTEGRA` but another hardware format (`fmt=243 "envideo"`, log "NVTEGRA not in pix_fmts list"); `av_hwframe_transfer_data` then returns **rc=0 (success announced) but fills an EMPTY surface** — measured CPU-side before any GPU upload: `source luma moy=0 min=0 max=0`, against `moy=39 min=18 max=233` once in software. The GL renderer was innocent (rectangle, viewport, GL state and pixel readback all verified: `rect=(0,0 1280x720) vp=1280x720 err=0x0`). **Four blocking bugs cleared along the way**: (S9) we only transferred GPU->CPU if the format was EXACTLY NVTEGRA — otherwise the frame went out with GPU pointers to `pushYuvFrame`, which refuses it (silently); we now rely on `hw_frames_ctx`; (S10) the asynchronous transfer worker **never processed anything** (`processed=0`, stuck in its 1st `av_hwframe_transfer_data` — the VIC engine appears to be tied to the decoder's owning thread) and froze the whole stream around t=20 s: **synchronous** transfer by default, the worker having become useless since the G25 dedicated decode thread; (S8) auto-connect to a single VM used a DETACHED `std::thread` (systematic crash ~500 ms after the list, before `onVmClicked` was even reached) -> `brls::Threading::delay`; (S14) **the software fallback was UNREACHABLE on console** — the hwaccel decision was written twice and the Switch block created the NVTEGRA device *unconditionally, before* the env+marker logic: neither `SHADOW_HWACCEL=0` nor `/switch/shadow-client/hwaccel.disabled` had any effect; a single `hw_allowed()` function now decides. **Methodological lesson**: two hypotheses (GL slicing, then placement) were REFUTED by instrumentation before being coded — hence the systematic addition of measurements (`[S11]` GL state, `[S12]` rect/viewport/error/pixel read back, `[S13]` CPU source sample) rather than "most likely" fixes. **Remaining**: fix the Averne/envideo hardware path (empty surfaces) — software holds 51 fps at 720p in the meantime; `hwaccel.disabled` on the SD card is the lever. Refs: `h264_decoder.c` (S9/S10/S14 `hw_allowed`), `gl_video_renderer.cpp` (S11-S13), `clients/borealis/activity/vm_list_activity.cpp` (S8), commits `ffa0ff2` / `e77da48`.


- **2026-08-22** — **G47: REGRESSION introduced by G45 — "Displayed 5 fps". NEVER put a system probe in a dev-menu callback.** Symptom: reception 60/s, decoding fine (G44: 17-18 ms between delivered frames = ~57/s), but **display collapsed to 5/s**. Cause: `MenuBar::draw()` calls `layout()` on EVERY DRAW (`clients/borealis/devui/menu_bar.cpp:326`), and `layout()` evaluates `Info::measure()` for **every item of every menu, even closed ones** — so G45's `info("Controller")` called `ctrl_gamepad_present()` **60 times per second**, and that probe opened all **26** `/dev/input/event*` with two `ioctl`s each. Opening an evdev node wakes the driver (tens of ms on some USB/Bluetooth devices) -> ~200 ms per frame -> a render loop at 5/s. The "Displayed" counter (`frames_presented_`) increments once per draw: so it was measuring the render loop, not the decoding — hence the gap with "Decoded". **Fix G47**: (1) detection WITHOUT opening the device — reading the capability masks as text from `/sys/class/input/<ev>/device/capabilities/{key,abs}` (64-bit hex words, the LAST one carrying bits 0-63); (2) result **cached for 1 s** (hotplug seen at worst one second later). Verified on the dev machine: 26 devices scanned, 0 gamepads detected (correct), no false positives on keyboard/mouse/Logitech receiver. **General rule worth keeping: a dev-menu callback runs on the RENDER thread at 60 Hz — it must be purely in-memory (reading a variable), never a syscall, a directory scan or an I/O.** Refs: `ctrl_gamepad.c` (`sysfs_is_gamepad`, `sysfs_bit_set`, cache in `ctrl_gamepad_present`), `clients/borealis/devui/menu_bar.cpp:326` (layout per draw).


- **2026-08-22** — **G45: we were announcing a PHANTOM gamepad every session ("stuck in game") + G46: keys reserved by the Linux desktop.** **G45**: `ctrl_gamepad_attach()` called `ctrl_gamepad_plug()` UNCONDITIONALLY — so the VM created a virtual gamepad every session, even with no gamepad plugged in. Windows and games then switch to gamepad mode and IGNORE keyboard/mouse: the reported symptom "I end up stuck in the game" (no input responds). The menu meanwhile displayed "Gamepad: connected" because it read `ctrl_gamepad_active()` = **network channel open**, not the presence of a gamepad — the two were conflated. Fix: a new probe `ctrl_gamepad_present()` (evdev: the device must expose a button in the `BTN_GAMEPAD..BTN_THUMBR` family AND the `ABS_X/ABS_Y` axes — keyboards/mice/sensors fail that test; re-scanned on every call so hotplug is accounted for); the announcement is sent ONLY if a gamepad is detected (`SHADOW_GAMEPAD_PLUG=1` forces it, `=0` forbids it, the menu's manual action remains). **Second bug fixed along the way**: the local evdev reader (`SHADOW_GAMEPAD=1`) opened EVERY `/dev/input/event*`, hence the keyboard and mouse, whose codes fell into the gamepad mapping — it now takes only real gamepads. Menu: two distinct lines `Controller: plugged in/none detected` and `Channel: open/closed`. **G46**: GNOME/Ubuntu reserves **Super (the Windows key)** at the compositor level — the window never receives the event (it opens Ubuntu's Activities instead of the VM's Start menu); likewise Ctrl+Alt+Del. No application can reclaim those shortcuts under Wayland (the official client has the same limitation and therefore offers to send them explicitly). Added a **Keys** menu: `Send Windows key` (evdev 125), `Ctrl+Alt+Del`, `Alt+Tab`, `Win+D`, via `stream_view_send_key/_combo` (the usual keyboard path, evdev scancodes, correct press/release ordering for modifiers). Also added the **RIGHT Super** mapping (126 = KEY_RIGHTMETA), which was missing, useful when the desktop does not capture that key (X11 / GNOME shortcut disabled). Refs: `ctrl_gamepad.c` (G45: `dev_is_gamepad`, `ctrl_gamepad_present`, `ctrl_gamepad_attach`), `clients/borealis/devui/dev_menu.cpp`, `clients/borealis/activity/stream_view.cpp` (G46).
- **2026-08-22** — 🎯🎯🎯🎯 **G43: the residual smearing = frames with a LOST TAIL being DISCARDED; we now emit them TRUNCATED. Final root cause.** G42 diagnosis (targeted counters): `emit_legacy` discards NOTHING (0/0); frames disappear as a subchan HOLE (~1.7 %), and the skipped frame's state is ALWAYS the same: `recv=(max-1)/max, sof=1, contig=max-1, first_missing=max-1` -> **only the LAST chunk (the bottom slice's tail, ~489 B) is missing**, everything else (SoF + top slice + almost all of the bottom) is there and contiguous. Those near-complete frames never reach the flush (completion requires recv>=max) -> they were left abandoned until the subchan was reused (256 frames) -> **a frame_num hole** -> with `max_num_ref_frames=1` the decoder's reference goes stale -> drift/smearing across the WHOLE picture until the next IDR, **worse under heavy motion** (big frames = more chunks = more risk of losing the tail — which matches the user's experience exactly). Yet the stream we SUPPLY decodes CLEANLY offline (crisp F1 frames rendered): the loss is invisible to the counters (`perdus` only counts at flush, and an incomplete frame never flushes) — the "0 errors != clean picture" trap. **Offline A/B validation** (simulated tail truncation on `stream.h264`): emitting the TRUNCATED frame (top + nearly complete bottom, incomplete bottom NAL KEPT) = **MAE ~0, max ~5** (near-perfect against the reference, only the last MB rows concealed, self-correcting) vs DISCARDING it = a persistent corrupt block, **max ~200**. **Fix G43** in two parts: **(A) trigger** — when frame N's SoF arrives, flush frame N-1 if it is still incomplete (`SHADOW_FLUSH_PREV_INCOMPLETE=1`), in order, instead of waiting for the subchan to be reused; **(B) emission** — `vid_reasm_flush` detects tail loss (`tail_loss`: everything received contiguous from 0, little missing, NON-IDR) and emits the truncated concatenation WITHOUT backing up to the last complete NAL (so keeping the truncated bottom NAL, unlike F8) and WITHOUT discarding (bypassing the EMIT_TRUNCATED gate): `SHADOW_EMIT_TAIL_TRUNC=1`. Incomplete IDRs are still discarded (F11). Expected bonus: no more subchan holes -> no more spontaneous IDR-on-hole (idr_t falls back). Key tools: G42 (subchan HOLE counters + skipped-frame state), offline test `/tmp/trunc_test.py`. Refs: `ctrl_session.c::vid_reasm_flush` (G43 tail_loss block, ~l.788) + G28 activation (trigger, ~l.1769).


- **2026-08-22** — 🎯🎯🎯🎯 **G40: THE RESIDUAL SMEARING = TWO FRAMES MERGED INTO A SINGLE ACCESS UNIT (boundary by ts instead of by structure).** Root cause found at last, proven by the 65 faulty AUs captured by G39. **Decisive diagnosis**: the software test (`SHADOW_HWACCEL=0`) artefacts TOO -> NOT the decoder (NVDEC exonerated). The full `stream.h264` of the artefacted session (76 MB, what we SUPPLY) decodes with **0 errors offline**, but live (software AND NVDEC) it errored. **G39** (dumping the exact AU at the moment of a libavcodec error) reveals that the 65 faulty AUs ALL contain exactly `P(mb0)+P(mb1760)+P(mb0)+P(mb1760)` = **2 FRAMES in a single access unit**. The 2 frames are DISTINCT (different sizes, not duplicates). **Mechanism**: `emit_legacy` accumulates slices in `g_display_buf` and flushes to `on_video` on a **timestamp change** (`ts != g_display_ts`). That is correct for joining the TOP slice (first_mb=0) and BOTTOM slice (first_mb!=0) of the same frame (same ts). BUT when two consecutive frames carry the SAME ts (especially under **heavy motion** — the user confirms: few artefacts on a slow scene, many when things move/flash everywhere), their 4 slices accumulate -> `on_video` receives 2 frames -> `feed_annexb` (which flushes 1 AU per call) makes ONE access unit with 2 pictures -> libavcodec sees a second first_mb=0 slice in the same AU -> `decode_slice_header error` + `Frame num change` -> a corrupt frame that drifts. **Proof of the fix**: ffmpeg decodes the faulty AUs with 0 errors BECAUSE IT RE-SPLITS THEM by first_mb==0 (the H.264 spec's AU boundary) — exactly what the fix does. **Fix G40**: in `emit_legacy`, detect the access-unit boundary by STRUCTURE (a first_mb==0 slice starts a new frame) and not by ts -> flush `g_display_buf` as soon as a leading slice arrives while a frame is already in progress. A frame's top+bottom stay together; two frames never merge, even at an identical ts. `SHADOW_AU_BY_TS=1` restores the old behaviour (debug). Permanent diagnostics added: **G38** decode queue 16->64 (`SHADOW_DEC_QUEUE`, had already removed the drops but not the artefact), **G39** automatic capture of faulty AUs (`SHADOW_DUMP_ERR_AU`, default on, capped at 40) — the key tool that made the 2 frames visible. Refs: `ctrl_session.c::emit_legacy` G40 block (~l.1250), `h264_decoder.c::flush_access_unit` G39 (~l.585), `memory/project_G37_idr_spam_nvdec_phantom.md`.


- **2026-08-22** — **G38: the RESIDUAL smearing (rare, severe) = DECODE QUEUE OVERFLOW (GUI), not the network — queue 16→64.** New GUI capture (user, very noisy picture + DEVUI panel): `perdus=0/708163, aband=0, skip=0, ok 100 %` (NO network loss) and G18=0 (G37 holds) — BUT `NAL top=12289` vs `decoded=12062` = **227 assembled frames never decoded** = overflows of the decode thread's queue (`ctrl_session_glue.c`, cap 16). Every frame dropped here breaks a reference -> severe drift/smearing (the picture shown = P-frames stacked on a missing reference, motion vectors in the scenery), AND it triggered an IDR (G34) -> a large 720p IDR at 34 Mbps -> more decode/render contention (NVDEC shares the GPU with GL rendering) -> more overflows = a spiral (idr_t=255 over 260 s despite G37). Triggering content = an F1 background that is **dark + fast-moving** = big frames = a peak in decode cost -> the spike overflows a queue of 16 (~320 ms). **Fix G38**: queue raised to 64 (`SHADOW_DEC_QUEUE`, array 256) to absorb the spike; in steady state it drains (49>43.8) so the added latency is negligible outside a spike; drops become rare -> G34 no longer spirals. The `[G38] file decodage PLEINE` log traces any residual overflow (chronic deficit vs spike). If the drops PERSIST at cap=64 -> a chronic deficit (decoder too slow for the bitrate) -> lower `SHADOW_BITRATE_MBPS`/fps. Cause confirmed GUI-only: the headless build (no GL rendering) decodes at ~50/s with no drops. Refs: `ctrl_session_glue.c` G38 block (~l.40, ~l.520).


- **2026-08-22** — **RE of the OFFICIAL client's video reassembly (`udp_frame`/`udp_packet_receiver`, source path `sufp/udp_frame.cpp::insert`) — corroborates G37 + verdict (a) on the incomplete frame.** Disassembly + Ghidra of the official binary (source path leaked in a log: `.../sufp/udp_frame.cpp`, fn `insert`). **Verdict on the incomplete frame = (a): discard the WHOLE frame, never a partial prefix** — the delivery gate requires TOTAL completeness: `emit` @0xd93dd0 only serialises (`vtable+0x20` @0xd93c80) and only delivers (`vtable+0x80(-6, list1)` @0xd7f1e0) if the completeness predicate @0xd89f40 (`frame[+0x18]==0`, a remaining-chunk counter initialised to `max+1`) AND contiguity (`subchan==cursor+1`) are true; incomplete frames go to `list2` = the FREE list (never decoded). This confirms our choice (drop the whole frame) and the F2/offline lesson (a partial prefix = decoder concealment). **Lifecycle model (differs from ours)**: frames are held in a **reorder LIST** per subchan (find-or-create), **created on ANY index** (no "expect idx==0": a tail arriving before the SoF CREATES the frame with `max` taken from the first chunk seen), abandoned by a **reorder window** (subchan distance `this+0x8`) OR **300 ms expiry** (`GC` @0xd911d0, `0x11E1A2FF` ns). The tail (idx=max) is stored like any index (no special case); **a duplicate tail = a silent discard** @0xd8ce33 (altering neither the counter nor the stored copy). **IDR recovery = DOWNSTREAM, lazy, rate-limited (ONE IDR per loss event, NOT hammering per drop), NO NACK, NO FEC** in the reassembly layer (it only drops and emits a typed loss notification -1/-2/-3/-4). **This directly validates G37**: the official client does not spam IDRs; our spam came from G18 (phantom NVDEC errors). Remaining structural divergence (our `g_vid_reasm[subchan]` requires idx==0 to activate + clears on emit → orphans a tail reordered before the SoF): harmless at perdus=0 (an ordered wire, 0 jumps measured), but the robust model if loss/reordering appear is **find-or-create + reorder list + 300 ms hold** (already opt-in via `SHADOW_REORDER_BUFFER`, cf. G24/G35). Key addresses: OnDataReceived 0xd7f1e0, parse 0xd85fa0 (video 0xd865c0), process 0xd941f0 (create 0xd94518), insert 0xd8cd50, emit 0xd93dd0, commit 0xd93c80, GC 0xd911d0, completeness 0xd89f40, has-start 0x7df800. Frame fields: +0x08 subchan, +0x0a max, +0x18 remaining counter, +0x10 arrival_ts, +0x28..0x30 chunk vector, +0x40 frame_id.

AND YET `idr_t` climbs from 1 to 228 (**~1.2 IDR/s**). Every request comes from **G18** (`[G18] derive detectee (N erreurs bitstream)`, N cumulative 4→24→…→200+). Root cause: `g_bitstream_errors` (h264_decoder.c) is **CUMULATIVE, never reset**, incremented on any libavcodec log ≤AV_LOG_ERROR; and the **NVDEC (CUDA)** path logs `decode_slice_header error` (416×) + `Frame num change from X to Y` (416×) at ERROR level on a stream that is NEVERTHELESS VALID — **the SAME stream decoded in software (ffmpeg offline) = 0 errors**. G18 counted those phantom errors → an IDR every 1.2 s → a growing IFR counter (`69 50 00 02 [ctr++]`) = **exactly the autofocus G5 had removed** (the server does not deduplicate a growing counter → a coarse keyframe every time → a pulsing of quality/blockiness on motion). **SELF-SUSTAINING LOOP**: an IDR resets frame_num to 0 → "Frame num change" → a new error → a new IDR. **Fix G37**: `SHADOW_IDR_ON_BITSTREAM_ERR` default **0** (G18 no longer triggers an IDR; the counting stays for diagnostics). - **2026-08-22** — **G36: smooth emission (on completion, IN ORDER) + IDR on a sequence hole — removes both the freezes AND the drift.** G35's reorder buffer (in-order with head-of-line waiting) removed the drift but caused MICRO-FREEZES (head-of-line blocking) that were annoying in game. Decisive measurement: emission on completion is already IN ORDER (subchans emitted 0,1,2…50,52 — consecutive), so the reorder buffer was pointless. Its only defect: ~1 % of frames never complete (tail lost, never stored) and are skipped -> reference drift (smearing) that accumulates because the server sends few spontaneous IDRs (idr_t=2). **Fix G36**: detect the hole (emitted subchan != previous+1) and request an IDR -> the drift is bounded to ~one recovery without EVER freezing the picture. Defaults: SHADOW_REORDER_BUFFER=0 (smooth), SHADOW_IDR_ON_GAP=1. Measured: idr_t 2->19, a late frame (n=450) SHARP, aband=0, smooth. The remaining root cause = the ~1 % of tails never stored (the TAIL packet does not reach the slot store despite a correct wire and a consistent max field — still to be rooted out); in the meantime, G36 bounds the impact. Refs: `ctrl_session.c` G36 block (~l.1005).

Headless decoding keeps up (~50 fps, aband=0, 0 ffmpeg errors) but the GUI (NVDEC sharing the GPU with GL rendering) cannot sustain that rate -> the decode thread's queue (G25, cap 16) overflows -> frames DROPPED -> the decoder drifts from the encoder -> smearing that sets in after 5 s. The headless build does not reproduce it (no rendering). **G34**: on a decode-queue drop, request an IDR (`ctrl_session_request_idr`) to resynchronise (otherwise the drift never corrects itself). **G33**: a "Capture" menu in the DEVUI (Photo / Burst) -> `glReadPixels` of the video area actually displayed -> `/tmp/shadow-client/capture_*.ppm`, to be triggered exactly when the artefact is visible (finally making the GUI-side defect VISIBLE, invisible headless). - **2026-08-22** — **G28: clean PER-SUBCHAN reassembly (replaces the SoF/g_pending/pour_attente/G9c/G11 machinery), abandons 2 % -> 0 %.** Final diagnosis of the residual artefacts ("global on motion, taskbar intact") = reference propagation from frames WRONGLY DISCARDED. Decisive measurement: the wire NEVER delivers a frame missing only its tail (0 %), yet the client abandoned ~1-2 % of frames all with "the tail (idx=max) missing" (`[AB-SoF] recv=56/57`). Cause: the tail arrives after the next frame has begun (G9), and the g_pending/pour_attente stack MISROUTED it instead of letting it fall back into its frame's per-subchan buffer → a frame docked of its tail until the subchan was reused (256 frames) → abandonment → propagation. But the per-subchan buffers (`g_vid_reasm[subchan]`) natively resolve a late tail. So G28 replaces ALL that machinery with clean logic (the reorder path's) for both modes: SoF(idx==0)→a fresh buffer; non-SoF on an emitted/empty buffer→ignored (straggler/duplicate); a different max→ignored; otherwise→stored (slot dedup, reordering accepted). Emission: default = on completion (the tails arrive IN ORDER so it is de facto ordered, with no head-of-line blocking/freeze); reorder = ordered drain. Measured: 3 runs at aband=0, 0 ffmpeg errors, clean motion PNGs. With G26 (IDR on real loss) for the rare genuine losses. `SHADOW_LEGACY_REASM=1` restores the old machinery (debug). Refs: `ctrl_session.c` G28 block (~l.1569).

- **2026-08-22** — **G26: request an IDR on ANY real loss (tail included) + recovery ~2× faster.** Diagnosis of the residual artefact reported ("global when the scene moves, the static taskbar intact"): an offline PNG confirms **reference propagation** — a lost frame corrupts the MOVING areas of subsequent P-frames (static MBs copy a clean reference → taskbar intact) until the next key frame. But on the default path (reorder off), a lost frame decodes WITHOUT a bitstream error (valid but wrong references) → the G18 logic (IDR on error) did not fire, AND the G12 logic suppressed the IDR request when ONLY the tail was missing (`que_la_queue`) — that is, the most frequent loss. Result: no key frame requested → corruption until the next spontaneous keyframe (several seconds). Fix: `SHADOW_IDR_ON_LOSS=1` (default) now requests an IDR on any real loss (post-grace g_pending = confirmed), tail-only included; the event rate limit is brought down from ~1 s (20 ticks) to ~500 ms (10). Verified end to end: evt=1 on loss → the server answers (idr_t/idr_b 8-12 per run vs 1), stream with 0 ffmpeg errors, no loss spiral. `=3` restores the old suppression, `=0` disables it. The cause (~1.2 % loss) remains: the complementary lever is lowering the bitrate (adaptive G19 / SHADOW_BITRATE_MBPS). Refs: `ctrl_session.c` G26 block (~l.736 and ~l.3248).

- **2026-08-22** — **G25: dedicated decode thread (2nd RE difference from the official client), default ON.** `on_video` (native path) now ENQUEUES access units into a bounded ring (16, owned pointers, mutex+condvar) and a dedicated thread (`dec_thread_fn`) DEQUEUES and calls `h264_decoder_feed_annexb` + the G13/G18/stats post-processing (extracted into `glue_decode_frame`). So the receive thread never blocks on `avcodec_*` again → it drains the UDP socket in real time (headroom to raise the bitrate without decode-lag loss). Teardown: we `pthread_join` the thread BEFORE `h264_decoder_destroy` (a single producer for the decoder → no lock on the decoder, cf. BUG2). Queue full (decoder behind) → we drop the OLDEST (bounded latency). 100 ms abort granularity (`pthread_cond_timedwait`) = compatible with the Switch constraint (any long thread polls abort <=100 ms); pthread is already used on Switch (input_drain_thread). Measured: `fil de decodage demarre`, decoded ~24 fps, 0 ffmpeg errors, no crashes. **G24 reorder buffer: stays opt-in** — measured `SHADOW_REORDER_MS` 300 vs 1500 ms → aband almost unchanged (~1.2 %), so the losses are REAL (not recoverable jitter); at 1.2 % network loss, the buffer's head-of-line blocking (~300 ms/loss) costs smoothness (21 vs 24 fps) — the official client gets away with it because its loss is far lower. To be enabled on a clean link via `SHADOW_REORDER_BUFFER=1`. Refs: `ctrl_session_glue.c` (dec_thread_fn, glue_decode_frame, on_video).

- **2026-08-22** — **RE of the OFFICIAL client's video path (decoding + reassembly) + G24 reorder buffer (opt-in).** Two disassemblies of the binary `06-shadow-recon-linux/ShadowPCDisplay` (RTTI not stripped; file→VA delta = +0x400000). **Decoding (`18FFMPEGVideoDecoder`)**: the SAME libavcodec as us (LIBAVCODEC_61), `avcodec_find_decoder(AV_CODEC_ID_H264)`, `avcodec_open2(ctx,codec,NULL)` — NO flags (no LOW_DELAY), no concealment, no extradata (SPS/PPS in-band), ONE complete access unit per packet (like us), no AUD, PTS=NOPTS, a drain loop on `avcodec_receive_frame`, no corrupt-frame check at all. Config @0xbcb100, feed @0xbcc440. **Architectural difference #1: decoding on a DEDICATED THREAD** (producer @0xc514f0 notify_one → consumer @0xc515c8 cond_timedwait → `call *0x18`=decode), so the receive thread never blocks. **Reassembly (`13VideoUdpFrame`/`16SufpUdpIOChannel`, parse @0xd85fa0)**: confirms our model byte-exact — `max`=LAST index (guard `idx<=max` @0xd862aa), flag=`pkt[10]&1`, AEAD strip 28, concatenation by ascending idx, tail-duplicate dedup by slot @0xd8ce1a. **Architectural difference #2: a REORDER BUFFER** (`udp_packet_receiver`, a list sorted by subchan with u8 wraparound): holds incomplete frames for ~**300 ms** (deadline 0x11E1A2FF ns @0xd911d0), emits **in sequence order** ONLY complete frames, and on a real loss (still incomplete after 300 ms) drops + `AskForIdrFrame` @0xc28434 (NO NACK, NO FEC). Us, before: emission in COMPLETION order, abandoning a frame as soon as one chunk was ~66 ms late. **G24** implements the reorder buffer (`ctrl_session.c`: `vid_drain_ordered`, ordered emission by subchan, 300 ms hold, drop+IDR). **Default OFF (opt-in `SHADOW_REORDER_BUFFER=1`)**: our SYNCHRONOUS decoding on the receive thread creates processing bursts that expire the head wrongly (~1 %), each one = a ~300 ms freeze. Making it the default requires the dedicated decode thread first (#1). Refs: `ctrl_session_glue.c::on_video` (synchronous feed), `memory/project_video_artifacts_solved.md`.

- **2026-08-22** — **G23: the GUI's "anti-green" post-processing (F22) was producing the artefact SQUARES; default back to 0.** Lead found by comparing against the official client (which has NO artefacts at all). F22 (`on_frame`, GUI only) replaces every 16x16 macroblock that is "green" OR DARK (Y<12) with the remembered content of a previous frame — a papering-over of the old broken stream (green blocks). Since G4/G21/G22 the bitstream is correct: no more green, but DARK scenes (an animated night-race background) have legitimately dark MBs -> F22 repaints them with stale content -> 16x16 squares. **The ffmpeg/PNG validation of the H.264 stream could not see it**: F22 runs after decoding, in the GUI only (headless runs have no GL context -> `on_frame` does not run). Lesson: validate the DISPLAYED output too (post-`on_frame`), not just the bitstream. Fix: `SHADOW_ANTI_GREEN` default 0. Other obsolete post-decode hacks to watch (already off by default): SHADOW_CROP (N25 clamp 540), SHADOW_CROP_BOTTOM (F19), all papering-over from before G21. Refs: `ctrl_session_glue.c::on_frame` G23 block.

- **2026-08-22** — **G22: RE8 (parity_decrypt) was corrupting the picture, default back to 0.** Once G21 was in place, the animated background stayed NOISY across the whole surface **with no ffmpeg errors at all** (valid but wrong bytes) — the classic "0 errors != clean picture" trap. Cause: the RE8 path calls `shadow_cipher_decrypt_unsafe` (WITHOUT verifying the poly1305 tag) on continuation chunks, which are already CLEARTEXT H.264 NAL (V11). So the "decrypt" verifies nothing, always "succeeds", and stores a WRONG plaintext shortened by 28 B in place of the raw bytes -> corrupt content that decodes without errors but displays as noise + propagates across the GOP. Proof: a **VISUAL** A/B (PNG) on the animated F1 background — `SHADOW_PARITY_DECRYPT=0` = a sharp car (ORACLE/Mobil1/Pirelli text readable); =1 = mush. Offline reconstruction (everything RAW) = sharp. Fix: `SHADOW_PARITY_DECRYPT` default **0**. **Final combo G21+G22**: on a lossless wire, client == offline == perfect picture (0 errors). What remains = genuine UDP loss (0-0.7 %/run, aband/tronq) propagating until the next key frame = a separate piece of work (loss recovery, IDR/NACK). Methodological lesson: validate video changes **visually on motion** (PNG); the ffmpeg error count does not see valid-but-wrong bytes. Refs: `ctrl_session.c` G22 block (~l.1756).

- **2026-08-22** — **G21: G20 was a REGRESSION, `max` is the LAST INDEX (back to G4).** The "bottom slice" residue recorded earlier the same day is RESOLVED. Direct proof (raw wire dump `/tmp/wire.bin`): a frame with `max_declared=120` carries chunks idx **0..120** (121 chunks); idx 120 is the short tail (~489 B, flag10=0) and is sent **twice** (redundancy). G20 (max=count) rejected `idx==max` at the `chunk_idx >= max_chunks` guard and flushed at recv=max (chunks 0..max-1), **truncating every frame of its bottom slice's tail** -> CABAC errors at the bottom + GOP propagation. G20's "idx never reaches max" measurement was an ARTEFACT: the [H1.V7 dbg] log sits AFTER the rejection guard, so idx==max never appeared in it. Decisive A/B: G20 = 626 ffmpeg errors, G21 (max+1) = **0 errors**. An independent offline reconstruction (decrypt chunk data, raw concatenation of ALL received idx) = 0 errors, confirming the tail is genuine H.264 payload. Fix: `SHADOW_MAX_COUNT_PLUS1` default back to **1**. G21 stats: aband=5/750 frames, perdus=5/34328 chunks (0.015 % = occasional real UDP loss), orph=751 all duplicates (= the duplicated tails after flush). Refs: `ctrl_session.c` G21 block (~l.1255), `memory/project_image_bug_SOLVED_offbyone_G4.md`.

- **2026-08-22** — 🎯🎯 **G20: `max_chunks` IS A COUNT, NOT A LAST INDEX — G4 (+1) was the cause of the artefacts**. Direct wire measurement (`SHADOW_DUMP_RAW_FULL`, 79 frames decoded by hand): each **subchannel = one frame** (it increments over time), `chunk_idx` runs from 0 to (the `max` field - 1), and index `max` is **never** sent. Before G16 we emitted truncated (it got through); since G16 (which discards incomplete frames) we discarded them ALL -> massive corruption, worse on motion. **G4 disabled: aband=0, perdus=0, near-perfect picture** (measured: blockiness 1.2 vs 1.5-2.0, ffmpeg 0 errors offline after... see the residue). Default flipped to `SHADOW_MAX_COUNT_PLUS1=0`. **So the §3.16 entry (G4) is REFUTED for the current stream**: either the format changed since June, or June's +1 was compensating for another bug; August's measurement is unambiguous (idx never reaches max across 79 frames).
