- **2026-08-22** — ✅ **VIDEO ARTEFACTS: root cause found and treated**. **[SUPERSEDED on 2026-09-10 for COLOUR: the stream is BT.709 LIMITED range, and this fix's uniforms were never set — see COL1 at the top of §9.]** Proofs: (1) the assembled H.264 stream decodes CLEANLY offline (ffmpeg 0 errors); (2) LIVE decoding is BIT-EXACT with the offline one (2600/2600 identical luma hashes); (3) rendering is FAITHFUL (glReadPixels ~ the decoded frame). Two genuine client-side defects. **Colour** ("washed out"): a FULL-RANGE stream (luma 0-255, VUI absent) but a limited-range shader -> fixed (full range + BT.709, `SHADOW_COLOR_RANGE/MATRIX`), confirmed perfect. **"Squares"**: the same thread for reception AND synchronous decoding -> at high bitrate the socket buffer overflows during decoding -> slices lost -> stale macroblocks. Measured on constant content: 50 Mbps=1.30 % loss (blocky), 12 Mbps=0.14 % (clean), 0.03 %=excellent. Treated by **G19** adaptive bitrate (starting at 25 Mbps, -25 % if loss>0.8 %, +3 towards the ceiling if <0.15 %; the server honours a live kUpdateSession). + G18 (IDR on libavcodec errors). The deeper future fix = decoding off the receive thread (extend the async NVTEGRA worker to CUDA on Linux). Tools: `tools/decode_offline.c`, `tools/compare_hash.py`, `tools/find_divergence.sh`, `SHADOW_DUMP_RENDER`.

- **2026-08-22** — 🎯 **The assembled stream is CLEAN — the corruption is on the DECODE/DISPLAY side, and there is a colour bug**. **[SUPERSEDED on 2026-09-10 for COLOUR: the stream is BT.709 LIMITED range, and this fix's uniforms were never set — see COL1 at the top of §9.]** Capture of a session "riddled with artefacts" (`tools/capture_for_offline.sh`, 246 MB): decoded offline, ffmpeg **0 errors**, our decoder **0 errors**, blockiness 1.5-2.0 with no catastrophic drift (the 44 "frame num gap"s = our abandoned incomplete frames, absorbed without error). So the bytes we assemble are good. On Linux the NVDEC transfer is SYNCHRONOUS (the async worker only serves the NVTEGRA/Switch format), single-threaded, with no buffer race — and the offline path goes through the same code cleanly. **Colour bug found**: the stream is FULL RANGE (luma measured 0-255, VUI absent) but the GL shader applied a limited-range conversion (`1.164·(Y-16)`), which washes out/clips — a direct candidate for "washed-out colours". Fixed: full-range shader + BT.709 matrix by default, `SHADOW_COLOR_RANGE`/`SHADOW_COLOR_MATRIX` to compare against the official client. Tools: `tools/decode_offline.c` (our decoder outside a session), `tools/capture_compare.sh` (dump stream+frames to compare live vs offline on the same session). Still to confirm whether LIVE decoding adds artefacts beyond the colour (frame-by-frame comparison pending).

- **2026-08-22** — 🎯 **G18: the real cause of the artefacts = UNRECOVERED REFERENCE DRIFT**. Direct visual proof (PPMs at the decoder's output, before any display): on animated content, frame 60 perfect, frame 1000 torn, frame 2000 catastrophic — the degradation accumulates across the GOP then resets at the IDR. A lost slice breaks a reference frame, and every intermediate frame that leans on it inherits the error. **The same stream (`ab_reference.h264`) decoded by OUR offline decoder (CUDA hardware verified AND software) is perfectly clean, identical to ffmpeg** — so neither the decoder nor its settings are at fault, it is a session with more loss. G13 does not recover: it waits for 15 frames with NO OUTPUT, and during the drift the decoder does produce frames (wrong ones). The reliable signal is libavcodec, which logs "error while decoding MB" / "Frame num gap" at ERROR level (68 "Frame num gap"s counted in one dump). We count them (`bitstream_errors`) and request a targeted IDR, rate-limited to 1/1.2 s: precise (it only fires on real corruption), hence without G12's quality cost. Tools: `tools/decode_offline.c` (our decoder outside a session, per-frame measurement, hw and sw).

- **2026-08-22** — ✅ **Video: the DRIFT is eliminated (+23.6 % -> ~0 %)**. Measurements on constant content (an animated wallpaper, bench `tools/ab_video.sh`), hence comparable at last. Spatial blockiness climbed from 1.02 to 1.6 over a session — our reference frame drifted from the encoder's and the error accumulated until the next key frame; that is what the user described as "the picture fills with artefacts". It is now flat (-0.8 % and -0.2 % across two runs) and its absolute level falls to 0.94. The remaining TRANSIENT artefacts (1 to 2.4 %) are within run-to-run measurement noise: neither `SHADOW_EMIT_TRUNCATED`, nor `SHADOW_NACK`, nor the periodic IFR stands out from it. Cumulative fixes: G12 (no more key frames for a late tail), G13 (key frame on a real decoder stall), G14 (bitrate announced in bytes/s — received 8.9 -> 27-35 Mbit/s), G17 (retransmission untangled from the IFR).

- **2026-08-22** — 🎯 **G17: retransmission was subordinated to the key-frame request**. The `rG` drain was nested INSIDE the block that emits the IFR: we only asked for a targeted repair when we were already asking for a full refresh, i.e. when it is no longer any use. Made visible by the A/B: `nack=0` without the periodic IFR, `nack=27` with it, on the same content. **Note**: once untangled, disabling the `rG`s degrades nothing — the server does not appear to honour them. To be confirmed before investing further in that avenue.

- **2026-08-22** — G14 validated by direct measurement: **received bitrate 8.9 -> 27.3 Mbit/s** as soon as we announce the real bitrate. The server took us at our word and stayed five times below. But the artefact count is not down yet, and the remaining causal chain is identified: `perdus=45/132838` (0.034 %) -> 45 truncated frames -> 14 decoder stalls -> 14 key frames requested -> a drop in quality. A tiny loss breaks a frame, and the broken frame breaks the reference chain. G15: the `rG` retransmission went out 100 ms late when the format carries no frame id at all — the indices can only designate the frame CURRENTLY being sent by the server, so waiting five frames made the request moot. Sent immediately, capped at 20 per second.

- **2026-08-22** — 🎯 **G14: we were announcing a bitrate five times lower than what we actually take**. Field 1 of the `gE` is an estimate of the receive bitrate in BYTES PER SECOND (determined in steady state: 146,564 against a real 151,571 B/s, a 3 % difference). Our formula was `bytes_received × 4`, whereas over a 50 ms interval bytes per second is `× 20`. The server's encoder, slaved to that underestimate, stayed right at the floor — an over-compressed picture and blockiness on motion, while the link carries five times more. Exact correlation established beforehand (`tools/correlate_artifacts.py`, with a COMPUTED and not guessed offset): 99 % of artefacted frames are assembled normally on our side, which made it possible to definitively rule out reassembly and look at what we TELL the server.

- **2026-08-22** — 🎯 **G13: the freeze comes from the decoder having lost its reference**. Observation on the panel during a multi-second freeze: "decoded" falls to zero while "received" keeps going — that is, we are feeding libavcodec and it returns nothing. That is its normal behaviour when it has lost its reference: it SILENTLY abandons the frames that lean on it, with no error and no log, until the next key frame. So the freeze lasts until the server sends one spontaneously. G12 had removed the pointless key-frame requests and, in the same move, what was accidentally getting us out of the freeze. The right trigger is neither loss nor a late tail, but the decoder ceasing to produce while we feed it — fifteen frames supplied without a single output, i.e. a quarter of a second at 60 fps. Rare and justified, hence without G12's quality cost.

- **2026-08-22** — ✅ **G12 confirmed: artefacts 7.78 % -> 3.18 %, key frames 22 -> 1**. Objective measurement by `tools/analyze_artifacts.py` across two comparable dumps. The key frames we requested on every late tail really were the main cause: more than half the artefacts disappear by ceasing to ask for them. 3.18 % remain, to be looked for elsewhere.
- **2026-08-22** — A multi-second freeze reported, with the receive counters still advancing. **The freeze is NOT in the assembled stream**: the longest run of near-identical frames in the dump is 41 frames (0.7 s), at start-up. So it sits between decoding and the screen. The panel's four distinct rates (received / decoded / displayed / display loop, cf. §HUD) make it possible to settle this live: whichever falls to zero during the freeze names the faulty stage.

- **2026-08-22** — 🎯 **G12: the artefacts are ENCODED at the source, at our own request**. A decisive correlation between artefacted frames (offline geometric detection) and their assembly state: **95 % of artefacted frames are assembled NORMALLY** — complete, no chunk missing. The overlap with incomplete frames is at noise level (11 out of 60, with no preferred offset). So our reassembly is in the clear. On the other hand `idr_t=22` in 45 s, i.e. one key frame every 2 s: we are the ones asking for them. G6 requested a refresh as soon as a frame was incomplete, and our incomplete frames are ALL missing exactly one chunk — the last one, the one that arrives after the start of the next frame (G9), not a loss. Each forced key frame costs a frame several times heavier; at a constrained bitrate the encoder compensates by lowering quality during and just after, hence blocks that appear then clean up. We now only ask on a real loss (several chunks, or a chunk other than the last). Measurement tool: `tools/analyze_artifacts.py` (geometric score, median 0.0 against peaks of 27-43).

- **2026-08-22** — 🎯 **The artefacts are IN the assembled stream, and invisible to every validity check**. ffmpeg decodes our 85 MB without a single error, and yet 10 % of frames carry an incoherent block for a fraction of a second (visually confirmed on frames 447 and 885). These are VALID frames with WRONG content: no decoding counter can see them. Detected by a temporal score — a brief artefact is a block that differs strongly from both the previous frame AND the next one, whereas real motion resembles one of the two (median 0.9 against peaks of 107). Tool: `tools/analyze_artifacts.py`, which gives an objective before/after measurement. Cause identified (G11): a chunk with an index LOWER than the highest already received is the previous frame's tail; when two neighbouring frames announced the same chunk count, it was filed into the CURRENT frame in place of an expected chunk. Yet the indices always arrive in increasing order (raw sequence, and 109 frames out of 110 in the official capture).

- **2026-08-22** — 🎯 **HW2: we were decoding in VDPAU with a CUDA context**. The format callback's comment announced "CUDA, then VDPAU, then VAAPI" but tested all three under the SAME condition: the loop returned the first format libavcodec offered, not the first on our list. Log: "cuda hwdevice created" followed by "hwaccel vdpau selected". That mismatch between the device created and the format chosen produces frames libavcodec validates without error but that are visually degraded — squares on motion. Fixed with a loop per format, in order. By that stage reassembly had already been cleaned up (real loss 0.04 %, no duplicate frames, no decode errors), which is what made it possible to isolate the decoder as the only remaining suspect.

- **2026-08-22** — ✅ **G9c: reassembly cleaned up — real loss 0.02 %**. Measured after the fix: `perdus=25/118738`, `aband=25` across 2042 frames, **zero decode errors**, top and bottom slices balanced (2060/2035). Before: 39 % "loss". None of it was real. It came from PHANTOM frames opened by a chunk with a non-zero index — the straggler of a frame already emitted — which could never be completed and were counted as 99 % lost. We no longer open them (`orph=` counts those stragglers, ~0.9 per frame: they are surplus copies, the server duplicating as it does for audio). The day's two real fixes: G7 (stop discarding a frame because the next one has a different size) and G9 (hold the pending frame so its tail can rejoin it). Debug logs kept behind `SHADOW_DIAG_REASM=1`.

- **2026-08-22** — 🎯 **G9: a frame's TAIL arrives after the START of the next one**. Direct measurement (`[OPEN]`): 15 reassemblies out of 15 are opened by the previous frame's last chunk — `idx = max-1`, continuation flag, short length (53 to 1136 B instead of 1280). That straggler opened a phantom frame counted as 99 % lost — hence 39 % "loss" without a byte missing — while its real frame went to the decoder docked of its tail, that is, of the end of the BOTTOM slice. The same damage as G4's off-by-one, by another route. Fix: the incomplete frame goes on hold instead of being emitted immediately; the straggler rejoins it and it leaves complete. No latency added for frames that arrive whole. Explanations eliminated along the way: network reordering (sequence 0..38 perfectly ordered), kernel losses (`SO_RXQ_OVFL` = 0), link saturation (39 % "loss" identical at 2.8 and at 8.9 Mbit/s). Cf §3.16.

- **2026-08-21** — 🎯 **Video artefacts = loss BEFORE our socket, for want of congestion feedback**. Measurements: the kernel drops nothing (`SO_RXQ_OVFL` = 0) but we expect 76,679 chunks for 47,467 packets received — 39 % never reach us. Received bitrate 8.9 Mbit/s for 20 requested. The official client, for its part, receives 109 complete frames out of 110. Difference: its `gE` message carries a delay-variation measurement (field 2, signed values), whereas we sent **always zero** there — i.e. "all is well" whatever happens, which gives the server no reason to slow down. Implemented: since bytes 6-9 of the video header are the SEND timestamp in µs, their difference against our receive clock gives an offset whose VARIATION measures congestion. Cf §3.27.

- **2026-08-21** — ⚠️ **CORRECTION: bytes 6-9 of the video header are NOT a frame id**, contrary to what §3.15 and `cur_frame_id`'s comment said. Measured across 60 consecutive packets: 60 distinct values, increasing by 10 to 60 per packet — it is a **timestamp in microseconds**, tied to the send rate. Switching the frame boundary onto it (G8) produced 38,185 abandoned frames in 75 s for 224 decoded: reverted. The boundary remains the chunk with index 0, and `max` is constant within a frame (verified across 110 frames).

- **2026-08-21** — The wire is CLEAN on the official client's side: across 4000 video packets, **109 complete frames out of 110**, indices in order, a single out-of-order frame. So our 41 % of incomplete frames do not come from the network. An `SO_RXQ_OVFL` counter added to find out whether it is the kernel dropping for want of room — a local loss having been indistinguishable from a network loss until now.

- **2026-08-21** — 🎯 **G8: the frame boundary is the IDENTIFIER, not chunk 0**. The `cur_frame_id` field existed, with a comment explaining that it serves to detect a frame change "more robustly than `is_data && chunk_idx==0`" — but it was **never assigned**, only reset. The safeguard described did not exist. So the boundary was being guessed (chunk 0, then size in G7), which fails as soon as two frames follow closely: we emitted frames barely begun, hence 57 chunks expected for 31 received. We now accumulate as long as the identifier does not change, and a chunk with an older identifier is discarded as a straggler instead of cutting the current frame. This also fixes the retransmission requests, which were going out with `frame_id=0`. Cf §3.16, §3.27.

- **2026-08-21** — 🎯 **G7: every other frame was discarded because the NEXT one had a different size**. The flush trigger carried `r->max_chunks == max_chunks`: the frame in progress was only emitted if the next one announced the same chunk count; otherwise the `max_chunks != max_chunks` path ERASED the reassembly without emitting anything, even when complete. And the size varies with every frame (variable bitrate). Symptom: areas that do not refresh, stale blocks, compression artefacts — the decoder keeping its macroblocks. The measurement that put us on the trail: **1228 frames abandoned for only 780 missing chunks, and zero late chunks** — so most of the abandonments were not losses. The start of a frame is marked by the SoF, not by its size. Cf §3.16.

- **2026-08-21** — Video artefacts traced: **39 % of frames arrive incomplete** (`aband=9745` for `decoded=15538` over 385 s). A truncated frame leaves its stale macroblocks on screen — hence areas that do not refresh until a large movement forces new data. The `gE` feedback is decoded (bandwidth / jitter / timestamp, 20 Hz) but we send two of its fields as zero or wrong, and our `rG` retransmission cannot work as written. Cf §3.27.

- **2026-08-21** — 🔊 **AUDIO: the channel demands a ping**. The official client emits `0x70` (1 byte) every 7.50 s on `:base+30`; without it the server does not feed the channel, hence one session in two with `cursor=0` and an audio that seemed elusive. And the cursor/sound split is done on the cleartext's LENGTH (8 B = a position), not on the first byte: both are `0x12`. Cf §3.26.

- **2026-08-21** — 🔊 **AUDIO FOUND**: **[SUPERSEDED as regards type sorting: `:base+30` is AudioOut alone, with no cursor (§3.37, 2026-08-26); `0x12` there is audio, Opus or FLAC, and `0x02` the stream descriptor — 13 B on FLAC, 271 B on Opus — which is neither a cursor nor audio (§3.26, 2026-09-11). So "0x02/0x12 = cursor" is wrong twice over.]** it is multiplexed on the CURSOR channel `:base+30`, same SUFP header and same encryption. Guided silence/sound capture: 19.8 -> 170.3 packets/s as soon as sound plays, variable sizes 129-246 B against a fixed 47 B for the cursor. Our Opus frames had been arriving all along and were falling into the cursor parser's `unknown type` counter. Split on the cleartext's first byte (0x02/0x12 = cursor, otherwise Opus). Cf §3.26.
- **2026-08-21** — Audio: the captures show that **only three UDP channels receive** anything at all (video, cursor, DTLS `:base+12`) and that no capture contains sound — the remote desktop stayed silent throughout the reverse engineering. `:base+12` is downstream (533 received / 3 sent) and carries 104 B of cleartext, the size of the input echo. Hypothesis: audio multiplexed there. Decoder wired up and a size census added; a capture with sound will settle it. Cf §3.26.

- **2026-08-21** — Switch build trap: `wolfssl/options.h` does `#undef _POSIX_THREADS`, which empties half of `pthread.h` under newlib. Any `.c` including wolfSSL before `<pthread.h>` breaks — on the Switch build only, Linux not flinching. Cf §7.5.

- **2026-08-21** — 🏆 **GAMEPAD: native path found**. It goes through `:base+13` over UDP chacha20 (238/238 decrypted), 14 B payloads: type `00` = button (id + state), `01` = axis, `02` = d-pad, `05` = keepalive, and a `04 01 03 02` plug announcement at opening. No `shadowusb`, no extra channel — the Joy-Cons route is open again. Cf §3.25.

- **2026-08-21** — Menu settings decoded: **bitrate** = a live `kUpdateSession` f13 (`f1{f2=794816, f4=bps}`), **codec** = unregister + re-announce, **4:4:4 colour** = `kRestart` f5 (an empty message) and a full restart. Our f13 put the bitrate in the wrong field — fixed. Cf §3.24.

- **2026-08-21** — Resizing: Shadow does **not** renegotiate the resolution (no f14 and no f10 mid-session). The client scales locally and requests a refresh with a `0x64` byte on `:base+20` (92 during resizes, 0 outside them). Cf §3.21.

- **2026-08-21** — 🏆 **SCROLL WHEEL decoded**: a distinct message family `(106=0,140=0,150=240)`, delta as a **float32 LE at `@148` = ±120.0** per notch, a widened vtable (counter at `@100`). Correlation 125/126 against ground truth, sign correct 125/125. Cf §3.21.

- **2026-08-21** — 🏆 **LEFT CLICK RESOLVED**: its release is not a click message but a **144 B movement message with `@127=2`**. Without it the button latched, the VM stayed in a drag and its desktop froze. Cf §3.21.

- **2026-08-21** — 🏆 **INPUT WORKING**: movements, clicks, drags and keyboard applied inside the VM. Visual proofs: a drag-select along the exact diagonal, and F11 toggling Firefox to full screen (3 times). Complete chain: DTLS `:base+12` + Connect `@83=0x01` + `@122` + a per-device counter at `@96` + the left-button release. Cf §3.21.

- **2026-08-21** — Input: two conformance gaps fixed without unblocking injection — we were emitting `f13`/`f14` that the desktop never sends, and we never deleted our clients (2 phantom `active` ones per run). Cf §3.21.

- **2026-08-21** — Shadow's encryption is **asymmetric in keys**: the client->server direction uses the key the CLIENT sends in its `Encryption` request (which we were discarding), the server->client direction the one in the reply. Proven by decrypting the `:base+13` keepalive (constant payload `04 00 05 00 …`), with a positive control on video and cursor. Cf §3.23.

- **2026-08-21** — Input restated: the server TRACKS our cursor (an exact closed loop) but **injects nothing** into the VM — no movement, no click, no key. The wire is fully verified byte-exact for all three types. Fixes K3/K5 (`@122`, per-device counter at `@96`). Open lead: the 42 B encrypted packet on `:base+13`, whose key is not the control channel's. Cf §3.21.

- **2026-08-21** — Input channel ALIVE: DTLS transport on `:base+12` + `@83=0x01` in the Connect. **The mouse is applied** (the server echoes the position, `+7,+7,+7,-7,-7` = our exact deltas). The keyboard remains unapplied despite byte-exact frames (K3 fixes `@122`). Cf §3.21.

- **2026-08-21** — **RESOLVED**: the input channel is a **DTLS/UDP session on `:base+12`**, not TCP on `:base+14`. Proven by the chain `UDP_RECVMSG :14012 -> BIO_WRITE -> SSL_READ 104 B` on the SSL object that carries the 1833 movements and 19 clicks. So `:base+12` is not the audio — hence the audio channel being silent all along. Cf §3.21.

- **2026-08-21** — Input channel: port `:base+14` is occupied by the **ComChan** on the desktop side and accepts only ONE TLS session; the official input channel is elsewhere (port still unknown, hook extended to `writev`/`readv` to find it). The input wire is verified byte-exact across 4 sessions. Bootstrap made byte-exact (Authentication 245 B, kHid 99 B). Cf §3.21, §3.22.

- **2026-08-21 (⌨️ §3.20 KEYBOARD DECODED — `@142` scancode, `@110` press/release)** — Guided capture with known phrases typed: 51 reliable messages. **`@142` = evdev scancode** (51/52 exact), **`@110` = 6 press / 0 release**, size 144 B. **No modifier state**: SHIFT_L is a key like any other. The keyboard is symmetric (52/52 emit), unlike the mouse's left button. Four defects fixed: KBD_TEMPLATE diverged on **50 constant bytes** (inherited from the WebRTC era — replaced by a real desktop message, diff 0), `@110` never written, `@114/@116/@128` written with invented values when they are constants. Only `@142` was right. → §3.20

- **2026-08-21 (🖱️ §3.20 CLICKS DECODED — press/release + button index)** — Guided protocol with isolated transitions (196 s, 14 transitions, dt 0.001-0.011 s). **PRESS**: `@106=16 @136=8 @138=8 @144=8`, `@140`=0 left / 6 right-middle, **`@150` = button index (0=L 1=R 2=M)**. **RELEASE**: `@106=18 @136=0 @138=6 @144=6 @140=8 @150=0` — the button is NOT identified there. It is not a mask: a simultaneous LR state creates no new value, one message per transition. Anomaly: the LEFT release emits no 152 B message at all (3/3). The 6 offsets our code was not writing are exactly those. PERF: the hook wrote in _IONBF to a /tmp on an NVMe 85 % full — it was slowing the client being observed (dt 0.1-0.3 s before, 0.001-0.011 s after a 256 KB buffer + /dev/shm). → §3.20

- **2026-08-21 (🖱️ §3.20 CLICKS — the desktop does emit 152 B, and our template is byte-exact)** — CORRECTS the previous conclusion, drawn from a capture in which nobody was clicking. Dedicated capture with **54 timestamped human clicks** (reading `/dev/input/event*`): **75 messages of 152 B, 71 correlated with a click**. Diff: across the desktop's **134 constant bytes, ZERO differ** from our `CLICK_LEFT_TEMPLATE`. The defect is that we do NOT patch `@106 @136 @138 @140 @150` (the button's state fields) nor `@52` (the high half of the u64 ts, fixed by I10). **`@140` discriminates the button: left=0, right=6/8.** A corpus of 75 messages archived. Tooling created: uinput injection, window location in pointer space, reading real clicks. → §3.20

- **2026-08-21 (🖱️ §3.20 INPUT — byte-exact diff against 697 desktop messages)** — Input channel located in the June capture (`fd=316` → `:8014`, port_base 8000), 697 messages of 144 B of which 109/144 bytes constant. ⚠️ **Two claims in this entry have since been CORRECTED**: (1) `@52` is not a constant field but the high half of a u64 timestamp (fix I10, revert `SHADOW_INPUT_TS32=1` — the `SHADOW_INPUT_F52` toggle never existed); (2) "the desktop never emits 152 B" was false, drawn from a capture in which nobody was clicking: a dedicated capture gives 75 messages of 152 B correlated with clicks, and **our click template is byte-exact**. See the full table in §3.20. → §3.20

- **2026-08-21 (🔊 §3.19 AUDIO RESOLVED — wolfSSL without WOLFSSL_IPV6)** — The `:base+12` DTLS handshake failed in 100 % of sessions: our first `sendto` returned **-174 = NOT_COMPILED_IN**, the ClientHello never left. `EmbedSendTo` refuses an IPv6 peer when the library is compiled without `WOLFSSL_IPV6` — and the audio channel resolves to IPv6. Fix `wolfSSL_set_dtls_fd_connected()` (the socket is already connected → `connected=1` → send()/recv(), no more peer path): **complete handshake**, sizes identical to the desktop capture (765/386/25/14 B). A3: the fix revealed that the rx_thread never exited (wolfSSL retransmits internally on a socket timeout) → the process blocked for 151 s where 30 s were asked, a §7.3 violation; fixed with `wolfSSL_dtls_set_using_nonblock`. The 2026-05-23 RE ("code ready, just a timeout") was wrong: nobody had traced OUR wire. Remaining: 0 app-data records, expected on a silent VM, to be confirmed with sound. → §3.19

- **2026-08-21 (🔧 §3.18 SWITCH BUILD RESTORED)** — The console target had not compiled since `19abcd2` (2026-05-18); the `.nro` on disk dated from 14 May. 3 defects, all of them "a libnx symbol redeclared by hand instead of including the header": `psmInitialize` (`int` vs `Result` → ambiguating declaration), `appletHook`/`appletGetFocusState` (redeclared in C++ without `extern "C"` → mangled → undefined reference, + a local typedef shadowing libnx's, + `<switch.h>` missing from the file), and `sctp_send_msg_redundant` referenced while `sctp.c` is excluded from the Switch build (stub added, signature verbatim from `sctp.h`). 13.5 MB NRO rebuilt, Linux unchanged. → §3.18

- **2026-08-21 (🏆 §3.17 FREEZE t≈118s RESOLVED — `/status` is a POLLING endpoint)** — ROOT CAUSE: the 2nd SSE targets `/N/status` (matching the desktop), which **answers 200 and terminates immediately** — it is not a persistent stream. But `sse_keepalive_thread` did only **ONE `curl_easy_perform`**: at the first return the thread exited, nobody re-polled, and the server closed the session ~118 s later (the binding rests on the DUAL SSE, §3.5). Fix D9 = a reconnection loop → **t=225 s with no closure** against 4 deaths out of 4 before; 306 polls all at `rc=0 http=200`. The headless build saw nothing because it opens `/stream` for both JWTs (a divergence from the official client) — hence the bug's invisibility. Four hypotheses refuted along the way: a RegisterSession lease, malformed input (move AND click), heartbeat starvation (4.10 Hz delivered), the render path (`SHADOW_GUI_NOVIDEO`). Still open and distinct: UDP loss ×3.3 in the GUI. → §3.17

> For historical traceability. The detailed entries live in the topic sections above. Here, just the pointer + a one-line summary.

- **2026-06-02 (🏆 §3.16 IMAGE BUG RESOLVED — off-by-one chunk count G4)** — ROOT CAUSE: `max_chunks` = the index of the last chunk (0-based), not a count. The server sends idx 0..max (max+1 chunks); the guard `chunk_idx >= max_chunks` dropped the last chunk (idx==max, flag10=0 = the bottom slice's tail) on every frame → truncated bottom → GOP propagation → illegible on motion. Fix `max_chunks+=1`. **Offline ffmpeg 761 errors → 0**, near-perfect picture confirmed by the user. + G3 bitrate 1→15 Mbps. Decisive diagnostic: `[DIAG] REJECT idx==max` on every frame. NOTE: the previous version "bug = the hardware path" was FALSE (the workflow had analysed static content). Remaining: occasional freezes = occasional UDP loss (future loss recovery). → §3.16, `memory/project_image_bug_SOLVED_offbyone_G4.md`
- **2026-06-02 (§3.16 v1 — false lead "hardware path", SUPERSEDED by G4 above)** — An 11-agent RE workflow: had concluded the bytestream was fine + an NVDEC bug. Invalidated: the dump analysed was static; the real bug (off-by-one) shows up on motion. Useful takeaways: FEC is dead (F31), trim = a regression, the software path = a good model.
- **2026-05-23 (🔓 §3.8 CHANNEL ANNOUNCEMENTS DECODED C95)** — The 8 channel-announcement control messages after RegisterSession decoded byte-exact. Mapping of outer fN → channel: **f1=Video, f2=Audio, f3=Input, f4=Cursor, f5=Mic, f6=Controller, f7=Clipboard, f8=FileTransfer**. CI_BODY_5..12 in `ctrl_msgs.c` confirmed byte-exact against the desktop (8/8 ✓ via a MASTER capture diff). **The multi-NAL trigger is NOT in this layer**; the remaining holy-grail candidate = `RegisterSession_StreamingProtocol.protoType=SSUFP`. → `memory/project_channel_announcements_8msgs_RE.md`, `tools/ida/out/CHANNEL_ANNOUNCEMENTS_RE.md`, KB §3.8
- **2026-05-18 23:00 (📺 FPS1 + I3 + I1p3 + Server modes)** — Reply M13 decoded: **20 server-supported modes** logged at runtime (= 1920×1080 @ 120, 60, 143.85 etc.), cf §3.8b. Audio I3 (Opus+ALSA) + Input I1p3 (mouse/keyboard via the `native_input` bridge) wired up on the native path. The "low" ~32 fps at idle = the encoder's VBR frame-skip, **not a client cap** — like the bitrate, it is a MAX. → `memory/project_FPS1_server_modes_VBR.md`, `memory/project_I3_audio_native_DONE.md`
- **2026-05-18 22:30 (⚡ PERF1 TCP_NODELAY + audio rcvbuf)** — `setsockopt(TCP_NODELAY)` added on the ctrl/input/video TCP channels (-40 ms per send = critical for input in games) + SO_RCVBUF=512KB on the audio DTLS (symmetry with N56). `<netinet/tcp.h>` added to `sockets_compat.h`. Test runs: 28.8 → 31.9 fps cumulative (+10 % vs baseline). → `memory/project_PERF1_latency_wins.md`
- **2026-05-18 22:00 (🎯 N56 BLURRY TASKBAR ROOT CAUSE FIX)** — The UDP pipeline's `SO_RCVBUF` was never set → the Linux kernel default of 208 KB. At 1080p and 1.2 MB/s → the buffer fills in 170 ms → the burst's first chunks dropped FIFO → chunk 0 (= the slice_header) systematically lost = `first_hole_idx=0` 76 % of the time → libavcodec `bytestream -5..-18` errors on MB row 60+ = a **blurry taskbar**. Fix `setsockopt(SO_RCVBUF, 4MB)` in `udp_register()`. Automated 3×30 s test: **0 HOLES** (against 12 baseline) + fps 30.2 vs 28.8 + abandoned ÷9. Tooling: `tools/test-N56-holes.sh`. → `memory/project_N56_udp_rcvbuf_FIX.md`
- **2026-05-18 20:30 (🐛 BUG2 decoder race cleanup)** — SEGV in `av_hwframe_transfer_data` on window close. A T43 libdatachannel worker racing T1's `finalize()` freeing the decoder. Fix: `std::mutex decoder_mtx` + a double-checked lock in the video/audio onMessage lambdas + a lock in finalize before destroying (`dc/dc_session.cpp:56-73 / 326-339 / 557-577`). → `memory/project_BUG2_decoder_race_fix.md`
- **2026-05-18 20:00 (🔧 HW1 hwaccel rc=-5 ROOT CAUSE FIX)** — `OUTPUT_CORRUPT`+`SHOW_ALL`+`error_concealment=259` are software-only in libavcodec (h264dec.c). In hwaccel mode (CUDA/VDPAU/VAAPI/NVTEGRA), forcing partially decoded frames out → `hwframe_transfer FAIL rc=-5 (Input/output error)` + `Failed to sync surface 0xNN`. Fix: flags conditional on `hwaccel ON/OFF` + 1 transient retry + a sticky `/tmp/shadow-client/hwaccel.disabled` marker after 5 failures. No more need for a manual `SHADOW_HWACCEL=0` after a crash. → `memory/project_HW1_hwaccel_rc5_fix.md`
- **2026-05-14 16:00 (🎯 MASTER capture after the hook fix)** — Hook fixed (= dump_n=g_max_dump), chacha20 KEY extracted, 1156/6125 packets decrypted OK. **698 top NAL + 84 bottom NAL = 10.7 % bottom CONFIRMED** on the desktop side. 132 multi-NAL packets. Our Switch = 0 % bottom. Control diffs: State 2 Hz on the desktop vs 1 Hz for us, **RegisterSession 8× desktop vs 1× us**, UnregSession 8× desktop vs 0× us. Build N48+N49 (500 ms heartbeat + periodic RegisterSession every 30 s) ready to test. → `memory/project_image_100pct_PROOF.md`, §3
- **2026-05-09 00:30 (🏆 H.264 ffprobe-VALIDATED)** — NAL bytestream dumped after stripping the Shadow VideoFrame header (6 B normal / 19 B keyframe). ffprobe: codec=h264 profile=High 1920x1080 yuv420p. The Shadow stream is fully reproducible. → §3.10
- **2026-05-09 00:10 (Cursor :13030 + libavcodec test)** — `:13030` also decrypts with the SAME chacha20 key (194/197 = 98 % MAC OK). An application-level VideoFrame layer identified on top of chacha20 (libavcodec type=0 = not standard RFC 6184). Still to RE the Shadow VideoFrame format for H.264 decoding. → §3.9
- **2026-05-08 23:55 (🏆 FULL NATIVE PIVOT)** — `shadow-test-cli` receives 1041 video frames over UDP :13010 + 126 chacha20 decrypts with a valid MAC and 0 failures. ### 3.8b M13 reply — the server **lists 20 supported modes** (343 B) — `[C100]` ⭐ DECODED
- **Source**: `webrtc.log` live runtime 2026-05-18, hex dump `[H1.V8]` + the inline `[FPS1]` parser in `core/protocol/ctrl_session.c:1131-1170` (production code)
- **⚠️ CORRECTED 2026-08-29 (L18)** — the claim "the 1st match is current_mode (= what the server accepted from our request)" is **FALSE**. Refuted by a complete session: requested `1280x720 @ 120`, decoded `1280x720`, and mode #1 = `2560x1440 @ 59.94`. The list moreover carries four `1280x720` entries (ranks 2, 6, 27, 67). It is a list of **supported** modes, with no established order, and **nothing in it designates the current mode**. That unverified annotation led to the wrong conclusion that the VM was rendering at 1440p and that the frame rate was capped there at 60 fps — see §9 (L15). **Where to read the mode actually granted remains an open question.**
- **Full wire structure** (343 B total):
  ```
  outer:
    f1 varint = 4                          (= an enum constant)
    f3 sub len ~280B = StreamingProfiles {
      f10 sub len ~280B = ProfileList {
        f2 sub len 11 = CurrentMode {      (= what the server accepted from our request)
          f1 varint = width
          f2 varint = height
          f3 fixed32 = fps
        }
        f3 sub len 5 = { f3 fixed32 = 1.0 } (= probably a scale factor)
        f4 sub len 11 x N = SupportedMode  (= the list of possible modes, see below)
        f4 sub len 25 = ServerVersion {
          f1 varint = 1
          f2 string = "6.2.0"
          f3 string = "ShadowStreamer"
        }
      }
    }
  ```
- **20 modes observed on a "Boost"-grade user plan, 2026-05-18 22:00**:
  ```
  #1   1920x1080 @ 143.85 fps  <- current (= matches our default request)
  #2   1920x1080 @  59.94 fps
  #3   2214x1080 @ 119.88 fps
  #4   1280x720  @  59.93 fps
  #5   1920x1200 @ 120.00 fps
  #6   1920x1080 @ 120.00 fps  <- the plan supports 1080p120
  #7   1680x1050 @ 120.00 fps
  #8   1600x1200 @ 120.00 fps
  #9   1600x1024 @ 120.00 fps
  #10  1600x900  @ 120.00 fps
  #11  1366x768  @ 120.00 fps
  #12  1360x768  @ 120.00 fps
  #13  1280x1024 @ 120.00 fps
  #14  1280x960  @ 120.00 fps
  #15  1280x800  @ 120.00 fps
  #16  1280x768  @ 120.00 fps
  #17  1280x720  @ 120.00 fps
  #18  1152x864  @ 120.00 fps
  #19  1024x768  @ 120.00 fps
  #20  800x600   @ 120.00 fps
  ```
- **Observed behaviour**: the server **accepts** what we advertise (= 143.85 fps, a custom value inserted as current_mode #1). Entries #2-#20 are the "preset" modes the plan supports.
- **EFFECTIVE fps != announced fps**: a runtime test with SHADOW_FPS=120 + SHADOW_BITRATE_MBPS=80 measures **32 fps + 17 Mbps**. Cause = **VBR encoder frame-skip** on an idle picture (= a near-static desktop image → the encoder only generates ~30 distinct frames/s). The announced fps is a **max**, not a target, **just like the bitrate**.
- **fps under activity** (= gaming/video) is expected to climb to 60-120. Still to validate live with `tools/test-fps-activity.sh`.
- **Cross-ref**: §3.8 (channel announcement builders), §3.7 (RegisterSession), `memory/project_FPS1_server_modes_VBR.md` (full analysis)
- **Confidence**: C100 on the wire decoding + C100 on "server-side fps = a VBR max", C70 on the behaviour under activity (to be validated)
- **History**: an older version of this section wrongly concluded that "fps=144 mismatch → a server-side cap". Falsified by RE1 (= the same bottom NAL at fps=60 or 144), then confirmed on 2026-05-18 that **fps is just a max, not a firm request** — the encoder sends what it decides based on the content.
